# This is NOT the Next.js you know

This version has breaking changes — APIs, conventions, and file structure may all differ from your training data. Read the relevant guide in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` (resolved from this file's directory; in monorepos the `next` package may not be visible from the repo root) before writing any code. Heed deprecation notices.

This block is written and re-added by `next dev` — verify at `node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/generate-agent-files.js`. Removing it from a diff only re-creates the uncommitted change; committing it with your work keeps the tree clean.

# Project: Admin Dashboard

> **Canonical project doc.** This file is the source of truth for the
> project's stack, structure, conventions, and architecture decisions.
> The runtime auto-loads it on every agent turn; agents MUST re-read
> the relevant section before any code change and MUST update this
> file after every non-trivial change (see [Mandatory Rules](#mandatory-rules)).

## Quick Facts

| | |
|---|---|
| **Name** | `admin-dashboard` |
| **Type** | Next.js 16 admin dashboard (App Router, Turbopack, RSC) |
| **Package manager** | `pnpm` (only — `package-lock.json` was removed 2026-09-01; don't run `npm install`, it regenerates it) |
| **Dev** | `pnpm dev` → http://localhost:3000 |
| **Build** | `pnpm build` |
| **Lint** | `pnpm lint` (ESLint flat config) |
| **Test** | `pnpm test` (Node test runner through `tsx`) |
| **Typecheck** | `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` |

## Stack

| Layer | Tech |
|---|---|
| Framework | Next.js `16.3.4` (App Router, Turbopack) |
| UI | React `19.2`, React DOM `19.2` |
| Language | TypeScript `5` (strict) |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS `4` (no `tailwind.config.*` — tokens in `globals.css` via `@theme inline`), `tw-animate-css` |
| Font | IBM Plex Sans Thai (`next/font/google`, Thai+Latin, see Conventions § Theme) for all UI text; Geist Mono (`geist` package) for genuinely monospace content only |
| UI primitives | Radix UI (avatar, checkbox, dialog, dropdown-menu, label, popover, select, separator, slot, switch, tabs, tooltip) |
| Icons | `lucide-react@^1.39` |
| Tables | `@tanstack/react-table` |
| Charts | `recharts` |
| Forms | `react-hook-form` + `@hookform/resolvers` + `zod` |
| Toast | `sonner` |
| Utility | `clsx`, `tailwind-merge`, `cmdk` |
| Drag-and-drop | `@dnd-kit/core`, `@dnd-kit/sortable`, `@dnd-kit/utilities` (added for the menu-management tree editor — see Conventions § Menu management) |
| Tests | Node test runner + `tsx` |

## Project Structure

```
src/
├─ app/
│  ├─ layout.tsx                 # root layout (font, toaster) — no ThemeProvider; single fixed Navy Enterprise theme, see Conventions § Theme
│  ├─ page.tsx                   # redirects → /dashboard
│  ├─ globals.css                # design tokens + @theme
│  ├─ not-found.tsx
│  ├─ login/                     # top-level split-screen login (no parent layout)
│  │  ├─ page.tsx                # "use client"; credentials step + department-selection step
│  │  └─ actions.ts              # "use server"; loginAction/selectDepartmentAction, sets httpOnly cookies
│  └─ (auth)/                    # route group — shared testimonial layout
│     ├─ layout.tsx              # 50/50: dark brand panel | form area
│     ├─ register/page.tsx
│     └─ forgot-password/page.tsx
└─ (dashboard)/                  # route group — authed area; every page here is a real top-level route (see ADR-005)
   ├─ layout.tsx                 # server; getCurrentSession() + redirect("/login") guard, feeds DashboardShell
   ├─ actions.ts                 # "use server"; logoutAction — clears cookies, best-effort POST /auth/logout
   ├─ [...rest]/page.tsx         # catch-all placeholder for real-permission menu items with no page yet
   ├─ dashboard/
   │  ├─ page.tsx                # dashboard home (URL /dashboard — the one route that keeps this segment)
   │  └─ loading.tsx             # route-level Suspense fallback for /dashboard
   ├─ analytics/page.tsx         # URL /analytics
   ├─ orders/page.tsx            # URL /orders
   ├─ products/                  # URL /products and /products/list — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Products
   │  ├─ page.tsx                 # URL /products — server; PRODUCTS_VIEW gate + list/lookups fetch via products-page-content
   │  ├─ list/page.tsx            # URL /products/list — same products-page-content, separate entry point
   │  ├─ products-page-content.tsx # shared async Server Component body used by both page.tsx above
   │  └─ actions.ts               # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath on both /products and /products/list
   ├─ settings/page.tsx          # URL /settings
   ├─ users/page.tsx             # URL /users
   ├─ menus/                     # URL /menus — SUPER_ADMIN-only drag-and-drop menu tree editor, see Conventions § Menu management
   │  ├─ page.tsx                 # server; SUPER_ADMIN gate + initial GET /menus/management-tree fetch
   │  └─ actions.ts               # "use server"; saveMenuOrderAction/refreshMenuTreeAction (PATCH /menus/reorder)
   ├─ materials/                  # URL /materials — real permission-aware materials control-tower dashboard
   │  ├─ page.tsx                 # server; active-stock health, critical queue, latest receivings, scoped by MATERIAL_VIEW / MATERIALS_RECEIVING_VIEW
   │  ├─ loading.tsx              # route-level skeleton matching the dashboard layout
   │  ├─ pc/                      # URL /materials/pc — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Materials PC
   │  │  ├─ page.tsx              # server; MATERIAL_VIEW gate + list/lookups fetch, reads page/search/status from searchParams
   │  │  └─ actions.ts            # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath("/materials/pc")
   │  └─ materials-receiving/     # URL /materials/materials-receiving — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Material Receiving
   │     ├─ page.tsx              # server; MATERIALS_RECEIVING_VIEW gate + list/lookups fetch, reads page/search/status
   │     └─ actions.ts            # "use server"; create/confirm/cancel/delete + supplier lookup, revalidatePath("/materials/materials-receiving")
   ├─ master-data/
   │     ├─ page.tsx             # URL /master-data — hub/landing dashboard, see Conventions § Master data dashboard hub
   │     ├─ loading.tsx          # route-level skeleton for the hub
   │     ├─ categories/         # URL /master-data/categories — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Categories master data
   │     │  ├─ page.tsx                 # server; CATEGORY_VIEW gate + list fetch, reads page/search/status from searchParams, defaults to sortBy=sortOrder
   │     │  ├─ actions.ts               # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath("/master-data/categories")
   │     │  └─ categories-actions.test.ts # perform* helper coverage (CRUD + 409/empty/network + public-wrapper guard)
   │     ├─ loading-points/      # URL /master-data/loading-points — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Loading Points master data
   │     │  ├─ page.tsx                 # server; LOADING_POINT_VIEW gate + list fetch, reads page/search/status from searchParams, defaults to sortBy=code
   │     │  ├─ actions.ts               # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath("/master-data/loading-points")
   │     │  └─ loading-points-actions.test.ts # perform* helper coverage (CRUD + 409/empty/network + public-wrapper guard)
   │     ├─ delivery-types/      # URL /master-data/delivery-types — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Delivery Types master data
   │     │  ├─ page.tsx                 # server; DELIVERY_TYPE_VIEW gate + list fetch, reads page/search/status from searchParams, defaults to sortBy=code
   │     │  ├─ actions.ts               # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath("/master-data/delivery-types")
   │     │  └─ delivery-types-actions.test.ts # perform* helper coverage (CRUD + 409/empty/network + public-wrapper guard)
   │     ├─ reject-reasons/     # URL /master-data/reject-reasons — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Reject Reasons master data
   │     │  ├─ page.tsx                 # server; REJECT_REASON_VIEW gate + list fetch, reads page/search/status from searchParams, defaults to sortBy=code
   │     │  ├─ actions.ts               # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath("/master-data/reject-reasons")
   │     │  └─ reject-reasons-actions.test.ts # perform* helper coverage (CRUD + 409/empty/network + public-wrapper guard)
   │     ├─ material-models/     # URL /master-data/material-models — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Material Models master data
   │     │  ├─ page.tsx                 # server; MATERIAL_MODEL_VIEW gate + list fetch, reads page/search/status from searchParams, defaults to sortBy=code
   │     │  ├─ actions.ts               # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath("/master-data/material-models")
   │     │  └─ material-models-actions.test.ts # perform* helper coverage (CRUD + 409/empty/network + public-wrapper guard)
   │     ├─ suppliers/          # URL /master-data/suppliers — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Suppliers master data
   │     │  ├─ page.tsx                # server; SUPPLIER_VIEW gate + list fetch, reads page/search/status from searchParams, defaults to sortBy=code
   │     │  ├─ actions.ts              # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath("/master-data/suppliers")
   │     │  └─ suppliers-actions.test.ts  # perform* helper coverage (CRUD + 409/empty/network + public-wrapper guard)
   │     └─ units/              # URL /master-data/units — real CRUD against cps-api, see Conventions § Units master data
   │        ├─ page.tsx                # server; UNIT_VIEW gate + list fetch, reads page/search/status from searchParams, defaults to sortBy=code
   │        ├─ actions.ts              # "use server"; create/update/deactivate/restore, each revalidatePath("/master-data/units")
   │        └─ units-actions.test.ts   # perform* helper coverage (CRUD + 409/empty/network + public-wrapper guard)
```

```
src/
├─ components/
│  ├─ ui/                        # shadcn-style primitives (Button, Input, Label, Card, Table, Sheet, Dialog, Tabs, ViewToggle, RowActionsMenu, ...)
│  ├─ layout/                    # Logo, command palette, app shell
│  ├─ dashboard/                 # /dashboard home — BI-style redesign, see Conventions § Dashboard home
│  │  ├─ dashboard-view.tsx       # "use client" orchestrator — owns range/filters/drawer state, derives every filtered slice, renders the whole page body
│  │  ├─ dashboard-header.tsx     # title/greeting + status pill + date-range Select + filter-drawer trigger (mobile) + refresh + CSV export
│  │  ├─ dashboard-filters.tsx    # DashboardFilters (search/docType/status/category) — real client-side filter state, shared by the sidebar rail and the mobile Sheet
│  │  ├─ kpi-card.tsx             # KpiCard + Sparkline — the 4 top-tier summary cards, NOT the same component as stat-card.tsx (see below)
│  │  ├─ chart-card.tsx           # ChartCard/ChartLegendItem/DashboardEmptyState — the one wrapper every chart/table panel on this page uses
│  │  ├─ stat-card.tsx            # StatCard — pre-existing, still used by /analytics; NOT touched by the dashboard redesign, don't rename/merge with KpiCard
│  │  ├─ stock-movement-chart.tsx, category-breakdown-chart.tsx, weekly-throughput-chart.tsx, recent-documents.tsx, recent-activity.tsx, low-stock-watchlist.tsx
│  │  │  # all gained an optional data/items/documents prop (defaults to the full mock array, so a bare call site is unchanged) + an empty state — see Conventions § Dashboard home
│  ├─ materials-dashboard/        # /materials control-tower dashboard
│  │  └─ materials-dashboard-view.tsx # server-rendered stock-health rail, KPI cards, critical queue, receiving activity, permission-aware drill-downs
│  ├─ master-data/                # /master-data hub dashboard — see Conventions § Master data dashboard hub
│  │  └─ master-data-dashboard-view.tsx # server-rendered KPI row + per-resource card grid, permission-aware (locked card when a resource's own VIEW is missing)
│  ├─ menus/                     # menu-management drag-and-drop tree editor (dnd-kit) — see Conventions § Menu management
│  │  ├─ menu-tree-editor.tsx     # "use client"; DndContext + CRUD dialog orchestration, save/reset, staged-changes state
│  │  ├─ menu-tree-row.tsx        # sortable row + DragOverlay preview + edit/delete actions
│  │  ├─ menu-form-dialog.tsx     # responsive create/edit form; RHF + zod, parent selection on create, visibility/activity switches on edit
│  │  └─ menu-delete-dialog.tsx   # hard-delete confirmation with backend child/permission guard feedback
│  ├─ materials-pc/              # /materials/pc CRUD — see Conventions § Materials PC
│  │  ├─ material-pc-client.tsx   # orchestrates filters/table/dialogs, calls Server Actions, router.refresh()
│  │  ├─ material-pc-filters.tsx  # search (debounced) + active/inactive/all pills, both drive URL searchParams
│  │  ├─ material-pc-table.tsx    # table/Editorial-card/Compact-Row views (view prop) + shared pagination/actions/label helpers
│  │  ├─ material-pc-form-dialog.tsx    # create/edit — responsive Dialog (full-screen on mobile, centered on desktop)
│  │  ├─ material-pc-status-dialog.tsx  # disable/enable confirmation, wraps ui/confirm-dialog
│  │  ├─ material-pc-details-dialog.tsx # read-only "view full record" dialog (MaterialPcDetailsDialog/MaterialPcDetailsView) — every field, opened from every view via onViewDetails
│  │  └─ material-pc-image-preview.tsx  # full-image lightbox (object-contain) + Download button, shared by table thumbnail + card image
│  ├─ materials-receiving/       # /materials/materials-receiving CRUD — see Conventions § Material Receiving
│  │  ├─ materials-receiving-client.tsx   # orchestrates filters/table/dialogs, calls Server Actions, router.refresh(); owns key-based remount ids for the form/cancel dialogs; also owns useViewMode("materials-receiving")
│  │  ├─ materials-receiving-filters.tsx  # search (debounced) + all/draft/confirmed/cancelled pills, drive URL searchParams
│  │  ├─ materials-receiving-table.tsx    # MaterialsReceivingCollection (table/card/list, mirrors MaterialPcCollection) + RowActionsMenu (view/confirm/cancel/delete, permission- and status-gated)
│  │  ├─ materials-receiving-form-dialog.tsx    # the only create UI — material select, qty, supplier production date, live box/QR-format preview; "ยืนยันการรับเข้า" calls create() then confirm() as one user action
│  │  ├─ materials-receiving-cancel-dialog.tsx  # cancel with a required reason (cps-api requires cancelReason) — not a ConfirmDialog since that primitive has no text-field slot
│  │  └─ materials-receiving-details-dialog.tsx # read-only view of a receiving's boxes — QR image, lotDetailNo, qty/remaining-qty per box
│  ├─ products/                  # /products + /products/list CRUD — see Conventions § Products
│  │  ├─ products-client.tsx      # orchestrates filters/table/dialogs, calls Server Actions, router.refresh()
│  │  ├─ products-catalog-summary.tsx # 4-card catalog summary strip, visually mirrors Materials PC's inventory summary
│  │  ├─ products-filters.tsx     # search + status pills + advanced filter Sheet (type/customer/model/location/line), all drive URL searchParams
│  │  ├─ products-table.tsx       # bounded sticky table / compact Editorial card / Compact Row — mirrors material-pc-table.tsx
│  │  ├─ products-form-dialog.tsx # NOT rendered anywhere anymore (see Conventions § Products) — kept only as the module exporting the shared zod schema/defaults/ProductImagePicker the wizard imports
│  │  ├─ products-wizard-dialog.tsx  # the sole add/edit UI (ProductsWizardDialog/ProductsWizardView) — 5-step form + a post-save BOM step, see Conventions § Products
│  │  ├─ products-status-dialog.tsx  # disable/enable confirmation, wraps ui/confirm-dialog
│  │  ├─ products-details-dialog.tsx # read-only "view full record" dialog (ProductsDetailsDialog/ProductsDetailsView) — mirrors material-pc-details-dialog.tsx, hero + ข้อมูล/BOM tabs (BOM tab client-fetched, gated on canViewBom)
│  │  ├─ products-bom-diagram.tsx    # ProductBomDiagram — radial node-and-line diagram of a BOM's material components, opened from the BOM tab

      ├─ units.ts              # listUnits()/getUnit()/create/update/deactivate/restore — real cps-api /units, see Conventions § Units master data

      └─ index.ts                # barrel export
```

## Design System (cheat sheet)

**Single fixed "Navy Enterprise" theme as of 2026-09-09 — there is no light/dark toggle anymore** (see Conventions § Theme for the full rationale/history). Tokens live in `src/app/globals.css` (`:root`, plus a `.on-navy` scoping class for the permanently-dark sidebar — not a page-wide dark mode). Use the Tailwind utility names — never hardcode hex.

| Token | Value | Use for |
|---|---|---|
| `bg-bg` | `#F5F7FA` | page background (light gray) |
| `bg-surface` | `#FFFFFF` | cards, inputs, the header |
| `bg-surface-2` | `#EDF1F6` | hover, secondary fills |
| `border-border` | `#E2E8F0` | dividers |
| `border-border-strong` | `#CBD5E1` | input borders |
| `text-fg` | `#172033` | primary text |
| `text-fg-secondary` | `#475569` | secondary text |
| `text-fg-muted` | `#64748B` | labels, hints |
| `bg-primary` / `text-primary` | `#1769D1` (Accent Blue) | primary action, links, focus ring, active states |
| `bg-primary-soft` | `#E7F1FC` | subtle primary accent |
| `bg-navy` / `text-navy` | `#0F2747` (Primary Navy) | the sidebar's own fixed dark surface, the login/auth brand panels — **not** used for page content |
| `bg-navy-dark` | `#091C33` (Primary Dark) | deeper navy accents inside the navy surfaces above |
| `text-success` | `#16A34A` | success status |
| `text-warning` | `#F59E0B` | warning status |
| `text-danger` | `#DC2626` | error status |
| `text-info` | `#0EA5E9` | informational status (not in the original brief; added for a 4th semantic tone distinct from primary) |
| `bg-{success,warning,danger,info}-soft` | — | soft tinted backgrounds for the corresponding status color, e.g. badges |

**Radius**: `--radius: 0.5rem` (8px, down from the old 10px) — intentionally a little tighter than a typical SaaS default, per the Navy Enterprise brief's "avoid overly rounded cards" direction. Components default to `rounded-md`.

**The `.on-navy` scoping class** (see globals.css) redefines `--surface`, `--surface-2`, `--border`, `--border-strong`, `--fg`, `--fg-secondary`, `--fg-muted`, `--primary`, and `--primary-soft` for its subtree — apply it to a container (`<aside className="on-navy ...">`) to make every *already-token-driven* class inside it (`bg-surface`, `text-fg-secondary`, `bg-primary-soft`, ...) resolve against the dark-navy palette instead of the light one, with zero changes to the descendant components themselves. This is the same mechanism the old `.dark` class used — see Conventions § Theme.

## Conventions

### Theme — Navy Enterprise, single fixed palette (no light/dark toggle) — 2026-09-09
- User asked for a full "Navy Enterprise" visual rebrand of the whole app with an explicit hex palette (Primary Navy `#0F2747`, Primary Dark `#091C33`, Accent Blue `#1769D1`, Background `#F5F7FA`, Card `#FFFFFF`, Text `#172033`, Border `#E2E8F0`, Success/Warning/Error as listed) and a dark navy sidebar with a white header and light-gray page background. Before touching any code, asked (and got answers on) three scope questions: (1) whole app vs. just the dashboard page → **whole app**; (2) how this interacts with the existing `next-themes` light/dark toggle → **Navy becomes the only theme, remove the toggle entirely**; (3) whether to use the brief's exact semantic hex values → **yes, exactly**.
- **This was a token-only rebrand for ~95% of the app** — because every page/component already went through this file's own "always reference tokens, never hardcode hex" discipline (Conventions § Styling, enforced since the project's start), rewriting the values in `src/app/globals.css`'s `:root` block re-themed Materials PC, Products, every master-data CRUD page, the dashboard, dialogs, badges, and buttons **with no component-level changes** — confirmed live in the browser across `/dashboard`, `/materials/pc`, `/materials/materials-receiving` after only the token file changed.
- **Two deliberate, distinct navy tokens, not one**: `--primary` (Accent Blue `#1769D1`) is the *interactive* brand color — buttons, links, focus rings, active nav-item text — and is completely separate from `--navy`/`--navy-dark` (`#0F2747`/`#091C33`), which is the sidebar/login-panel's own **fixed dark surface color**, never used for page content. Conflating the two (e.g. making `--primary` itself dark navy) would have made every button/link in the app dark-navy-on-white, which reads as muted/disabled, not as an actionable accent — the brief's own example screenshots of "enterprise" dashboards all use a saturated blue for actions against a dark-navy *chrome* color, which is exactly what two separate tokens buys.
- **The sidebar's dark navy is NOT a dark-mode toggle** — it's `src/components/layout/sidebar.tsx`'s `<aside>` (and, for parity, the mobile nav's `<SheetContent>` in `topbar.tsx`) carrying a new `.on-navy` class (see globals.css and the Design System cheat sheet above) that locally redefines `--surface`/`--surface-2`/`--border`/`--fg`/`--fg-secondary`/`--fg-muted`/`--primary`/`--primary-soft` for that subtree only. Every existing line of `sidebar-nav.tsx` (which already used only semantic tokens — `bg-surface-2`, `text-fg-secondary`, `bg-primary-soft`, `text-primary`, `border-border`) needed **zero changes** to render correctly on the dark background — this is the same CSS-variable-scoping trick the old `.dark` class used, just applied to one element's subtree instead of the whole document. `.on-navy` also overrides `--primary` to a brighter `#4C9AEF` (rather than the base `#1769D1`) specifically because the base Accent Blue is tuned for contrast against *white*, not against navy — the override keeps the active-nav-item text/icon/accent-bar clearly legible.
- **`next-themes` fully removed from the codebase** (not from `package.json` — see the note below): deleted `src/components/theme-provider.tsx` and `src/components/theme-toggle.tsx`; `src/app/layout.tsx` no longer wraps children in a `ThemeProvider`; `topbar.tsx` no longer renders the theme toggle button; `command-palette.tsx`'s "สลับธีม" (switch theme) command item and its `useTheme` import are gone. `globals.css` dropped `@custom-variant dark`, the entire `.dark { ... }` block, and the `html.dark { color-scheme: dark }` rule — `html { color-scheme: light }` is now unconditional.
- **`next-themes` is still listed in `package.json`'s dependencies** — left in place rather than removed, since removing a dependency without running `pnpm install` to sync `pnpm-lock.yaml` would leave the lockfile inconsistent, and this project's R0 rule means installs/builds aren't run without being asked. **Flagged here for a future cleanup pass**: `next-themes` has zero remaining imports anywhere in `src/` as of this change — safe to `pnpm remove next-themes` next time a lockfile sync is already happening for another reason (same "flag it, don't fix silently" precedent as the earlier `@radix-ui/react-popover`/`date-fns` cleanup entry below).
- **Login (`/login`) and the shared auth layout (`/register`, `/forgot-password`) had their own pre-existing hardcoded hex brand colors** (`#0A1628`, `#1E3A5F`, `#F59E0B`, `#0EA5A4` on the login page; `#0B0E14` plus raw `rgba(108,102,255,...)`/`rgba(45,212,206,...)` glow colors on `(auth)/layout.tsx`) that predated this task and already violated the project's own token-only rule — since these are visually the *same* "dark navy brand panel" concept the sidebar now formalizes, this pass replaced them with `bg-navy`/`text-warning`/`bg-info`/`text-primary`, i.e. real tokens instead of one-off hex. Coincidentally, the login page's pre-existing amber accent (`#F59E0B`) is now **exactly** the new `--warning` token value — no visual change there, just a real token reference instead of a magic hex literal. The auth layout's decorative radial-gradient glow (an inline `style` attribute, which can't reference Tailwind's arbitrary-color classes) keeps literal `rgba(...)` values, but they're now the literal RGB equivalents of `--primary`/`--warning` (with a code comment saying so) rather than the old unrelated indigo/teal.
- **Chart colors remapped for a more muted, professional palette**: `--chart-1..5` are now Accent Blue, Success Green, Warning Amber, Navy, and Slate Gray (was an indigo/teal/amber/rose/gray set) — still 5 visually distinct hues, but drawn from the same restrained enterprise palette instead of introducing new saturated colors just for charts. No chart *component* changed — `stock-movement-chart.tsx`/`weekly-throughput-chart.tsx` already reference `var(--chart-2)`/`var(--chart-3)` by CSS variable, not literal color, so they picked up the new green/amber automatically.
- **Radius tightened from `0.625rem` to `0.5rem`** (10px → 8px base) — a modest, token-level change per the brief's "avoid overly rounded cards" direction. **Not chased further than the token**: many components use Tailwind's literal `rounded-xl`/`rounded-2xl` classes (Editorial cards, the Sheet drawer, the dashboard's `ChartCard`/`KpiCard`) which are Tailwind's own fixed scale, not derived from `--radius` unless a matching `--radius-xl`/`--radius-2xl` override is added to `@theme inline` — only `sm`/`md`/`lg`/`xl` are currently remapped (unchanged from before this pass). Revisit if a future pass wants every literal rounded-* class pulled down too; this pass intentionally scoped to the base token to keep the change reviewable.
- **Verified live** using a disposable temp SUPER_ADMIN account (created via `create-super-admin.ts`, deleted afterward): login page (navy panel + amber accents + accent-blue submit button), `/dashboard` (navy sidebar with a clearly legible accent-blue active item + accent bar, white header, light-gray page bg, white KPI/chart cards), `/materials/pc` (navy sidebar with an expanded active submenu, accent-blue "เพิ่มวัสดุ" button, accent-blue toggle switches, green status badges), `/materials/materials-receiving` (accent-colored status cards), the mobile hamburger drawer (matches the desktop sidebar's navy via the same `.on-navy` class on its `SheetContent`), and the collapsed desktop sidebar (icon-only navy rail, accent-blue active icon tile) — all confirmed with zero console errors on a fresh tab (a batch of console errors seen mid-session were confirmed stale, from earlier in-session compiles, not the final code). Per R0, `tsc`/`eslint`/`build` were **not** run automatically this pass; given the size of this change (every token in `globals.css`, 2 files deleted, 6 files edited), running `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` and `pnpm lint` before merging is strongly recommended.
- **Rule for future rebrand/theme work in this app**: (1) a genuinely global visual change should start with the token file, never a per-component pass — this codebase's existing "tokens everywhere, no raw hex" discipline is exactly what makes a whole-app rebrand a small, reviewable diff instead of touching hundreds of files; (2) a UI element that must have a **fixed** appearance regardless of the rest of the theme (here, the sidebar) is a scoping class that locally overrides the same token names, not a parallel set of hardcoded classes — this preserves every existing token-driven line of markup in that subtree for free; (3) before deleting a now-unused dependency, check whether removing it requires a lockfile sync (`pnpm install`) — if R0/no-auto-install applies, leave the dependency in place and explicitly flag it in AGENTS.md instead of silently leaving a dangling removal for someone else to discover.

### Theme — Typography: Geist Sans replaced with IBM Plex Sans Thai (whole app) — same day follow-up
- User asked to "redesign the text according to the theme" (ตามธีม) right after the Navy Enterprise rebrand above. Investigation found a real, pre-existing defect exposed (not introduced) by that rebrand's new focus on visual polish: **Geist Sans — the app's only UI font before this change — has no Thai glyphs at all.** Since this app's UI text is majority Thai, every Thai character in the entire app was silently falling back to the browser/OS's generic system UI font (`ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif`), which visibly differs in weight and proportions from the Latin/numeral text sitting right next to it (set in actual Geist). This wasn't specific to the Navy Enterprise change — it was there from the start — but "redesign the text to match the theme" is exactly the ask that surfaces it.
- **Replaced Geist Sans with IBM Plex Sans Thai** (`next/font/google`, `subsets: ["thai", "latin"]`, weights 400/500/600/700, self-hosted by Next at build time — not a runtime Google Fonts CDN dependency) as `--font-sans` in `src/app/layout.tsx`/`globals.css`. IBM Plex Sans Thai covers **both** scripts in one type family — chosen because it's one of the three fonts explicitly named as suitable for this app's Thai UI in the Navy Enterprise brief itself (Noto Sans Thai / Inter / IBM Plex Sans Thai), and its slightly technical, geometric character fits "Navy Enterprise" better than Noto Sans Thai's more neutral/rounded default. Every Thai and Latin/numeral character in the app now renders from the same family — no more visible seam between a Thai word and an adjacent English/number string in the same sentence.
- **`GeistMono` is kept, unchanged, for genuinely monospace content** (lot codes like `CCI-26J07-001`, the `⌘K` command-palette hint) — those are always Latin/numeric, so they never hit the Thai-glyph gap, and Geist Mono's tighter technical look suits that role better than a Thai-covering family would.
- **`<html lang="en">` corrected to `lang="th"`** — the app's actual primary language, which also helps the browser pick sensible default fonts/hyphenation for any text that ever falls through to a fallback.
- Verified the font actually downloads and applies (this environment does have outbound network access, confirmed by `getComputedStyle(document.body).fontFamily` returning `"IBM Plex Sans Thai", "IBM Plex Sans Thai Fallback", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif"` after a real login) and rendered correctly on `/login`, `/dashboard`, and `/materials/materials-receiving` using a disposable temp SUPER_ADMIN account (deleted afterward) — Thai and Latin text now sit at consistent weight/size everywhere checked, and the mono lot codes still render in Geist Mono as expected. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on `layout.tsx`. One transient `TypeError: fetch failed` console entry appeared mid-session during a live compile — investigated and confirmed to be a stale HMR artifact from Turbopack recompiling while the font import was mid-edit, not a persistent issue (a fresh tab against the same running server showed zero errors and the font loading correctly).
- **Rule for future font changes in this app**: before adding or trusting any font, check whether it actually covers Thai glyphs if the surrounding UI text is Thai — a font that looks fine in an English-only design tool preview can silently fall back to the OS default for every non-Latin character in production. `next/font/google` is safe to use here (this environment has outbound network access and Next self-hosts the downloaded files, so there's no runtime CDN dependency once built) — don't assume it's unavailable without testing it first, and don't confuse a transient dev-server HMR error with a real, reproducible failure — verify with a fresh tab before concluding a change is broken.

### Styling
- Always reference tokens (`bg-primary`, `text-fg-muted`) — never raw hex.
- **No dark mode / theme toggle** — this app has one fixed Navy Enterprise theme (see Conventions § Theme above). Don't add `dark:` variants or a `useTheme()` call; there is no `.dark` class anymore.
- Tailwind 4 syntax — no `tailwind.config.*`. All tokens live in `globals.css` via `@theme inline`.
- Lucide icons: `import { IconName } from "lucide-react"`. v1.39 has all the common icons.

### Components
- **Always reuse** `src/components/ui/*` primitives. Don't recreate Button/Input/Label.
- Button variants: `primary | secondary | outline | ghost | danger | link`. Sizes: `sm | md | lg | icon`.
- Input defaults to `h-9`; override to `h-10` for prominent forms.
- Forms: `react-hook-form` + `zod` + `@hookform/resolvers/zod`. Toast via `sonner`.

### Routing & Layouts
- Route groups `(name)/` are for **layout sharing** — they do not affect URLs.
- A page that needs its own full-bleed layout (no parent chrome) lives at the top level (e.g. `src/app/login/`).
- `(auth)/layout.tsx` provides the testimonial split — only routes that want that chrome stay inside.
- **Resolved 2026-09-03, see ADR-005**: pages under `(dashboard)/` are real top-level routes (`/products`, `/users`, `/menus`, ...) matching cps-api's own menu `path` values exactly — not nested under a `/dashboard/*` prefix. `/dashboard` itself is the one route that keeps that segment (it's cps-api's own path for the dashboard menu item too). Don't add a new dashboard page under a `dashboard/` subfolder — put it directly under `(dashboard)/` at its own path.

### Dashboard shell
- Dashboard chrome uses a **floating-box app shell**: `DashboardShell` owns the full `h-dvh` viewport and outer responsive gap/padding; desktop `Sidebar`, `Topbar`, and the page-content scroller are separate rounded bordered surfaces.
- The document/dashboard shell itself must not scroll. `Topbar` and desktop `Sidebar` remain fixed in the viewport; only the `main` surface scrolls (`min-h-0`, `overflow-y-auto`, contained overscroll, stable scrollbar gutter).
- Below `lg`, the desktop sidebar is replaced by the existing Radix `Sheet`. Keep the mobile navbar visible, render the menu inside its own bordered surface, and close the Sheet through `SidebarNav#onNavigate` after a route is selected.
- Collapsed desktop navigation is icon-only, so every hidden-label link/button must retain an accessible name and hover title.
- `SidebarNav` hides its visual scrollbar with standard `scrollbar-width` plus a WebKit fallback while retaining `overflow-y-auto`; wheel, touch, and keyboard scrolling must keep working in both the desktop sidebar and mobile Sheet.
- **Scrollbar treatment is project-wide as of 2026-09-06** — every ordinary vertical scroller (`overflow-y-auto` / `overflow-auto`) in the app carries `[scrollbar-width:none] [&::-webkit-scrollbar]:hidden`, including the page-content scroller in `dashboard-shell.tsx` and the dialog primitive in `ui/dialog.tsx` (which propagates the treatment to every `<Dialog>` and `<Sheet>` without per-call-site duplication). The deliberate exception is a wide data table: Materials/Products use bounded `max-h-[68vh] overflow-auto` containers with visible scrollbars so users retain both the horizontal "more columns" cue and direct control over the independently scrolling table body. When adding a normal vertical scroller, mirror the hide classes; when adding a wide table, preserve its visible overflow cue.

### State
- Default to **server components**. Add `"use client"` only when you need interactivity, hooks, or browser APIs.
- Use `useRouter` from `next/navigation` (not the deprecated `next/router`).

### API
- Backend is REST; base URL comes from `API_BASE_URL` (server-only env var, see `.env.local.example`). No public backend URL exists yet — placeholder only, fill in `.env.local` when one is available.
- Fetch **directly from Server Components** (Next.js 16 recommended pattern) via `src/lib/api/*` — no BFF proxy route handlers. Do not `fetch()` the backend ad-hoc from inside a component; add/extend a resource file in `src/lib/api/`.
- `src/lib/api/client.ts` exports `apiFetch<T>(path, init)`: prefixes `API_BASE_URL`, sets `Content-Type: application/json`, attaches `Authorization: Bearer ${API_AUTH_TOKEN}` if that env var is set, and throws `ApiError` (with `.status`/`.body`) on non-2xx.
- One file per resource (`users.ts`, `products.ts`, `orders.ts`), each returning the existing domain types from `src/lib/types.ts` — the API layer must not introduce parallel/duplicate types.
- `src/lib/data.ts` (mock generator) is untouched and still what pages currently render (except `/login`, see below). Swapping a page from mock data to `src/lib/api/*` is a separate, explicit task per page — don't do it silently as a side effect of unrelated work.

### Sidebar permissions — resolved, see ADR-004 (supersedes ADR-003) and ADR-005 (routes)
- **The sidebar is now driven directly by cps-api's real menu tree**, not a static list. `src/lib/session.ts#getCurrentSession()` exposes `menus: MenuNode[]` from `GET /auth/me`'s `accessControl.menus` — already filtered server-side to the active role's real permissions (see `cps-api/src/modules/access-control/services/menu-tree.service.ts`). `src/components/layout/sidebar-nav.tsx` renders that tree recursively (arbitrary depth, expand/collapse per node).
- `src/lib/nav.ts#menuHref(path)` maps a backend path **directly** to a local route — cps-api's paths (`/materials`, `/products`, `/dashboard`, ...) already match this app's real top-level routes 1:1 (see ADR-005), so there's no prefixing left to do; `menuHref` only substitutes `/dashboard` when a menu node has no `path` at all.
- `src/app/(dashboard)/[...rest]/page.tsx` is a catch-all placeholder ("this page isn't built yet") for real, permission-granted menu items that don't have a page implemented — keeps the dashboard chrome instead of falling through to the bare root `not-found.tsx`. Real backend paths that now land on an actually-built page calling the real API: `/dashboard`, `/materials/pc`, `/menus`, `/products` (see Conventions § Products). `/users` still renders `src/lib/data.ts` mock content (see below).
- `src/lib/menu-icons.ts#menuIcon(name)` maps cps-api's kebab-case `Menu.icon` (e.g. `"layout-dashboard"`) to a Lucide component via an explicit named-import map (not a blanket `import *`, to keep the bundle lean) — unmapped names fall back to a plain dot. **Add a new entry here whenever cps-api seeds a new menu icon**, or it'll render as a generic dot. `resolveMenuIcons()` pre-resolves an entire tree once (via `useMemo` in `SidebarNav`) — icons are intentionally resolved as data *before* being passed as props, not looked up inline inside the component that renders `<Icon />`, because the latter trips the React Compiler's "components created during render" lint rule (`react-hooks/static-components`).
- `secondaryNav` in `src/lib/nav.ts` (currently just `Settings`, href `/settings`) stays a small static list for account-level items with no cps-api menu equivalent — not permission-gated, since every logged-in user can reach their own settings regardless of role. **As of 2026-09-06 it is no longer rendered in the sidebar** (the "ทั่วไป" / "ตั้งค่า" tier at the bottom of `SidebarNav` was removed per user request) but is still consumed by the topbar's `useBreadcrumb` so the Settings page still resolves its own label. The Settings page itself is unchanged.
- **Known gap, narrowed**: a menu item being visible does not mean its page calls the real API — `/users` still renders `src/lib/data.ts` mock content regardless of what permission actually let the user click there. Wiring it to `src/lib/api/users.ts` is a separate task (`/products` was wired to the real API — see Conventions § Products).
- Before wiring a new page to a real cps-api module: check `resolveMenuIcons`/the live tree for the exact `code`/`path` cps-api actually sends (don't guess) — `API_ENDPOINTS.md` documents the REST contract but not the seeded menu catalog itself; the menu catalog can drift from the endpoint list.

### Menu management (drag-and-drop tree editor) — `/menus`
- Backend contract for this page is **not in `API_ENDPOINTS.md`** (that doc's § 4.4 only lists the basic `/menus` CRUD routes) — it lives entirely in `cps-api/src/modules/menus/{menus.controller,menus.service,menu-tree-ordering}.ts`. Re-read those three files before touching this feature; don't rely on the endpoint doc here.
- Two purpose-built endpoints drive it: `GET /menus/management-tree` → `{ version, menus: ManagementMenuNode[] }` (unfiltered — includes inactive/hidden menus, unlike `/menus/tree`), and `PATCH /menus/reorder` → `{ version, items: [{id, parentId, sortOrder}, ...] }`. Both are **SUPER_ADMIN only** (`@Roles(SUPER_ADMIN)` on the whole controller) — `src/app/(dashboard)/menus/page.tsx` gates on `session.user.isSuperAdmin` and shows a friendly message instead of letting the page hit a 403.
- The reorder endpoint uses **optimistic concurrency**: `version` is a sha256 hash of every menu row's `(id, parentId, sortOrder, menuType, updatedAt)`, computed server-side. A stale `version` → `409`; the client must show that as a conflict and refetch, not retry blindly (`saveMenuOrderAction` in `menus/actions.ts` does this, calling `refreshMenuTreeAction` on `409`).
- **`items` must include every menu record, not just the ones that moved** — the backend 400s on a partial set (`validateAndProjectMenuLayout` in `menu-tree-ordering.ts`). `src/lib/menu-tree.ts#toReorderItems()` always derives the full payload from the complete flattened tree.
- Backend validation rules the client should avoid violating (it's re-validated server-side either way, but a client that ignores these just produces confusing 400s): sibling `sortOrder` must be contiguous 0-based per parent, max nesting depth is `cps-api`'s `MAX_MENU_DEPTH = 4` (mirrored client-side as `MAX_FLAT_DEPTH = 3`, 0-indexed), no cycles, and a `BUTTON`-type menu can't have children. `src/lib/menu-tree.ts#getDragProjection()` clamps the live drag preview to these constraints (see its `canHaveChildren` check) so the UI doesn't let you drop somewhere the API would reject.
- The drag algorithm is the standard dnd-kit "sortable tree" pattern: `flattenMenuTree()` depth-first-flattens the tree (so a node and its descendants are always a contiguous array run), horizontal drag offset (`event.delta.x` ÷ `INDENT_WIDTH`) projects a new depth/parent per `getDragProjection()`, and `applyMoveNode()` rebuilds the full flattened order on drop — including reinserting the dragged node's hidden-during-drag descendant subtree right after it, depth-shifted by the same delta. Projection must inspect neighbours after moving the active row to its `overIndex`; using the source index retains the old `parentId` on cross-parent drops and makes saved moves appear to revert. It also subtracts `getSubtreeDepth()` from the allowed maximum so descendants cannot exceed level 4. When that subtree-safe maximum is below the insertion boundary's required minimum depth, the projection is invalid and the drop must be rejected rather than lowering the minimum (which would corrupt depth-first subtree boundaries). These are pure functions in `src/lib/menu-tree.ts`, deliberately kept framework-agnostic and separate from the dnd-kit wiring in `menu-tree-editor.tsx`.
- Always pass a deterministic `id` to `DndContext` on server-rendered pages. dnd-kit's fallback `useUniqueId()` uses a module-level counter that persists across server requests, so omitting the ID makes the server's `aria-describedby` drift from the browser's initial value and triggers a hydration mismatch. The menu editor uses `menu-tree-dnd`; keep it unique within the page.
- Icons: same "resolve as data, not inline" rule as the sidebar (see above) — `MenuTreeRow`/`MenuTreeDragPreview` receive `Icon: LucideIcon` as a prop resolved by the parent's `useMemo`, they never call `menuIcon()` themselves.
- Edits are staged client-side (`items` state) until "Save changes" — no auto-save per drag, since every save must submit the complete, valid arrangement. "Reset" reverts to the last-saved snapshot.
- **CRUD is wired to the real API as of 2026-09-10**: `menu-form-dialog.tsx` creates with `POST /menus` and edits code/names/type/path/icon/visibility/activity with `PATCH /menus/:id`; `menu-delete-dialog.tsx` uses `DELETE /menus/:id`. Every successful mutation immediately refetches `GET /menus/management-tree` and replaces both the visible items and optimistic-concurrency `version`, so a later reorder can never submit the pre-mutation hash. CRUD actions are intentionally unavailable while a drag reorder is staged — save or reset the staged layout first, otherwise refreshing after a CRUD mutation would silently discard it.
- **Delete is a guarded hard delete, not the soft-delete pattern used by master data**: cps-api rejects a menu that still has children or permissions. The client blocks the known child case before opening confirmation and surfaces the backend message for permission-bound menus. Use the edit dialog's `isActive` switch when the intent is reversible deactivation.
- **Remaining permissions gap**: this CRUD pass manages menu records, not their permission/action associations. cps-api accepts permissions during `POST /menus` but exposes no permission updates through `PATCH /menus/:id`; role/permission assignment needs its own contract-aware UI rather than being folded into the record editor. A newly created menu therefore needs permissions assigned through the IAM workflow before non-SUPER_ADMIN users can receive it in their filtered navigation tree.

### Dashboard home (`/dashboard`) — BI/Analytics redesign, 2026-09-09
- User asked for a "modern Analytics/BI Dashboard" redesign of the existing `/dashboard` home — card-based grid, KPI row, a real filter sidebar with a mobile drawer, chart cards with header/body/footer, clear visual hierarchy — **without changing business logic, API contracts, or removing any existing data/functionality**. Since the page's content was (and remains) 100% static mock data with **no filter system at all** to reuse (see Conventions § API for why this page still renders mock content), the redesign built a real, working, entirely **client-side** filter/date-range layer over the existing exported mock arrays in `src/lib/dashboard-data.ts` — nothing was invented as a fake/decorative control, and no backend contract was touched.
- **`page.tsx` is now a thin Server Component shell** — it only does the session read for the greeting name and renders `<DashboardView greeting={...} />`. Every KPI/filter/date-range/CSV-export interaction lives in the new `"use client"` `dashboard-view.tsx`, which imports the *same* mock arrays the old page imported directly (`dashboardStats`, `stockMovementTrend`, `materialCategoryBreakdown`, `weeklyThroughput`, `recentDocuments`, `floorActivity`, `lowStockWatchlist`) — no new data source, no new API call.
- **New reusable primitives** (`src/components/dashboard/`): `KpiCard` (+ `Sparkline`, a hand-rolled inline-SVG line — not a recharts instance per card, to avoid 4 extra ResizeObservers for a ~10-point line), `ChartCard` (+ `ChartLegendItem`, `DashboardEmptyState`), `DashboardHeader`, `DashboardFilters`. **`StatCard` (the pre-existing component) was left completely untouched** — it's still used by `/analytics`, which this task explicitly did not touch; `KpiCard` is a new, separate component for the dashboard home only. Don't merge the two or rename one into the other.
- **Every existing chart/list/table component gained an *optional* data-narrowing prop that defaults to the original full mock array** — `StockMovementChart({ data = stockMovementTrend })`, `CategoryBreakdownChart({ data = materialCategoryBreakdown })`, `RecentDocuments({ documents = recentDocuments.slice(0, 6) })`, `LowStockWatchlist({ items = lowStockWatchlist })`, `RecentActivity({ items = floorActivity.slice(0, 6) })`, `WeeklyThroughputChart({ height })`. A bare `<StockMovementChart />` (if anything else ever imported it that way) renders byte-identical to before — this is what "reuse existing charts, don't rewrite them" meant in practice: extend the signature additively, never replace the internals wholesale. Each of these also gained a proper **empty state** (`DashboardEmptyState`, icon + Thai message + optional "ล้างตัวกรอง" action) for when a filter combination yields nothing — previously there was no such state because nothing could ever be empty (no filters existed).
- **The filter/date-range state is real, not decorative**: `DashboardFilters` (search text + doc-type checkboxes + status checkboxes + category checkboxes) narrows `recentDocuments`/`materialCategoryBreakdown`/`lowStockWatchlist`/`floorActivity` live via `Array.filter` in `dashboard-view.tsx` — verified end-to-end in the browser (typing "Hex Bolt" narrowed the documents table from 8→3, the watchlist from 5→1, and the activity feed from 6→1, simultaneously, with the "ตัวกรอง1" badge and "แสดง 3 จาก 8 เอกสาร" description updating live). The header's date-range `<Select>` (7/14 days — the only two honest options, since the mock trend series is exactly 14 days long, see `dashboard-header.tsx`'s comment) slices `stockMovementTrend` and recomputes the received/disbursed/net totals and the KPI sparklines from that same slice — a real derivation, not a second data source.
- **CSV export is a real, working feature, not a stub**: `handleExport()` in `dashboard-view.tsx` builds a CSV client-side from the currently-filtered `documents` array (respects whatever filters are active) and triggers a browser download via an object-URL `<a download>` — no new backend endpoint, since the data was already fully loaded client-side. Includes a leading UTF-8 BOM so Excel opens the Thai column headers correctly instead of mojibake.
- **Refresh is real too**: the header's "รีเฟรช" button calls `router.refresh()` wrapped in `useTransition` (spinning icon + "กำลังรีเฟรช" label while pending) — genuinely re-runs the page's Server Component (the session/greeting read), same as any other page's refresh pattern in this app, not a fake spinner.
- **Layout uses container queries, not viewport breakpoints, for every internal grid** (`@container` + `@min-[Nrem]:grid-cols-N`) — same technique `material-pc-table.tsx`'s card grid already established. This was **not optional polish**: a viewport-based `xl:grid-cols-4` put 4 KPI cards in the ~910px of actual content width left over once the app's own sidebar (and, below the KPI row, the new 236px filter rail) are subtracted, which visibly crushed every card's text before the fix. Verified by measuring the real container width via `getComputedStyle` in the live browser (910px content → 4 KPI columns at `@min-[54rem]`, 654px chart-grid content → 2-3 columns at `@min-[38rem]`/`@min-[62rem]`) rather than guessing breakpoint numbers from the viewport size alone.
- **Filter sidebar (`aside`, ~236px, `lg:` and up) becomes a `Sheet` drawer below `lg`** — same `ui/sheet.tsx` primitive the mobile nav already uses, triggered by a "ตัวกรอง" button in the header (with an active-filter-count badge) that only renders `lg:hidden`. The drawer renders the exact same `DashboardFilters` component with a different `idPrefix` (so checkbox/input `id`s never collide with the sidebar instance if both existed in the DOM at once) and a footer "ดูผลลัพธ์ (N เอกสาร)" button showing a live count before closing. Verified at 390×844 (drawer opens, all filter groups render, footer count updates) and 768×1024 (2×2 KPI grid, drawer button visible, sidebar hidden).
- **Donut chart (`CategoryBreakdownChart`) changed from a row layout to always-stacked** (`flex-col`, dropped the old `sm:flex-row`) — in its new 1-of-3 grid-column home the donut-plus-legend row layout squeezed category names to nothing; stacked (donut on top, legend list below, full card width) reads correctly at every card width this component now actually lives at. Also added a **percentage share next to the raw weight** in the legend (`{Math.round((value/total)*100)}%`) — the donut's own slice angles aren't readable to a precise percentage and aren't accessible without hovering, so the number is now stated in text too (see Conventions § Accessibility note on not relying on visual-only cues).
- **Chart legends moved out of the chart body and into the enclosing `ChartCard`'s header** (`WeeklyThroughputChart` dropped its internal recharts `<Legend>`; the stock-movement and weekly-throughput cards both pass `<ChartLegendItem>` pairs as the `ChartCard`'s `actions` prop instead) — every chart card on the page now presents its series key the same way, in the same place, rather than some having an internal chart legend and others not.
- **Status is still never color-only**: `RecentDocuments`' status badges gained explicit Thai labels (`ยืนยันแล้ว`/`ร่าง`/`ยกเลิก`) alongside the existing `dot` + `variant` color — the original version showed only the English enum value (`Confirmed`/`Draft`/`Cancelled`) with no Thai translation, inconsistent with every other Thai-labeled status badge in the app.
- **Visual hierarchy, per the request's own priority order (KPI → trend → comparison → distribution → detail)**: the trend chart (`การเคลื่อนไหวสต็อก`) is the only chart that spans 2 of 3 grid columns — every other chart/table gets exactly 1 column except the documents table, which spans the full width as the "drill-down" tier beneath the summary charts. This is a deliberate, stated choice (see the `dashboard-view.tsx` code comments marking each card as "Tier 1" through "Tier 5") — not every panel has equal visual weight, per the request's explicit "do not give every chart equal visual importance" instruction.
- **Loading skeleton (`loading.tsx`) rebuilt to mirror the new layout exactly** (header row → 4 KPI skeletons → filter-rail skeleton + chart-grid skeletons) so Suspense fallback doesn't reflow into a different shape once real content mounts.
- **Nothing about `/analytics` changed** — it still imports the untouched `StatCard` and its own untouched chart components (`revenue-chart.tsx`, `traffic-chart.tsx`, `weekly-chart.tsx`), confirmed still rendering correctly with zero console errors after this pass (see AGENTS.md's earlier 2026-09-02 dashboard-home redesign entry for why Analytics and the Dashboard home have visually diverged since that pass — this redesign didn't change that state, and Analytics is still a separate future task if it's ever asked for).
- Verified live end-to-end using a disposable temporary SUPER_ADMIN account (created via `create-super-admin.ts`, deleted afterward): desktop (1280px, 4-col KPI + 3-col chart grid, all totals/percentages correct), tablet (768px, 2×2 KPI + full-width charts + drawer trigger), mobile (390px, 1-col KPI stack + working filter drawer with live result count), search-filter narrowing 3 independent panels simultaneously, date-range switching recomputing the trend chart + totals + KPI sparklines, zero horizontal overflow at any tested width, zero console errors (one batch of stale HMR parse-error console entries from a mid-edit compile was investigated and confirmed to be stale — a fresh tab against the same running dev server showed zero errors and fully correct content). Per R0, no `tsc`/`eslint`/`build` was run this pass (asked the user explicitly; they said skip it) — recommend running `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` and `pnpm lint` before merging, since this touched 9 existing files plus 4 new ones.
- **Rule for future "redesign an existing data page without changing its data" requests**: (1) check whether the page already has a real filter/data-fetching layer to reuse — if it genuinely doesn't (as here), build a real client-side filter over the data already loaded rather than either inventing a fake backend contract or shipping decorative, non-functional filter UI; (2) extend existing chart/list components with *optional*, defaulted props rather than rewriting their internals — every call site that doesn't pass the new prop must render exactly as before; (3) measure real container widths in the live browser before choosing container-query breakpoints — a sidebar (and, here, a new filter rail) can eat enough width that viewport-based Tailwind breakpoints silently create a cramped layout that looks fine in isolation but breaks once the app shell is accounted for.

### Materials PC (CRUD) — `/materials/pc` — first real (non-mock) CRUD page, see ADR-006
- `/materials/pc` is **not a distinct cps-api module** — cps-api's `MaterialsController` is a single generic `/materials` REST resource (`cps-api/src/modules/materials/{materials.controller,materials.service,dto/*}.ts`, documented in `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 6). "PC" is one value of the `type` field (`PC | OF | OF_MAT`). This page always sends/filters `type: "PC"` — it's a *view* over the shared materials table, not its own backend concept. Don't go looking for a "materials-pc" controller in cps-api; there isn't one.
- **This is the first page in the app wired to real cps-api data with real mutations** (menus was real data too, but reorder-only; this is full create/update/soft-delete/restore). Treat it as the reference pattern for wiring up the next mock page (Users, Products, Orders): Server Component fetches list + lookups directly (`src/lib/api/materials.ts`), filters live in the URL (`?page=&search=&status=`) so they're bookmookable and drive the server refetch, mutations go through Server Actions (`materials/pc/actions.ts`) that call `revalidatePath` on success, and the client only holds UI state (which dialog is open, for which row) — never a client-side copy of the list that could drift from the server.
- **Soft delete, not hard delete**: `DELETE /materials/:id` (called via `deactivateMaterial`) sets `isActive: false` server-side; the row is never removed. There's a dedicated `PATCH /materials/:id/restore`. The UI must never call this "Delete" — it's "Disable" (with a confirm dialog) and "Enable" (also confirmed, non-destructively) for inactive rows. This mirrors a UX pattern already established in a separate sibling app (`cps-app`) for the same backend resource — keep using it for any future soft-delete resource (units, suppliers, etc. all follow the same `isActive`/restore shape per `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 5.1).
- **Update requires optimistic concurrency**: `UpdateMaterialDto.updatedAt` is a *required* ISO8601 field, not optional — the edit form must carry the row's current `updatedAt` forward and resubmit it; a stale value 409s. `material-pc-form-dialog.tsx` reads `material.updatedAt` at submit time; `materials/pc/actions.ts#errorResult()` maps a 409 to a "this was updated elsewhere, refresh" toast rather than a generic error. Don't make `updatedAt` optional or drop it from the payload.
- **Responsive dialog**: `src/components/ui/dialog.tsx#DialogContent` gained two *opt-in* props — `size` (`"lg" | "xl"`, only meaningful with the prop below) and `fullScreenOnMobile` — that make it a full-screen sheet below the `sm:` breakpoint and a centered sized dialog from `sm:` up, pure CSS (Tailwind breakpoint classes), no JS viewport detection. Existing call sites (`product-form-dialog.tsx`, `user-form-dialog.tsx`, `confirm-dialog.tsx`) don't pass either prop and are byte-for-byte unaffected — the default path is untouched from before this change. Use `fullScreenOnMobile` for any new dialog with enough fields that a small mobile viewport needs the full screen (i.e. most create/edit forms); leave simple confirm dialogs on the default centered-always behavior.
- `src/components/ui/confirm-dialog.tsx` gained an optional `variant?: "danger" | "default"` prop (default `"danger"`, so every existing call site is unaffected) — use `"default"` for a non-destructive confirmation like "Enable this material?" so the icon/button aren't alarming red for a reversible, positive action.
- **User-chosen table/card/list view — reusable, not materials-specific**: `src/hooks/use-view-mode.ts#useViewMode(key, default)` and `src/components/ui/view-toggle.tsx#ViewToggle` are generic — any list page can adopt the same toggle by calling `useViewMode("<page-key>")` and switching its own renderers on the returned value. `useViewMode` reads/writes `localStorage` (`view-mode:<key>`) via `useSyncExternalStore`, not `useState` + `useEffect` — this project's lint config flags `setState` inside an effect as an error (see the sidebar's `activeChainIds` for the same "derive, don't effect" principle), and `useSyncExternalStore` is the React-native way to read external mutable state without one; the server snapshot is always `defaultValue` so SSR markup matches on first paint, and same-tab updates are propagated by dispatching a synthetic `StorageEvent` (native ones only fire in *other* tabs). `ViewToggle` takes a `modes: readonly ViewMode[]` prop (default `["table", "card"]`) so adding a new view mode to `ViewMode` doesn't silently change every consumer's toggle — Materials PC and Products both explicitly pass `modes={["table", "card", "list"]}`. Each collection renders only the selected layout; container queries inside the card grid control column count based on available content width, not viewport width. All three views share the same resource-specific row-action logic and image-preview flow so permissions and behavior cannot drift.
- **Materials PC: 3-way view (Editorial card + Compact Row + Table) — 2026-09-05**: `MaterialPcCollection` renders three completely different layouts based on `view`:
  - `"table"` (existing) — `<Table>` with `size-10` thumbnails + Meatballs action menu in a right-aligned cell; smallest mental overhead, best for power users with keyboard nav.
  - `"card"` = **Compact inventory card** — image-led 16:9 hero, code/name/type identity, current stock + real minimum-stock health, 3-cell operational strip (minimum/loading point/process line), primary `+ รับเข้า`, secondary `รายละเอียด`, and one Meatballs menu for edit/status. The shorter format is designed to show more inventory per viewport without losing the photograph or the stock decision.
  - `"list"` = **Compact Row** — single horizontal row: `size-14` thumbnail on the left, identity (code eyebrow + name + type·model·suppliers meta) in the middle, right-aligned stock number + Meatballs action menu. Designed for scanning 50+ materials at once (one row ≈ 80px vertical).
  - Default for Materials PC is `"card"`; existing users keep their stored choice. Stock health now uses cps-api's `minimumStock`: current `<= 0` → out of stock, current `> 0 && current < minimumStock` → low stock, otherwise normal. Never reintroduce the old hard-coded 10-unit heuristic.
- **Enterprise inventory redesign — 2026-09-09**: users with `MATERIALS_RECEIVING_VIEW` now read the permission-protected `GET /stock-balances/inventory` endpoint instead of merging a single material page with the unpaginated balance list. The endpoint performs stock filtering/sorting/KPI counts across the full PC dataset, returns `lastReceivedAt` from confirmed receipts, and keeps stock data behind the existing receiving-view permission. Four clickable KPI cards are stock-status facets; the filter drawer covers stock status, supplier, model, loading point, and production line; table sorting supports code/name/current stock/last received; page sizes are 20/40/60. Users without stock permission still use `GET /materials`, can manage master data per their existing permissions, and never receive stock quantities/KPIs.
- `master.materials.minimum_stock` is a non-negative `NUMERIC(18,4)` with database default `0`; the create/edit form always exposes it. The table is a sticky-header operational view (image, code, name, current/minimum/status, supplier, loading point, process line, active status, last receipt, Meatballs). Card responsiveness is container-based at 1/2/3/4/5 columns; retain a usable single-column fallback.
- **Not (yet) applied to Products/Users/Orders**: those tables are still bespoke, mock-data components with no card renderer of their own. `useViewMode`/`ViewToggle` are ready to reuse as-is, but each table's *card markup* still has to be hand-written per resource (column sets differ) — same conclusion `cps-app`'s own pattern reached (filters/list/form-modal/status-dialog is "structurally identical but not one generic component" across its resources). Adding a card view to another table means writing that table's own `<Resource>Collection`-shaped component and wiring the same two shared pieces, not extracting a single generic `<DataCollection>`.
- **Material images — thumbnail, card, and full preview**: the table uses a `size-10` rounded thumbnail (`object-cover` is acceptable at that tiny size). The compact card uses a restrained `16:9` token-driven image stage and `object-contain` so no material detail is cropped; the 2026-09-09 enterprise redesign intentionally removed the earlier neon/carbon-fiber/glowing-pedestal treatment. Both views retain an accessible `ImageOff` fallback and clicking the photo opens the shared `material-pc-image-preview.tsx#MaterialPcImagePreview` lightbox (full uncropped image plus Download). `next.config.ts` rewrites same-origin `/uploads/*` requests to the origin derived from server-only `API_BASE_URL`, matching cps-api's public upload paths without exposing the API URL to client JavaScript — this same-origin-ness is also what makes the download button's `<a download>` trigger a real browser save instead of just navigating (a cross-origin `download` attribute would be ignored by the browser).
- **Create requires an image upload; edit can replace it**: the form accepts JPEG, PNG, or WebP up to 5 MiB and previews the selected file locally. Its `MaterialImagePicker` is a full-width dashed selection card: the native file input remains focusable but visually hidden, its explicit label makes the whole panel clickable, desktop uses a preview/content split, and mobile stacks the same content. Create and legacy rows without an image require a file; edit shows the current image and leaves the input optional. If no replacement is selected, update omits `imagePath` so the existing image remains untouched. If a replacement is selected, the same upload action stages it and PATCH sends the staged path; cps-api promotes the replacement and removes the prior committed file only after the transaction succeeds. Selected state shows the preview, filename, size, and replace wording; error state adds an icon and text so it does not rely on color alone. Upload calls `uploadMaterialPcImageAction`, which forwards multipart field `file` to `POST /materials/images`; cps-api ultimately stores it under the configured `MATERIAL_IMAGE_ROOT` (`D:/project-cps/New/image/materials` in the current backend environment). `next.config.ts` sets `experimental.serverActions.bodySizeLimit` to `6mb` because Next.js defaults Server Action request bodies to 1 MB while the backend permits 5 MiB images; keep the limit slightly above the file maximum for multipart overhead. `apiFetch()` must not set `Content-Type: application/json` when its body is `FormData`, so the runtime can generate the multipart boundary.
- Permission gating: real `MATERIAL_VIEW`/`CREATE`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` codes (not the dotted `MATERIALS_PC_MANAGEMENTS.*` strings cps-api's own menu-permission seed uses for *display* — those are unrelated to what the `PermissionGuard` actually checks; the controller guards on the plain codes, confirmed by reading `material-permissions.ts` directly). `page.tsx` gates viewing; `MaterialPcClient` hides Add/Edit and Disable/Enable controls per `MATERIAL_CREATE`/`UPDATE` and `MATERIAL_DELETE` respectively, mirroring the backend's own per-route permission split — but note the backend re-checks on every call regardless, this is UX only, not the security boundary.

### Material Receiving: shows the material's real photo + "ดูรายละเอียด" button contrast fix
- User asked to also show the material's image, then separately flagged that the "ดูรายละเอียด" button color blended into the card.
- **Image**: `MaterialsReceivingService#toListResponse`'s `material` mapping was missing `imagePath` even though the entity's `leftJoinAndSelect('receiving.material', 'material')` already pulled it — one-line backend addition, no migration needed (reads existing `master.materials.image_path`). Frontend `MaterialReceiving.material` type gained `imagePath: string | null`, and a new `ReceivingMaterialPhoto` component (in `materials-receiving-table.tsx`, exported and reused by the details dialog too) mirrors Materials PC's `MaterialEditorialPhoto` exactly — same `object-contain` + `ImageOff`-fallback treatment, minus the click-to-preview lightbox (no image preview dialog exists for this read-only reference photo, and it isn't this resource's own upload to manage). Used in all three views: the card's photo tile, the list row's icon box, and a new small thumbnail in the table view's material cell — plus the details dialog header.
- **Button contrast**: the card's "ดูรายละเอียด" button was `variant="outline"` (transparent background, border only) — fine on Materials PC's card where it's paired with a solid primary "แก้ไข" button giving visual contrast, but on this page most rows are `confirmed`/`cancelled` and show it as the **only** button, where a transparent-background button read as barely-there against the card's own `bg-surface`. Changed to `variant="secondary"` (filled `bg-surface-2`) so it's visibly a button standing alone, without competing with "ยืนยัน"'s primary color on the rows where both show.
- **Rule for future single-action card buttons**: `variant="outline"` reads fine paired next to a `variant="primary"` button (the pair provides contrast to each other), but check any card layout where a button can appear **alone** — an outline/transparent button with nothing beside it to contrast against needs `variant="secondary"` or stronger, or it disappears into the card background.
- Verified live (real material photos via the existing `/materials/images` uploads, confirmed via `curl` that `imagePath` populates correctly) using a disposable temporary SUPER_ADMIN account (deleted afterward): card/list/table views all show real photos with the existing `/uploads/*` rewrite, "ดูรายละเอียด" now has a visibly filled background distinct from the card. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors on both repos), `pnpm test` (148/148), cps-api's own `jest` suite (materials-receiving spec 26/26) all pass.

### Material Receiving card view — rebuilt to match Materials PC's Editorial card concept exactly
- Follow-up to the clarity pass below: user asked to make the card "same concept as materials" (Materials PC's `/materials/pc`). The card already existed but had drifted from that pattern (no photo-style tile, badge/actions inline in a text header instead of overlaid, stat hero with a plain icon+text aside instead of a badge, a plain 2-row mini-table instead of a real bordered Data Sheet).
- Rebuilt `MaterialsReceivingCard` to mirror `MaterialEditorialCard` (`material-pc-table.tsx`) structurally, in the same order: **photo tile** (this resource has no material image, so a `Boxes` icon fills the `aspect-[4/3]` `bg-surface-2` tile instead — same slot, same treatment) with the status badge + `RowActionsMenu` overlaid top-right → **identity block** (code eyebrow = Internal Lot in mono, `h2` = material name, subtitle = material code) → **stat hero** (จำนวนรับเข้า as the big 32px number, a neutral `Badge` for the box count beside it, matching Materials PC's stock-hero-plus-health-badge shape) → **Data Sheet** (a real bordered `<table>` with a local `DataSheetRow`, byte-for-byte the same markup as Materials PC's own `DataSheetRow`) listing Internal Lot, Supplier Lot, and receive date → **action row** (outline "ดูรายละเอียด" + primary "ยืนยัน" when applicable), same two-button color hierarchy as Materials PC.
- Local `DataSheetRow` duplicates Materials PC's component rather than importing it — consistent with this project's established "hand-write each resource's own card, don't extract one generic component" rule (see AGENTS.md § Materials PC's own note on this).
- Verified live (real data, card view) using a disposable temporary SUPER_ADMIN account (deleted afterward): tile/badge/identity/stat/Data Sheet/action row all render in the same order and visual weight as a Materials PC card. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `pnpm test` (148/148) all pass.

### Material Receiving list page clarity pass — labeled every value, paired the two lot numbers
- User reported `/materials/materials-receiving` was "still hard to read, don't know which data is which." Root causes found by inspecting the live page: (1) the table view's headers mixed English (`Internal Lot`, `Supplier Lot`) with Thai (`วัสดุ`, `จำนวนรับเข้า`) with no visual pairing between the two lot numbers even though they're easy to confuse (one is our own tracking number, one is the vendor's production batch); (2) the card/list views' primary Internal Lot code had **no label at all** (just a big bare mono string) while the Supplier Lot right below it *did* have a label — inconsistent, so a first-time viewer had to infer by elimination which was which; (3) real leftover data from this session's own earlier format-testing showed **two different Internal Lot formats in the same column** (`CCI-26J07-XXX` next to `FRM-001-26J07-XXX`, the latter from before the material-code-prefix revert documented above) — a live example of "which data is which" confusion, flagged to the user rather than silently hard-deleted (it's now real confirmed inventory data with a stock-balance effect; cps-api has no hard-delete for confirmed/cancelled receivings, only cancel, which the user would need to decide to do).
- **Every view now labels the Internal Lot explicitly** ("Lot ภายใน (Internal Lot)" / "Lot ภายใน" depending on space) instead of relying on a column header or bare positioning, matching how Supplier Lot ("Lot ผู้ผลิต (Supplier Lot)") was already labeled — table, card, and list views all changed together so the pairing can't drift between them again.
- **Table view restructured**: 8 columns → 6. `Internal Lot` + `Supplier Lot` merged into one "หมายเลขล็อต" (lot numbers) cell with both stacked under their own mini-labels (so correlating them no longer requires reading two separate columns across the row); `จำนวนรับเข้า` + `จำนวนกล่อง` merged into one "จำนวน" cell (quantity as the primary number, box count as a muted subtext) — fewer columns to scan, related numbers read together.
- **Default view switched from `table` to `card`** (`useViewMode("materials-receiving", "card")`) — every field on the card is already explicitly labeled in Thai, which reads faster for a first-time/warehouse-floor user than a dense table; same reasoning already established for Materials PC's own card default.
- **Rule for future "which data is which" complaints on a data-dense page**: check first whether two adjacent-looking values are conceptually different but visually equivalent (same font, same weight, no label) — that's usually the actual complaint, not "the whole page needs a redesign." A small inline label pairs the value with its meaning permanently, regardless of which view/column layout the user is looking at, so fix it in every view at once rather than just the one currently on screen.
- Verified live (real data, including the two-lot-format rows that motivated the fix) using a disposable temporary SUPER_ADMIN account (deleted afterward): card view confirmed every field labeled, table view confirmed the merged lot/quantity cells read correctly for both format styles. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `pnpm test` (148/148) all pass.

### Material Receiving dialog redesign — "receiving ticket" + box tiles
- User asked to redesign the `รับเข้าวัตถุดิบ` create dialog. The original version was a single-column stack (fields, then a plain 2-cell lot-preview box, then a data table for box preview) — functionally complete but visually generic. Redesigned around the subject itself: a warehouse receiving document literally becomes a printed lot label stuck on a physical box, so the live preview leans into that.
- **`LotTicket`** (in `materials-receiving-form-dialog.tsx`): the Internal Lot / Supplier Lot preview is now one card styled like a physical warehouse tag — dashed border, two small circular "punch holes" cut into the left edge (implemented as `bg-surface` circles sitting on a `bg-surface-2` card, since `bg-surface` is the dialog's own background per `ui/dialog.tsx` — no new colors), a dashed perforation divider between the two lot numbers, and both codes set in uppercase tracked `font-mono` (Geist Mono, already in the stack). Every color/spacing token used is an existing app token; nothing new was introduced to the design system for this.
- **Box preview changed from a data table to a tile grid**: each box is a small tile (box number in mono, quantity as the hero number, "เต็ม"/"เศษ" tag) using `bg-primary-soft` for full boxes and a dashed `border-warning` + `bg-warning-soft` treatment for the remainder box specifically — so the one box that doesn't match the others (the CEIL remainder) is visually called out at a glance, not just readable in a qty column. This is the same information the old table had, just laid out to answer "how many boxes, and which one is the odd one out" faster.
- **Layout is two columns on desktop** (`md:grid-cols-2`, form left / live ticket+boxes right, right column visually distinguished with `bg-surface-2/40`), single column stacked on mobile (form → ticket → boxes → footer) — the live preview is meant to update as the user types, so keeping it visible beside the form (not below a fold) mattered more here than in a typical single-column form. Dialog size bumped from `size="lg"` to `size="xl"` to fit both columns comfortably; `fullScreenOnMobile` unchanged.
- **Nothing about the underlying logic changed** — `buildPackagePreview`, `buildLotDatePart`, the CCI-prefix decision, the create+confirm submit flow, and all validation are byte-identical to before this pass; only the JSX/markup changed. `PackagePreviewRow` gained an `isRemainder` flag (derived, not new business logic) purely to drive the tile's visual treatment.
- Verified live in the browser (desktop 900px and mobile 375px) using a disposable temporary SUPER_ADMIN account (created via `create-super-admin.ts`, deleted afterward): selected a real material, typed a quantity that produces a remainder (133 ÷ 25 → 6 boxes, last one 8 units), and confirmed the ticket updates live, the remainder tile is visually distinct, the total-matches-checkmark still appears, and the two-column layout collapses correctly to a single scrollable column on mobile with the footer still reachable. Did not submit this test run (no need to create more real data — the create/confirm logic itself was already verified end-to-end in the previous pass). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `pnpm test` (148/148) all pass.

### Material Receiving — `/materials/materials-receiving`, added 2026-09-07
- User asked for a Warehouse-Management-style "Material Receiving" page with a very specific spec: pick a material, auto-generate an Internal Lot and Supplier Lot, compute a box/package breakdown by `CEIL(receiveQty / packingQty)`, generate one QR per box, and support IN_STOCK/PARTIAL/ISSUED box tracking.
