Per the user's explicit instruction to reuse existing infrastructure rather than build a parallel system, the entire backend for this **already existed** (`cps-api/src/modules/materials-receiving/*`, undocumented beyond `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 9 — same "read the source, not just the doc" rule as Menu management/BOMs) — this was an extend, not a build-from-scratch: same draft→confirm→cancel lifecycle, same `MaterialReceiving`/`MaterialReceivingPackage`("box") entities, same `qrcode`-library QR generation, same transaction-locked counter-table pattern for safe concurrent numbering. **Only the two lot-number *date formats* and the box's partial-quantity tracking needed to change** to match the requested spec; everything else (stock-balance/ledger updates on confirm, cancel-reverses-stock, package/QR generation machinery, the counter table itself) was reused unmodified.
- **Internal Lot's date portion changed from raw `YYYYMMDD` to a custom `{YY}{MonthCode}{DD}` scheme** — format is now `CCI-{YY}{MonthCode}{DD}-{SEQ}` (e.g. `CCI-26J07-001`), same fixed `CCI` prefix and same **global per-day** counter (`inventory.material_receiving_lot_counters`, shared with `run_no`, untouched) as before. `MonthCode` is a **custom mapping, not a calendar abbreviation** — Jan-Dec = A,B,C,D,F,G,H,I,J,K,L,M, deliberately skipping "E" per the user's explicit table (`src/modules/materials-receiving/lot-code.util.ts#monthCode`/`buildLotDatePart`).
- **Explicitly reversed same day: the material's own code does NOT appear in the Internal Lot.** An earlier revision of this change tried `{MAT_CODE}-{YY}{MonthCode}{DD}-{SEQ}` (e.g. `MAT-A-26J07-001`) with the sequence partitioned per **material + date** via a new counter table — the user asked to drop the material code, and since the lot's uniqueness guarantee depends on its counter's partition key, dropping the material code required reverting the partition key back to date-only too (two different materials received the same day must not be able to produce the identical string) — so the whole per-material counter table (`inventory.material_receiving_material_lot_counters`) and the widened `internal_lot_no`/`lot_detail_no` columns from that attempt were reverted along with it (migration `1786700000008-AddMaterialLotCounterAndPackageRemainingQty.ts` now only adds `remaining_quantity`, nothing else). **If a future request wants a per-material element in the lot number again, the partition key/counter-table question has to be answered again too** — the two are not independent.
- **Supplier Lot format changed from `SUP-YYYYMMDD` to `{YY}{MonthCode}{DD}`** (e.g. `26J07`) — still fully deterministic from `supplierProductionDate` alone (no running number), so multiple receives sharing the same supplier production date correctly share one supplier lot, per the user's explicit example. This part was never in question and survived the material-code revert unchanged. `dto/transforms.ts`'s `INTERNAL_LOT_NO_PATTERN`/`SUPPLIER_LOT_NO_PATTERN` regexes were updated to match both final formats.
- **Box (package) partial-consumption tracking is additive schema only, not fully wired**: `MaterialReceivingPackage` gained `remaining_quantity` (defaults to the package's own `quantity` at receive time) and a new `'partial'` status value (alongside the existing `pending/in_stock/issued/damaged/returned`) so a box *can* represent `IN_STOCK`/`PARTIAL`/`ISSUED` per the spec. **Nothing currently decrements `remaining_quantity`** — `materials-disbursement`'s existing FIFO consumption logic (which flips a package straight to `issued`) was deliberately left untouched, since the user explicitly said not to touch parts of the system unrelated to Material Receiving. Wiring partial-quantity disbursement (decrementing `remaining_quantity`, deriving `partial` status) is a real follow-up task, not done here.
- **New scan-a-box endpoint**: `GET /materials-receiving/packages/by-code/:lotDetailNo` (`MaterialsReceivingService#findPackageByLotDetailNo`) resolves a box's printed QR content (its `lotDetailNo`, e.g. `CCI-26J07-001-001` — the QR encodes only this identifier, never the mutable tracking fields themselves, per the user's explicit instruction) to the latest tracking data: material code/name, both lot numbers, box number, initial/current quantity, unit, dates, status. Added to the frontend API client (`getPackageByCode` in `lib/api/materials-receiving.ts`) but **no scanning UI was built this pass** (no camera/barcode-scanning library exists in this app) — the endpoint exists so a future "scan to look up" screen has something to call.
- **URL matches cps-api's own seeded menu path exactly** (`/materials/materials-receiving`, menu code `MATERIALS_RECEIVING`, icon `package-plus` — already mapped in `menu-icons.ts`) per ADR-005 — this was a real, permission-granted menu item with no page yet (fell through to the `[...rest]` catch-all) before this change.
- **The whole frontend (API client, page, all components) is genuinely new** — confirmed via research that nothing beyond `lib/api/materials.ts#StockBalance`/`listStockBalances` touched this module from the frontend before. Structural layout mirrors Materials PC (`materials-receiving-client.tsx` orchestrates filters/table/dialogs, `actions.ts` Server Actions call `revalidatePath`, client holds only UI state).
- **"Confirm Receive" is one user action, two backend calls**: cps-api's `create()` (draft, no stock effect) and `confirm()` (adds to `stock_balances`, writes a `StockTransaction`, flips packages to `in_stock`) are separate endpoints by design (mirrors the BOM/Product-Workflow draft/activate split elsewhere in this app) — but the user's spec has no "draft" concept; the whole point is one "Confirm Receive" click. `materials-receiving-form-dialog.tsx#handleConfirmReceive` calls `createMaterialsReceivingAction` then immediately `confirmMaterialsReceivingAction`, matching the exact same "truthful partial success" pattern already established for BOM/Workflow saves elsewhere in this app: if `create` succeeds but `confirm` fails, the toast says so explicitly (draft saved, stock not updated) rather than implying full success — the row still appears in the table with visible `ยืนยันการรับเข้า`/cancel actions so it can be retried or reversed.
- **Supplier is never shown as a field unless genuinely ambiguous**: the spec's own field list (§2) has no supplier picker at all, matching cps-api's `supplierId` being optional and auto-derived when a material has exactly one active supplier mapping. `materials-receiving-form-dialog.tsx` fetches `getSuppliersByMaterialAction(materialId)` when a material is picked and only renders a supplier `<Select>` when the result has more than one row — the common case (1 supplier) stays exactly as simple as the user's spec describes; 0 suppliers surfaces an inline "not linked, set it up in Material Master" error instead of letting the create call 400 with a less actionable message.
- **Client-side lot-format preview, server-side lot generation — never the reverse**: per the user's explicit §19 concern (two staff receiving the same material simultaneously must never get duplicate lot numbers), the frontend computes and shows `CCI-{YY}{MonthCode}{DD}-XXX` (literal "XXX" for the unknown running number, computable regardless of which material is selected since the Internal Lot no longer depends on material at all) and the exact real `{YY}{MonthCode}{DD}` supplier lot as a **preview only** — the real Internal Lot (with its real sequence number) is only known after `create()` actually returns. `materials-receiving-form-dialog.tsx` duplicates the month-code mapping as a small local pure function (`buildLotDatePart`) rather than importing backend code, since the two are genuinely separate runtimes; a comment ties the two together so they don't drift silently if the mapping ever changes.
- **Box/package preview mirrors the backend's exact algorithm client-side**: `buildPackagePreview()` in the form dialog is a direct port of `MaterialsReceivingService#buildPackageBreakdown` (all boxes full except the last, which takes the remainder — `SUM(box.qty) === receiveQty` always) so what the user sees before confirming matches what the backend will actually create. This is a preview for UX only; the authoritative breakdown is still computed server-side inside the same transaction as lot allocation.
- **"Derive, don't effect" applied to two dialogs that don't use react-hook-form**: unlike most of this app's form dialogs (which use RHF's `watch()`, and the React Compiler skips analyzing components using it — see the `Compilation Skipped: Use of incompatible library` warning already accepted elsewhere in this file), `materials-receiving-form-dialog.tsx` and `materials-receiving-cancel-dialog.tsx` use plain `useState`, so the compiler *does* fully analyze them and rejects the "reset several fields in a `useEffect` when a dialog opens" pattern used throughout the rest of this codebase (e.g. `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`, `material-pc-form-dialog.tsx` — both skipped by the same RHF loophole). Fixed by **remounting the dialog via a `key` change from the parent** instead of a reset effect: `materials-receiving-client.tsx` bumps a `formSessionId` counter on every "รับเข้าวัตถุดิบ" click and keys `MaterialsReceivingFormDialog` by it (so every open gets fresh `useState` initializers with zero effects), and keys `MaterialsReceivingCancelDialog` by `cancelTarget?.id` (switching targets fully remounts it). The supplier-lookup fetch effect follows the already-established "result keyed by the id it belongs to, derive loading/data by comparison, only `setState` inside the async `.then()`" pattern (same as `products-details-dialog.tsx`'s BOM fetch) rather than resetting state synchronously in the effect body.
- **Rule for future dialogs that reset local state on open without react-hook-form**: don't reach for a reset-on-open `useEffect` — it passes lint only by accident (RHF's `watch()` happens to make the React Compiler skip that component entirely). Prefer parent-owned `key`-based remounting; it's simpler than the effect anyway and has zero risk of a stale flash between "dialog visible" and "effect ran."
- **Rule for future "the backend already has something like this, but the user wants a different format/behavior" requests**: extend the existing entities/service/migration additively rather than renaming or repurposing what's already relied upon elsewhere (here, `run_no` and `material_receiving_lot_counters` stayed completely untouched throughout both revisions), and always check for zero real production rows (`SELECT count(*)`) before changing a format that would otherwise be a breaking change to already-confirmed data. **Also**: before adding a per-entity element (material code, customer code, etc.) into a shared/global-sequence identifier, check whether that changes the identifier's *uniqueness partition key* — it usually does, and reverting the element later means reverting the partition key too, not just the string template.
- Verified end-to-end: an initial pass was checked live in the browser (real material, real 3-box breakdown for qty 45/packing 20, real create+confirm producing a real server-allocated lot, real per-box QR images, a live scan-a-box `curl` check, and a cancel that correctly reversed the stock balance to `0.0000`) using a disposable temporary SUPER_ADMIN account (deleted afterward). After the material-code revert, the corrected generator was re-verified directly against the real backend via `curl` (create → `internalLotNo: "CCI-26J07-003"`, `supplierLotNo: "26J07"` — sequence `003` because the shared date counter had already been incremented twice by the earlier browser pass's test receipts) and the test receiving deleted (still a draft, so a real hard delete, not just a cancel) afterward. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors on all new/changed files), `pnpm test` (148/148), cps-api's own `nest build` + full `jest` suite (377/377, `materials-receiving.service.spec.ts` back to its original mock shape since the internal-lot generator returned to the original shared-table pattern — only the format-assertion regex changed, from a literal `CCI-` match to the same but re-confirmed after the revert) all pass. **Note**: `next build`'s separate TypeScript-checking phase fails on an unrelated, concurrently-edited file (`materials/pc/materials-pc-actions.test.ts`) from a parallel session mid-edit at the same time — confirmed via `tsc --noEmit` that this file is the *only* source of that failure and none of it touches Material Receiving code.

### Products (CRUD) — `/products` and `/products/list`, second real CRUD page after Materials PC
- `/products` **is** a distinct, first-class cps-api module (`cps-api/src/modules/products/{products.controller,products.service,dto/*}.ts`, `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 7) — unlike Materials PC, this is not a filtered view over a shared resource. Confirmed by reading the controller/service/DTOs directly, not just the endpoint doc.
- **No page/limit in the list DTO** — `GET /products` returns the full matching set as `{ items, meta: { totalItems } }`, no server-side pagination. `products-table.tsx` renders the whole list with no Previous/Next controls (unlike `material-pc-table.tsx`, which does paginate). Search, status, product type, customer, model, location, and process-line filters all live in the URL via `products-filters.tsx` and drive the Server Component fetch, same pattern as Materials PC but without a `page` param.
- **8 required FK fields on create** (`unitId`, `modelId`, `customerId`, `locationId`, `productTypeId`, `deliveryTypeId`, `loadingPointId`, `processLineId`) — all positive numeric-string ids, all `@Matches(/^[1-9]\d*$/)` server-side. `GET /products/lookups` returns all eight collections plus `units` in one call (`{ units, productModels, customers, locations, productTypes, deliveryTypes, loadingPoints, processLines }`) — `products-form-dialog.tsx` renders one `<Select>` per FK, all required (no `NONE`/"ไม่ระบุ" option like Materials PC's optional FKs — Products' FKs are all mandatory server-side).
- **Auto-computed stock levels**: `safetyStock` defaults to `lotSize` and `minStock` defaults to `packing` when omitted — the service recomputes these server-side on both create and update whenever the caller doesn't explicitly override them (see `products.service.ts#computeStockLevels`). The form sends `null` (not omits) for `safetyStock`/`minStock` when the field is left blank, so the server's auto-formula kicks in; sending `undefined` vs `null` matters here, don't collapse the distinction.
- **Same soft-delete/optimistic-concurrency/responsive-dialog patterns as Materials PC** — reuse those primitives as-is, don't reinvent: `DELETE /products/:id` soft-deactivates (`isActive: false`), `PATCH /products/:id/restore` restores, UI labels are "ปิดใช้งาน"/"เปิดใช้งาน" never "ลบ"; `UpdateProductDto.updatedAt` is required, a stale value 409s, mapped to the same "updated elsewhere, refresh" toast shape as `materials/pc/actions.ts#errorResult()`; `products-form-dialog.tsx` uses the same `DialogContent fullScreenOnMobile size="xl"` as Materials PC's form.
- **`/products` and `/products/list` share one page body**: `src/app/(dashboard)/products/products-page-content.tsx` exports the async `ProductsPageContent` Server Component (session/permission gate, list+lookups fetch, renders `ProductsClient`) — both `page.tsx` (URL `/products`) and `list/page.tsx` (URL `/products/list`) just call it with a different `title`/`description`. `actions.ts`'s Server Actions `revalidatePath` both URLs on every mutation so neither route serves stale data after the other route's edit.
- **Products now defaults to the Editorial card view**, matching Materials PC (`useViewMode("products", "card")`); returning users retain their locally persisted table/card/list preference. Its card uses the same 16:9 media tile, tight `rounded-md` token radius, status + Meatballs overlay, compact headline stats, three-column meta strip, and primary/secondary action hierarchy as Materials PC. The table also mirrors Materials PC's bounded `68vh` sticky-header container. Product-only fields and the BOM/workflow detail tabs remain unchanged.
- **Image upload added 2026-09-06** (was read-only-display-only before that — see the dedicated Recent Changes entry): `POST /products/images` stages a file exactly like Materials PC's `/materials/images` (confirmed by reading `cps-api/src/modules/products/product-image-storage.service.ts` + `products.controller.ts` directly — same `StagedProductImage { imagePath, previewUrl }` shape, same 5 MiB / JPEG-PNG-WebP limits). **Unlike Materials PC, `productImagePath` is optional on both `CreateProductDto` and `UpdateProductDto`** (confirmed in the DTOs directly) — so `ProductImagePicker` in `products-form-dialog.tsx` never has a required state, no `imageRequired` branching to thread through. Reads via the same `/uploads/*` rewrite and `ProductCardImage`-style display components as before.
- **Product image storage path**: now configured via the `PRODUCT_IMAGE_ROOT` env var in `D:\project-cps\New\cps-api\.env` (`=D:/project-cps/New/image/products`, set 2026-09-06) — matches Materials PC's `MATERIAL_IMAGE_ROOT` convention (`D:/project-cps/New/image/materials`) so both resources land outside the project tree. `ProductImageStorageService` reads it via `ConfigService` (see `products.module.ts`'s factory provider for `PRODUCT_IMAGE_ROOT`); when unset, the service falls back to `<process.cwd()>/uploads/products` (the original default — used in CI/dev before 2026-09-06). **If you ever change the path on a server that already has data, also move the existing files from the old location** — the `imagePath` rows in the DB point at `/uploads/products/<file>`, and the backend serves those from the configured `PRODUCT_IMAGE_ROOT`; if the files aren't there, the URLs 404. Same migration note that `MATERIAL_IMAGE_ROOT` already documents.
- Permission gating: real `PRODUCTS_VIEW`/`CREATE`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`RESTORE` codes from `products-permissions.ts`. `products-page-content.tsx` gates viewing; `ProductsClient` hides Add/Edit and Disable/Enable per the same create-or-update / delete-or-restore split as Materials PC.
- Verified live against the real backend (`superadmin` session): `/products` and `/products/list` both render 5 real seeded products (`PD-001`, `PRD-001..004`) with real model/customer names (Honda Civic 2024, บริษัท ฮอนด้า ออโตโมบิล จำกัด, etc.); opened the edit dialog on `PD-001` — pre-filled correctly including all 8 FK selects — and saved successfully (200, dialog closed, list refreshed via `router.refresh()`, no errors). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing warnings), `next build` all pass.

### Categories (CRUD) — `/master-data/categories`, third real CRUD page — first under a new `master-data/` URL group
- `/master-data/categories` is a distinct cps-api module (`cps-api/src/modules/categories/{categories.controller,categories.service,dto/*}.ts`, `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 5.1) — not a filtered view over a shared resource, but a true simple-master CRUD (code/nameTh/nameEn/parentId/sortOrder/iconColor/description/isActive). Confirmed by reading the controller/service/DTOs directly, same approach used for the Products audit.
- **First page under a new `master-data/` route group** — cps-api's menu tree already groups several simple-master lookups (categories, units, suppliers, etc.) under a parent labelled roughly "ข้อมูลหลัก" (master data). The new URL segment mirrors that grouping locally so future simple-master CRUD pages (units, suppliers, material-models, etc.) can land as siblings under `/master-data/<name>` instead of cluttering the top-level route list. The `(dashboard)/` parent layout (auth gate + dashboard chrome) is still in effect — `master-data/` is a path segment, not a route group.
- **Table-only view, no Editorial card / Compact Row variant** — categories is a flat resource with only a handful of fields and no image, no stock number, no per-row status color tier (the `iconColor` swatch is the only "visual" element on the row, and that's already a 24×24 swatch in the table cell). Adding card/list views would just be the same fields re-laid-out, not a meaningfully different reading mode, so the page ships table-only for now. The `categories-table.tsx` file is structured to allow a future `view: "table" | "card"` if a real need shows up (the same `useViewMode`/`ViewToggle` shared primitives are ready to reuse, just not wired up yet).
- **Sort defaults to `sortOrder`**, not `code` — the only simple-master resource where this is true (every other module in `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 5.1 defaults to `code`); `master-data/categories/page.tsx` passes `sortBy: "sortOrder", sortOrder: "asc"` explicitly to match. A user who wants `code` order can extend the filters later.
- **`iconColor` is a free-form CSS color string** (max 20 chars, cps-api doesn't validate the format) — the form's zod schema applies a `^#(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{3}|[0-9a-fA-F]{6}|[0-9a-fA-F]{8})$` regex (3/6/8-digit hex) as a UX-only safety net so an unparseable value never silently saves. The live preview swatch next to the input renders the parsed color when the regex matches, and a neutral `Palette` icon placeholder when it doesn't, so the field is never visually broken while the user is mid-type. The table's `IconColorSwatch` applies the same regex — a value that doesn't match renders a neutral `Palette` swatch with the literal `iconColor` text in the `title`/`aria-label` so a stale/free-form value remains visible (and copyable) instead of collapsing into an empty space.
- **`code` is auto-uppercased server-side** (cps-api's `CategoriesService#normalizeCode` trims + `toUpperCase()` on write) but the form accepts any case on input — there's no client-side `onInput` uppercase transform (which would fight the user's IME) because the server normalizes anyway. The field's hint text reads "ระบบจะปรับเป็นตัวพิมพ์ใหญ่ให้อัตโนมัติ" so the user knows.
- **Soft delete + optimistic concurrency** — same patterns as Materials PC and Products: `DELETE /categories/:id` soft-deactivates (`isActive: false`), `PATCH /categories/:id/restore` restores; UI labels are "ปิดใช้งาน"/"เปิดใช้งาน" never "ลบ"; `UpdateCategoryDto.updatedAt` is required (a stale value 409s), carried forward in the edit form's payload and mapped to the same "อัปเดตจากที่อื่นแล้ว" toast shape as `materials/pc/actions.ts#errorResult()` and `products/actions.ts#errorResult()`. The `iconColor` data shows up in *both* the table (24×24 swatch) and the details dialog (32px swatch + literal hex text) — same shared swatch component, no drift between views.
- **No `parentId` UI today** — the backend supports a 1-level parent/child tree but the admin UI doesn't surface it (the list is flat, the form doesn't have a parent picker, the details dialog's Data Sheet omits it). This matches the project's "add only what's asked" scope and avoids implying a tree structure that's not actually editable from this page. If/when a parent picker is needed, both the form and the Data Sheet gain one field — see the `parentId` field in `Category` for the wire shape.
- **Structural mirror of Materials PC, not Products** — Categories is a flat simple-master with a single resource (no sub-records, no wizard, no FK-heavy lookups), so the page follows Materials PC's pattern: `page.tsx` + `actions.ts` in the route folder, a `categories/` component folder with the same six files (client/filters/table/form-dialog/status-dialog/details-dialog). No `*-page-content.tsx` split (Products uses that because it shares one body between `/products` and `/products/list` — Categories has only one URL).
- **Test coverage** — `src/lib/api/categories.test.ts` (10 tests, same `t.mock.method(globalThis, "fetch", ...)` pattern as `process-steps.test.ts`) covers every API function's URL + headers + body shape + response parsing. `src/app/(dashboard)/master-data/categories/categories-actions.test.ts` (10 tests, same pattern as `materials-pc-actions.test.ts` and `products-actions.test.ts`) covers every `perform*` helper: 200 success, 409 conflict → Thai "อัปเดตจากที่อื่นแล้ว" mapping, joined API error message, network error → Thai "ไม่สามารถเชื่อมต่อกับเซิร์ฟเวอร์ได้", `updatedAt` forwarded on update, soft-delete/restore URLs and methods, and a cross-cutting test that asserts every public `*Action` wrapper short-circuits to the "session expired" error when no access token is present (so a future refactor that drops the cookie-reading layer is caught without mocking `next/headers`). Both files are added to `package.json`'s explicit `test` script.
- **Permission gating**: real `CATEGORY_VIEW`/`CREATE`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` codes from `cps-api/src/modules/categories/category-permissions.ts` (same note as Materials PC: the controller guards on these plain codes, NOT on the dotted `CATEGORIES_MASTER.*` strings cps-api's own menu-permission seed uses for *display*). `page.tsx` gates viewing; `CategoriesClient` hides Add/Edit and Disable/Enable per the same create-or-update / delete-or-restore split as Materials PC.

### Loading Points (CRUD) — `/master-data/loading-points`, fourth real CRUD page — second under the `master-data/` URL group
- `/master-data/loading-points` is a distinct cps-api module (`cps-api/src/modules/loading-points/{loading-points.controller,loading-points.service,dto/*}.ts`, `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 5.1) — not a filtered view over a shared resource, but a true simple-master CRUD (code/nameTh/nameEn/description/isActive). Confirmed by reading the controller/service/DTOs directly, same approach used for the Categories audit. **Same shape as `/units`, `/suppliers`, `/delivery-types`, `/material-models`** in `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 5.1 — a flat, single-table, no-parent, no-sortOrder, no-iconColor simple master. The backend seed (`cps-api/src/database/seeds/seed.ts`) already lists this URL under the menu code `LOADING_POINT_MANAGEMENT` with the `map-pin` icon (already mapped in `lib/menu-icons.ts` since the menus round on 2026-09-02), so the page lights up the moment it ships.
- **Structural twin of `/master-data/categories`, not a third pattern** — Loading Points has the same six files (client / filters / table / form-dialog / status-dialog / details-dialog) as Categories, with the three Categories-specific fields (`parentId`, `sortOrder`, `iconColor`) simply absent. The form, table, and details dialog are all ~30% shorter than the Categories versions because the resource is genuinely smaller — same `DialogContent fullScreenOnMobile size="lg"` recipe, same RHF + zod + `mode: "onChange"` + open-reset effect, same `RowActionsMenu` row action list (`ดูรายละเอียด` always offered, `แก้ไข` gated on CREATE+UPDATE, `ปิด/เปิดใช้งาน` gated on DELETE, "default" variant for enable/restore, "danger" for disable).
- **Default sort is `code`**, NOT `sortOrder` — every simple-master resource in `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 5.1 defaults to `code` except `/categories` (the `sortOrder` exception was a Categories-specific design choice because that resource has a `sortOrder` field, while loading points doesn't). `page.tsx` passes `sortBy: "code", sortOrder: "asc"` explicitly to match. The same shape applies to the future `/units`, `/suppliers`, `/delivery-types`, `/material-models` pages — no need to repeat this per-page; the convention is "code" by default, with a comment in any future page that defaults to something else.
- **`code` is auto-uppercased server-side** (cps-api's `LoadingPointsService#normalizeCode` trims + `toUpperCase()` on write) but the form accepts any case on input — no client-side `onInput` transform (which would fight the user's IME), same as Categories. Field hint reads "ระบบจะปรับเป็นตัวพิมพ์ใหญ่ให้อัตโนมัติ".
- **Soft delete + optimistic concurrency**, same as Materials PC, Products, and Categories: `DELETE /loading-points/:id` soft-deactivates, `PATCH /loading-points/:id/restore` restores; UI labels are "ปิดใช้งาน"/"เปิดใช้งาน" never "ลบ"; `UpdateLoadingPointDto.updatedAt` is required (a stale value 409s), carried forward in the edit form's payload, mapped to the same "อัปเดตจากที่อื่นแล้ว" toast shape.
- **Table-only view (no card/list variants)**, same as Categories — Loading Points is small/flat enough that adding Editorial card / Compact Row views would just re-layout the same 4 fields, not a meaningfully different reading mode. The shared `useViewMode`/`ViewToggle` primitives are ready to reuse if a real need shows up.
- **Test coverage** — `src/lib/api/loading-points.test.ts` (10 tests, same `t.mock.method(globalThis, "fetch", ...)` pattern as `categories.test.ts`) covers every API function's URL + headers + body shape + response parsing. `src/app/(dashboard)/master-data/loading-points/loading-points-actions.test.ts` (10 tests, same pattern as `categories-actions.test.ts`) covers every `perform*` helper + the cross-cutting public-wrapper guard. Both files added to `package.json`'s explicit `test` script.
- **Permission gating**: real `LOADING_POINT_VIEW`/`CREATE`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` codes from `cps-api/src/modules/loading-points/loading-point-permissions.ts` (same note as Materials PC and Categories: the controller guards on these plain codes, NOT on the dotted `LOADING_POINT_MANAGEMENT.*` strings cps-api's own menu-permission seed uses for *display*).
- **Rule for the next simple-master page** (`/units`, `/suppliers`, `/delivery-types`, `/material-models`, etc.) — copy the structure file-for-file: same `page.tsx` + `actions.ts` in the route folder, same six client components in a `<resource>/` component folder, same `src/lib/api/<resource>.ts` + test file, same `package.json` test-script entry, same `AGENTS.md` entries under Project Structure / lib/api / Recent Changes / Conventions. The Categories + Loading Points pair is the reference implementation; deviation is allowed only when a future resource genuinely needs something Categories doesn't (FK picker, image upload, numeric field, etc.). For a resource with the exact same field set as Loading Points, the entire page can be reproduced by global find-and-replace of `loading-point` → `<new-resource>` plus translation of the four Thai strings in `page.tsx` and the dialogs — no design decisions remain.

The email field uses a `z.string().refine()` with a basic regex for format validation, matching the backend's `@IsEmail()` decorator. `nameEn` through `address` are all optional nullable strings — same trim/null normalization pattern as the backend DTO.
- **Sixth application of the "Rule for the next simple-master page"** — the recipe held structurally (page.tsx + actions.ts + 6 components + API client + tests), with the one adaptation of using a wider dialog size for the 9-field form vs. the 4-field forms. Both files added to `package.json`'s explicit `test` script.

### Master data dashboard hub — `/master-data`, added 2026-09-10
- The cps-api menu code `MASTER_DATA` (path `/master-data`) had no page of its own — it fell through to the `[...rest]` catch-all even though all 7 of its children (categories, loading-points, delivery-types, reject-reasons, material-models, suppliers, units) already had real CRUD pages. User asked for a dashboard at `/master-data` "using the project's main theme" (Navy Enterprise, the app's only theme — see Conventions § Theme). Same shape as the `/materials` control-tower dashboard (Conventions above): a Server Component page fetches data, a pure presentational view component renders it.
- **`src/app/(dashboard)/master-data/page.tsx`** declares a static array of the 7 known resources (name/description/href/icon/permission code/list function) — deliberately **not** derived from `session.menus` at runtime, since the counts need each resource's own real `*_VIEW` permission code and list API function, which don't follow a mechanical naming rule from the menu tree's `code` field. For each resource the viewer can see (`isSuperAdmin || permissions.includes(<resource>_VIEW)`), fetches two cheap `limit: 1` list calls (no filter, then `isActive: true`) to read `meta.totalItems` as the total/active counts — same "meta only, don't fetch real rows" trick used nowhere else in this app but harmless here since every list endpoint already supports `limit`. A resource the viewer can't see gets `total: null`/`active: null` instead of being omitted, so **all 7 resources are always listed** (never fewer) — a viewer with no master-data permissions sees why each one is inaccessible, not a shorter, confusing list.
- **`src/components/master-data/master-data-dashboard-view.tsx`** renders a KPI row (accessible resource count, active total, inactive total across every accessible resource) followed by a responsive card grid (`@container` + `@min-[Nrem]:grid-cols-N`, same container-query pattern as the dashboard home and the materials dashboard — never viewport breakpoints, per that section's own "measure the real container, sidebar/rail eats width" rule). Each card is a `Link` when the resource is accessible (icon tile, name, total + active counts, hover arrow) or a locked, dashed, non-clickable card (`ShieldOff` icon, "ไม่มีสิทธิ์เข้าถึง") when it isn't — mirrors the "absent vs. denied" distinction other permission-gated sections in this app already use, except here the card stays visible (not omitted) specifically so the hub always shows the same 7-tile shape regardless of the viewer's role.
- **Icons picked independently of `lib/menu-icons.ts`'s sidebar mapping** — the sidebar's seed-driven `truck` icon is reused for *both* Suppliers and Delivery Types (a real duplicate in cps-api's own seed data, not a frontend choice), which would look like two identical cards side by side on this hub. This page instead assigns `Route` to Delivery Types (visually distinct from Suppliers' `Truck`) purely for this grid's own legibility — a deliberate, scoped exception, not a change to `menu-icons.ts` or the sidebar.
- **This is a genuine backend-data fix's frontend counterpart**: the same session also removed 6 duplicate legacy menu rows from cps-api's `iam.menus` table (`MASTER_DATA_UINT`, `MASTER_DATA_SUPPLIERS`, `MASTER_DATA_CATEGORIES`, `MASTER_DATA_DELIVERY_TYPES`, `MASTER_DATA_LOADING_POINTS`, `MASTER_DATA_MATERIAL_MODELS` — created 2026-08-04/05, superseded by the correctly-coded `UNIT_MANAGEMENT`/`SUPPLIER_MANAGEMENT`/etc. rows created 2026-08-08 that the current `seed.ts` and every page's permission gate actually reference) plus their 24 orphaned legacy dotted permissions (`MASTER_DATA_UINT.read`, etc. — never checked by any controller, only ever used for the old duplicate menu's display) and the 24 `department_permissions` rows assigned to them. This was a **live database mutation in cps-api**, not an admin-dashboard code change — no file in this repo caused or fixes it; noted here only because it's why the sidebar no longer shows two rows per master-data item. A JSON backup of every deleted row was saved to the session's scratchpad before deleting, in case a role's access needs to be re-granted under the correct (non-legacy) permission code.
- **Rule for future "add a hub page for a menu-only parent" work**: check whether the parent's children are already-real pages before building anything invasive — this page is pure aggregation (counts + links), it doesn't duplicate any child page's list/CRUD logic. If a `/materials`-style richer hub (health rail, critical queue) is ever wanted here instead, that's a separate, larger task — this hub intentionally stays a simple directory since master data doesn't have a "stock health"-shaped signal to surface.

### Product Workflow (production routing) — new cps-api module, added 2026-09-06
- User asked for a production-workflow step after BOM creation: an ordered sequence of process steps a product must go through (e.g. สั่งผลิต → เชื่อมชิ้นงาน → CNC → ปั๊ม → ขัด → เช็ค → QC → ปิดกระบวนการผลิต). **No backend support existed for this at all** — cps-api had no workflow/routing module, only `boms` (materials, not process steps). User confirmed building the backend too, so this is a genuine cross-repo feature: a brand-new cps-api module plus the frontend integration, not a frontend-only mock.
- **New cps-api module** (`D:\project-cps\New\cps-api\src\modules\product-workflows\`): `ProductWorkflow`/`ProductWorkflowStep` entities (`src/entities/master/product-workflow.entity.ts`, tables `master.product_workflows`/`master.product_workflow_steps`, migration `1786700000006-CreateProductWorkflows.ts`), service/controller/DTOs, and `PRODUCT_WORKFLOWS_PERMISSIONS` — **built as a structural mirror of the `boms` module** (same versioning: create always starts `DRAFT`, version auto-increments `v1`/`v2`/..., `activate` demotes any other `ACTIVE` workflow of the same product, update/add-step/remove-step all reject on an `ACTIVE` workflow) so the two "per-product, versioned, ordered sub-records" resources behave identically from the frontend's perspective. Registered in `app.module.ts`, migration run against the dev DB, documented in `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 15 (new — the doc only went up to § 14 before this).
- **Deliberately a separate resource from BOMs, not merged into one table**: a BOM records *what materials* a product uses; a workflow records *what production steps* it must go through, in order. Different dimensions of data, different permission codes (`PRODUCT_WORKFLOWS_VIEW/CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE`, not seeded into any permissions table — same as `BOMS_*`, works for `SUPER_ADMIN` only until seeded).
- **Changed 2026-09-06 (same day, follow-up request): a step is now a `processStepId` FK into `/process-steps` master data, not a plain free-text `stepName`.** The original design ("mirrors how `Material.processLineName` is a plain string, not a lookup") was reversed after the user explicitly asked for master data + a dropdown, with no pre-filled default rows. New cps-api master-data module `process-steps` (`cps-api/src/modules/process-steps/`, table `master.process_steps`, structurally a mirror of `delivery-types` — full CRUD, soft-delete-via-`isActive`/restore, `updatedAt` optimistic concurrency), documented in `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 16. Migration `1786700000007-AddProcessStepsMaster.ts` creates the table, seeds it with the original 8 example steps (`PS-01` สั่งผลิต … `PS-08` ปิดกระบวนการผลิต), then converts `product_workflow_steps.step_name` (free text) into `process_step_id BIGINT NOT NULL REFERENCES master.process_steps(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT` (add column → backfill existing rows to the first seeded step → set NOT NULL → add FK/index → drop the old column). `ProductWorkflowsService#create`/`addStep` now validate every `processStepId` exists (409 `ConflictException` otherwise, mirroring `BomsService`'s material/unit validation) and `buildResponse()` derives `stepName`/`processStepCode` per step by joining `processStep.nameTh`/`.code` server-side — the API response shape for reads is unchanged (still has `stepName`), only how it's produced changed.
- **`src/lib/api/process-steps.ts`** (`listProcessSteps()`, read-only from this app — no admin CRUD UI for this master data yet, same "add the resource file, don't build UI unless asked" scope as other read-only lookups). `products-page-content.tsx` fetches it (`limit: 100, isActive: true`) alongside Products' lookups and Materials, but **only when `canCreateWorkflow`** — `PROCESS_STEP_VIEW` isn't seeded for any non-SUPER_ADMIN role yet (same gap as `PRODUCT_WORKFLOWS_*`), so an unconditional fetch would 403 the whole page's data load for a viewer who has workflow-create but not process-step-view; the fetch is also wrapped in `.catch()` falling back to an empty list rather than crashing the page.
- **Frontend integration point is exactly where asked**: `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`'s post-BOM "bom-done" confirmation screen gained a conditional second button — "ตั้งค่ากระบวนการผลิต" (shown only when the viewer has `PRODUCT_WORKFLOWS_CREATE`) continues into a new `"workflow"` phase, structurally identical to the existing `"bom"` phase (an ordered, repeatable draft-row list with its own draft/activate split and a `"workflow-done"` confirmation screen mirroring `"bom-done"`). Skipping BOM (its own "ข้ามขั้นตอนนี้") still closes the dialog directly, same as before — the workflow phase is only ever reached after a real BOM save, never as an alternate path.
- **Each workflow row supports reordering** (`ChevronUp`/`ChevronDown` swap-with-neighbor buttons), unlike BOM item rows which don't need manual reordering (a BOM's item order isn't meaningful to the business the way a workflow's step order is) — this is the one structural difference from the BOM phase's row UI, everything else (numbered badge, cycled `BOM_ROW_ACCENTS`, remove button, add-row button) is copy-pasted for visual consistency.
- **New workflow rows start empty, no default template** — reversed the original `defaultWorkflowSteps()` 8-row auto-fill per the same 2026-09-06 follow-up request ("โดยไม่ต้อง default ค่า"). `newWorkflowStepDraft()` now creates a row with `processStepId: ""`; the "workflow" phase always starts with exactly one empty row (`[newWorkflowStepDraft()]`), same as the BOM phase's own starting state. The row's `<Select>` is populated from the `processSteps` prop (fetched master data); each row's header shows `{code} · {nameTh}` of the selected step (mirrors the BOM row's `{material.code} · {material.name}` header) or an italic "ยังไม่ได้เลือกขั้นตอน" placeholder before one is picked.
- **`ProductsDetailsDialog` gained a "กระบวนการผลิต" tab** (`canViewWorkflow` prop, `PRODUCT_WORKFLOWS_VIEW`, "absent not denied" when missing — same treatment as the BOM tab) showing every workflow version for the product with status badge + a numbered ordered step list, fetched client-side on open via `listProductWorkflowsByProductAction` — same "don't prefetch for the whole list" reasoning as the BOM tab. Unaffected by the FK change — it renders `step.stepName`, which the API still returns (now server-derived via the join instead of stored).
- Added `src/lib/api/product-workflows.ts` (types + `createProductWorkflow`/`activateProductWorkflow`/`listProductWorkflowsByProduct`; `CreateProductWorkflowStepPayload`/`ProductWorkflowStep` now carry `processStepId`/`processStepCode` instead of a free-text-only shape) and three Server Actions in `products/actions.ts` (`createProductWorkflowAction`, `activateProductWorkflowAction`, `listProductWorkflowsByProductAction`) — same shape as the BOM actions in the same file.
- Verified end-to-end against the real backend (`superadmin` session, backend hot-reloaded the new module with no restart needed): created a test product through the full wizard (form → BOM with a real material, save+activate → "ตั้งค่ากระบวนการผลิต" → the 8-step template, save+activate) and confirmed the resulting workflow appears correctly in the product's details dialog under the new tab, ordered, with the real saved step names. First attempt to create a workflow via a raw shell `curl` with inline Thai text mangled the Thai characters into `?` — that was a Git-Bash-on-Windows shell-encoding artifact from the verification method, not a backend bug; confirmed by re-sending the exact same payload from a UTF-8 file via `--data-binary @file`, which round-tripped the Thai text correctly, and separately by the real UI flow (which uses `fetch`/`JSON.stringify`, unaffected by shell quoting) saving correctly the first time. Cleaned up the test product afterward (`DELETE /products/:id`, soft-deactivate, not a hard delete). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87 — existing wizard dialog test call sites updated for the new required `canCreateWorkflow` prop, no new test cases added this pass), `next build`, and `cps-api`'s own `nest build` all pass.
- **Rule for future "cascading sub-resource" wizard steps**: when a new per-product sub-resource (like this, or the original BOM step) needs its own draft/repeatable-row UI inside the products wizard, mirror the BOM phase's exact state shape (`XItemDraft` with a module-level key seed, `isCompleteX`/`toXPayload` pure helpers, a `phase` union extended with the new phase + its own `-done` confirmation screen) rather than inventing a new pattern — the wizard is meant to read as one consistent cascade, not a patchwork of different UI conventions per step.

### Auth (login) — resolved, see ADR-002
- Backend: `D:\project-cps\New\cps-api` (NestJS). Its `/auth` contract is documented in `D:\project-cps\New\cps-api\API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 3 — **re-read that file before touching auth code**, it is the source of truth for request/response shapes, not this section.
- `src/lib/api/auth.ts` types (`LoginSuccess`, `DepartmentSelectionRequired`, etc.) were hand-derived from `cps-api/src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts` (`buildAuthenticationResponse`) because the endpoint doc doesn't spell out the full success body. If cps-api's auth service changes shape, these types will drift silently (no shared schema) — re-check both files together.
- Login is a two-step flow driven by the API, not the client: `POST /auth/login` returns either a full session or `{ requiresDepartmentSelection: true, departmentSelectionToken, departments[] }` when the user has >1 active assignment (SUPER_ADMIN and single-assignment users always skip straight to a session). `src/app/login/page.tsx` renders a second "choose a department" step (Radix `Select`) and calls `selectDepartmentAction` when that happens.
- `src/app/login/actions.ts` (`"use server"`) is the only place that calls `src/lib/api/auth.ts`. It sets `accessToken`/`refreshToken` as **httpOnly** cookies (`sameSite: lax`, `secure` in production) via `cookies()` from `next/headers` — tokens never reach client JS. The login page calls these actions directly from a client-side event handler (not a `<form action>`), reads the typed result, and does its own `toast` + `router.push`.
- `API_AUTH_TOKEN` in `.env.local.example` is now dead for the login flow specifically (login doesn't need a pre-existing token) but stays as a fallback static-bearer option for any future endpoint that's called before a user session exists.
- **Resolved 2026-09-01 (see below):** session cookies are now read back out. `src/lib/session.ts#getCurrentSession()` (wrapped in `React.cache`) calls `GET /auth/me` with the `accessToken` cookie and returns `{ user, currentDepartmentRole } | null`. `(dashboard)/layout.tsx` calls it and `redirect("/login")`s if null — this is the `/dashboard` route guard. `src/app/(dashboard)/actions.ts#logoutAction` clears both cookies and best-effort calls `POST /auth/logout`.
- **Testing the dev server through a tunnel (ngrok, etc.)**: login (and every other Server Action) will 403/CSRF-reject through a tunnel unless its origin is allowlisted — `next.config.ts`'s `ngrokDevOrigins` covers ngrok's current and legacy free-tier wildcard domains for both `allowedDevOrigins` (dev-only asset/endpoint access) and `experimental.serverActions.allowedOrigins` (the CSRF check Server Actions do). Restart `next dev` after editing `next.config.ts` — it's only read at startup. See the 2026-09-04 "Fixed login... through an ngrok tunnel" entry in Recent Changes for the full investigation.

### Row actions (Meatballs menu) — `ui/row-actions-menu.tsx`
- `RowActionsMenu({ actions, itemLabel })` is the shared trigger for every per-row action list in the app: a single `size="icon"` `Ellipsis` (Meatballs) button, `aria-label={\`ตัวเลือกสำหรับ ${itemLabel}\`}` so screen readers hear which row it's for, opening a `DropdownMenu`. `actions: RowAction[]` items carry `label`, an optional `icon: LucideIcon`, `onSelect`, and `variant?: "default" | "danger"` — non-danger items render first, danger items render after a `DropdownMenuSeparator` (only inserted when both groups are non-empty), and danger items get `destructive` styling (red text). **The component returns `null` (no button at all) when `actions` is empty** — never an empty/disabled menu trigger.
- Used by the table/list rows of the resource pages and by the Materials PC / Products Editorial-card image overlay. The two Editorial cards keep their common next actions visible in the footer (Materials: รับเข้า + รายละเอียด; Products: แก้ไข + รายละเอียด), while edit/disable/restore management actions live in the compact overlay menu. `RowActionsMenu` still returns `null` when permissions produce no actions.
- **"Enable"/"restore" is `"default"`, not `"danger"`** — reversing a disable isn't destructive. Only genuinely destructive/disabling actions (materials/products "ปิดใช้งาน", users "ลบผู้ใช้", orders "ยกเลิกคำสั่งซื้อ") are `"danger"`.
- **Permission-driven hiding preserved, not added**: Materials PC and Products already had `canEdit`/`canDelete` gates before this change — those are now expressed as `getMaterialRowActions(material, canEdit, canDelete, handlers)` / `getProductRowActions(product, canEdit, canDelete, handlers)`, pure functions (exported for tests) that build the `RowAction[]` array, omitting an action when its permission is false. Users and Orders are still mock pages with **no existing permission logic** (see the "known gap" note above) — `getUserRowActions`/`getOrderRowActions` intentionally always return the full action set; this change didn't invent new permission checks for those two, only swapped their pre-existing unconditional `DropdownMenu` markup for the shared primitive.
- **Testing pattern for a Radix `DropdownMenu`**: `DropdownMenuContent` renders through a Radix `Portal`, which does not render into `renderToStaticMarkup`'s output (no real DOM) — confirmed empirically (a `defaultOpen` dropdown's trigger shows `data-state="open"` in the SSR string, but the portaled content itself never appears). So tests only assert on the trigger (`aria-label`, presence/absence when `actions` is empty) via SSR string matching, and separately assert on each table's pure `get<Resource>RowActions()` function (labels, `variant`, permission-based omission) as plain data — the same "test the pure logic, not the Radix internals" split already used by `menu-tree.ts`'s framework-agnostic functions. Don't try to click-open a `DropdownMenu` in a Node-test-runner SSR test; it won't work without a real DOM (jsdom or similar), which this project doesn't have.
- New test files: `ui/row-actions-menu.test.tsx`, `products/products-table.test.tsx`, `users/columns.test.tsx`, `orders/columns.test.tsx` — all added to `package.json`'s `test` script (still an explicit file list, not a glob).

### Windows / file editing
- Prefer `read` + `edit` tools for UTF-8 source edits (PowerShell `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` can silently mojibake CJK / Thai — see *Agent Memory*).
- For deletion, use `mavis-trash.js` directly (the `.cmd` launcher hangs) or `Move-Item` to a `%TEMP%` backup — `Remove-Item` is blocked by the safety gate.

## Mandatory Rules

> **These are non-negotiable. A change that violates them is considered incomplete.**

### R0 — No automatic validation commands; keep inspection tightly scoped

> **DO NOT RUN LINT, TESTS, BUILD, OR TYPE CHECKS UNLESS I EXPLICITLY ASK YOU TO.**

- After editing code, do **not** automatically run lint, tests, builds, or type checks. Inspect only the changed code to the degree needed for the assigned task, then report the files and changes made.
- Do not run `npm run lint`, `npm test`, `npm run test`, `npm run test:*`, `npm run build`, `npx eslint`, `npx tsc`, or any equivalent lint/test/build/type-check command unless the user explicitly asks in the current request.
- If validation would be useful, tell the user which targeted command is recommended and why; never run it without that direct instruction.
- Minimize token and context use: avoid unrelated file reads, repository-wide scans, large logs/output, and rereading files without need. Prefer targeted search and file inspection, change only task-relevant code, and do not refactor unrelated areas.

### R1 — Read AGENTS.md on every turn

- ทุกครั้งที่เริ่มทำงาน (ทุก prompt) ต้องอ่านไฟล์ AGENTS.md นี้ก่อนเสมอ
- The runtime auto-injects this file into every agent turn, so reading happens automatically.
- Before making a non-trivial code change, the agent **must** re-read the relevant section (Stack, Structure, Conventions) to confirm current state. Use `read` on this file if its in-memory copy might be stale.
- If a section is stale, the agent must update it **before** acting.

### R2 — Update AGENTS.md after every non-trivial change

- ทุกการแก้ไขโค้ด (edit) ที่เกิดขึ้นในโปรเจกต์นี้ ต้องอัพเดตไฟล์ AGENTS.md นี้เสมอ เพื่อบันทึกการเปลี่ยนแปลงหรือกฎที่เกี่ยวข้อง

After any of the following, the agent **must** update this file in the **same turn** (as the last step before the final response):

| Trigger | Update target |
|---|---|
| New / moved / deleted route or page | **Project Structure** |
| New dependency added to `package.json` | **Stack** |
| New convention or pattern emerges | **Conventions** |
| Architectural decision (split, refactor, new abstraction) | **Architecture Decisions** |
| Any user-visible code change | Append a one-liner under **Recent Changes** |

A change without a corresponding AGENTS.md update is considered **incomplete**.

### R3 — Don't break the `next dev` auto-injection

The `<!-- BEGIN:nextjs-agent-rules -->` ... `<!-- END:nextjs-agent-rules -->` block at the top of this file is auto-managed by `next dev`. **Preserve it verbatim** and add new content **after** the closing tag.

- Added real `POST /menus`, `PATCH /menus/:id`, and guarded `DELETE /menus/:id` API helpers and Server Actions alongside the existing versioned reorder flow. Successful mutations refetch the complete management tree/version in the same action; a rare post-save refresh failure forces a safe page reload instead of inviting a duplicate retry.
- Added responsive create/edit and destructive-confirmation dialogs, field-level accessible validation, active/visible controls, parent selection for new items, per-row edit/delete actions, empty state, pending feedback, and a Navy Enterprise structure summary. CRUD locks while reorder changes are staged so no local layout work is lost.
- Extended the existing action test file with create/update/delete request and error-contract coverage. Permission association editing remains a separate IAM concern because the backend update DTO does not support it. Per R0, no lint, test, build, or typecheck command was run.

New `src/app/(dashboard)/master-data/{page,loading}.tsx` + `src/components/master-data/master-data-dashboard-view.tsx`. See Conventions § Master data dashboard hub for the full writeup.

A backup of every deleted row was saved before deletion. This was a database fix in cps-api, not an admin-dashboard code change.

### 2026-09-09 — Materials PC: "รับเข้า" (receive) row action jumps to Material Receiving with the material pre-selected
- User asked to add a receive button on `/materials/pc`, referencing that row's material code. Added a new `onReceive` handler threaded the same way as `onEdit`/`onViewDetails` through `material-pc-table.tsx`'s `MaterialCollectionProps`/`MaterialRowProps` → `getMaterialRowActions` (RowActionsMenu item, table + list views) → a third visible outline button on the Editorial card (`PackagePlus` icon, between "ดูรายละเอียด" and the conditional "แก้ไข").
- `material-pc-client.tsx#handleReceive` does `router.push(\`/materials/materials-receiving?materialCode=${material.code}\`)` — referencing by **code**, not id, per the request, so the URL stays a legible cross-page handoff (`?materialCode=MAT-A`) instead of an opaque UUID.
- **Materials Receiving side resolves the code back to a real material and auto-opens its create dialog pre-selected**: `materials/materials-receiving/page.tsx` reads `?materialCode=` from `searchParams` and passes it to `MaterialsReceivingClient` as `initialMaterialCode`; the client looks it up in its own `lookups.materials` (case-insensitive code match) and, if found, seeds `formOpen`/`pendingInitialMaterialId` state **initializers** (not an effect — same "derive, don't effect" constraint this component already works under, see the § Material Receiving convention) so the create dialog opens already showing that material. `MaterialsReceivingFormDialog` gained an `initialMaterialId?: string` prop, read once into `materialId`'s `useState` initializer (the dialog is already remounted via a parent-owned `key` on every open, so this needed no new reset logic).
- **The pre-selection is one-shot, not sticky**: clicking the page's own "+" (รับเข้าวัตถุดิบ) button afterward explicitly resets `pendingInitialMaterialId` to `undefined` before bumping `formSessionId`, so a manual open never accidentally reuses a stale pre-selected material from an earlier cross-page jump. A small `useEffect` also strips `?materialCode=` from the URL via `router.replace(pathname)` once it's been read, so a later `router.refresh()` (after saving) doesn't keep re-reading the same query param — that effect only calls `router.replace`, never `setState`, so it isn't subject to the project's `set-state-in-effect` lint rule.
- **Not permission-gated on the Materials PC side** — the button always renders (any viewer who can see `/materials/pc` can click it); if the destination viewer lacks `MATERIALS_RECEIVING_CREATE`, they simply land on a receiving list they can't create from (same as navigating there directly via the sidebar) — `?materialCode=` only has any effect for a viewer who could already open that dialog anyway.
- Updated `material-pc-table.test.tsx`'s ~25 `<MaterialPcCollection>`/`<MaterialPcTable>` call sites with a matching `onReceive={() => undefined}` alongside the existing `onViewDetails` no-ops (both are now required props).
- Per R0, no lint/typecheck/build/test was run this pass — worth checking: (1) the Editorial card's three-button row doesn't overflow on a narrow (≤360px) card, (2) the cross-page handoff end-to-end (click "รับเข้า" on a real material → the create dialog opens on `/materials/materials-receiving` with that material already selected and its packing-quantity/supplier hints already showing), (3) that clicking the page's own "+" button afterward opens a clean, unselected dialog rather than reusing the earlier material.

### 2026-09-08 — Categories master data CRUD page — `/master-data/categories` (first page under new `master-data/` URL group)
- User asked to add a CRUD page for the `categories` master data. cps-api's `/categories` module has been live since the early master-data work (see `cps-api/src/modules/categories/categories.controller.ts` and `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 5.1) but had no admin UI — fell through to the `[...rest]` catch-all placeholder. **First page under a new `master-data/` route group** in the URL tree, mirroring how cps-api's own menu tree groups several simple-master lookups under a roughly "ข้อมูลหมู่" (master data) parent — future simple-master CRUD pages (units, suppliers, material-models, etc.) now have a natural home at `/master-data/<name>`.
- **Full mirror of the Materials PC pattern**, not Products — Categories is a flat simple-master with no sub-records, no wizard, no FK-heavy lookups, so the page is `page.tsx` + `actions.ts` in the route folder plus a `categories/` component folder with the same six files (client / filters / table / form-dialog / status-dialog / details-dialog), instead of Products' `*-page-content.tsx` + wizard split. `master-data/categories/page.tsx` mirrors `materials/pc/page.tsx`: `CATEGORY_VIEW` gate, `page`/`search`/`status` from searchParams, single `listCategories()` call, the client owns dialog state + Server Actions.
- **Table-only view (no card/list variants)** — categories is small/flat enough that adding Editorial card / Compact Row views would just re-layout the same handful of fields, not a meaningfully different reading mode. The `categories-table.tsx` file is structured to allow a future `view: "table" | "card"` if a real need shows up; the shared `useViewMode`/`ViewToggle` primitives are ready to reuse as-is.
- **`sortBy` defaults to `sortOrder`** (the only simple-master resource in `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 5.1 with this default — every other module defaults to `code`). Explicit in `page.tsx` so a future reader doesn't assume `code` ordering.
- **`iconColor` is a free-form CSS color string** (cps-api doesn't validate the format, max 20 chars). The form's zod schema applies a `^#(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{3}|[0-9a-fA-F]{6}|[0-9a-fA-F]{8})$` regex as a UX-only safety net so an unparseable value never silently saves. The live preview swatch + the table's `IconColorSwatch` both render the parsed color when the regex matches, and a neutral `Palette` icon placeholder when it doesn't — so the field is never visually broken while the user is mid-type and a stale/free-form value remains visible (and copyable) instead of collapsing into an empty space.
- **`code` is auto-uppercased server-side** (cps-api's `CategoriesService#normalizeCode` trims + `toUpperCase()` on write) but the form accepts any case on input — no client-side `onInput` transform (which would fight the user's IME). Field hint reads "ระบบจะปรับเป็นตัวพิมพ์ใหญ่ให้อัตโนมัติ".
- **Soft delete + optimistic concurrency**, same as Materials PC and Products: `DELETE /categories/:id` soft-deactivates, `PATCH /categories/:id/restore` restores; UI labels are "ปิดใช้งาน"/"เปิดใช้งาน" never "ลบ"; `UpdateCategoryDto.updatedAt` is required (a stale value 409s), carried forward in the edit form's payload, mapped to the same "อัปเดตจากที่อื่นแล้ว" toast shape.
- **No `parentId` UI today** — the backend supports a 1-level parent/child tree but the admin UI doesn't surface it (list is flat, form has no parent picker, details dialog's Data Sheet omits the row). Matches the "add only what's asked" scope; if a parent picker is needed later, the wire shape is already there on the `Category` type. See the new "Categories (CRUD)" section under Conventions for the full writeup.
- **Test coverage** — `src/lib/api/categories.test.ts` (10 tests, same `t.mock.method(globalThis, "fetch", ...)` pattern as `process-steps.test.ts`) covers every API function's URL + headers + body shape + response parsing. `src/app/(dashboard)/master-data/categories/categories-actions.test.ts` (10 tests, same pattern as `materials-pc-actions.test.ts` and `products-actions.test.ts`) covers every `perform*` helper: 200 success, 409 → Thai "อัปเดตจากที่อื่นแล้ว" mapping, joined API error message, network error → Thai "ไม่สามารถเชื่อมต่อกับเซิร์ฟเวอร์ได้", `updatedAt` forwarded on update, soft-delete/restore URLs and methods, and a cross-cutting test that asserts every public `*Action` wrapper short-circuits to the "session expired" error when no access token is present (so a future refactor that drops the cookie-reading layer is caught without mocking `next/headers`). ### 2026-09-07 — Material Receiving: status-driven gradient accent across all three views
- Follow-up to the button-contrast fix below: user asked to "เพิ่มการไล่เฉดสี ให้ดูง่าย และให้ข้อมูลสำคัญดูเด่น" (add color gradients, make it easy to read, make important info stand out) on `/materials/materials-receiving`.
- Added a `STATUS_ACCENT` map in `materials-receiving-table.tsx` keyed by `MaterialReceivingStatus` — each status carries a soft gradient `wash` class (draft → warm amber wash, confirmed → settled green wash, cancelled → neutral gray wash) and a `barColor` (the status's own token color) used as a left accent bar. Applied consistently to **all three views**: the Editorial card (photo-tile background + a left border bar + a dedicated gradient "stage" panel around the จำนวนรับเข้า hero stat, `from-primary-soft/60 to-transparent`), the Compact List row (row background wash + left bar), and the dense Table row (row background wash + left bar). A viewer can now tell a row's status from its color before reading any text, on every view, not just from the badge.
- **Hit and fixed a real CSS cascade bug while wiring this up**: the left accent bar was originally a Tailwind `border-l-{color}` utility class (e.g. `border-l-success`) applied alongside a base `border border-border` class. Both target `border-left-color` at equal specificity in the same `@layer utilities`, and — confirmed empirically via computed-style checks, including an isolated dynamically-injected `<style>` reproduction — the utility that "wins" is **not** reliably "whichever class comes later in the `className` string" the way plain non-layered CSS behaves; `border-border` kept winning regardless of class order. **Fixed by moving the left-border color to an inline `style={{ borderLeftColor: accent.barColor }}`** (using the raw CSS custom property, e.g. `var(--success)`) instead of a Tailwind class, and scoping the base border class to `border-y border-r` (Card/List) so it no longer touches the left side at all. Verified the fix by reading the actual computed `border-left-color` in the live browser after the change (`rgb(52, 211, 153)` = `--success`, `rgb(46, 54, 68)` = `--border-strong` for cancelled) — this is a case where a screenshot alone would not have proven correctness, since the wrong color was a *subtle* muted gray that could pass a casual visual glance.
- **Rule for future accent-bar / conflicting-border work**: don't rely on Tailwind class order to resolve a same-property, same-specificity, same-layer conflict (e.g. a directional `border-l-*` color utility vs. an all-sides `border-{color}` utility) — either scope the base class to exclude that side (`border-y border-r` instead of `border`) or, if the color needs to be data-driven from a JS map anyway, just set it via inline `style` and skip the ambiguity entirely.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors on the changed file), `pnpm test` (148/148, unaffected — pure styling), live-verified against the real backend via a disposable temp SUPER_ADMIN account (created and deleted via `create-super-admin.ts` + a throwaway cleanup script) — confirmed via `getComputedStyle` in the browser that Card, List, and Table rows all resolve the correct status color on the left border for both `confirmed` (green) and `cancelled` (muted) rows in the current dataset.

### 2026-09-07 — Material Receiving: table/card/list view toggle (mirrors Materials PC)
- User asked `/materials/materials-receiving` to show every display type (card, table, etc.) like the existing examples in the app. Reused the exact `useViewMode`/`ViewToggle` mechanism Materials PC already established (see Conventions § Materials PC) — `materials-receiving-client.tsx` now calls `useViewMode("materials-receiving", "table")` and renders `<ViewToggle modes={["table", "card", "list"]} />` next to the filters, same placement pattern as Materials PC's toolbar.
- `materials-receiving-table.tsx` gained a `MaterialsReceivingCollection` component (mirrors `MaterialPcCollection`'s shape exactly) that branches on `view` into the existing table renderer (unchanged), a new **card** renderer, and a new **list** (compact row) renderer. `MaterialsReceivingTable` now takes a `view` prop and delegates to `MaterialsReceivingCollection`, keeping pagination/empty-state markup as before.
- **No material photo exists on this resource** (`MaterialReceiving` has no `imagePath`), unlike Materials PC's card — so the card's visual anchor is the **Internal Lot number** as a mono uppercase identity line (echoing the create dialog's `LotTicket` treatment, see AGENTS.md § Material Receiving dialog redesign) instead of an image. The received quantity is the data-hero number (28px, tabular-nums), box count sits beside it with a `Boxes` icon, and a small 2-row data-sheet table (Supplier Lot, วันที่รับเข้า) sits below — same `DataSheetRow`-style `<table>` markup Materials PC's Editorial card uses for its facts. Actions are the same `getMaterialsReceivingRowActions` used by the table (via `RowActionsMenu`) plus a visible "ดูรายละเอียด" button and, for a draft the viewer can confirm, a visible primary "ยืนยัน" button — same "1-2 visible buttons on a card, Meatballs in dense rows" split documented in Conventions § Row actions.
- The **list** (compact row) view mirrors Materials PC's `MaterialListItem`: a small icon tile (no photo to show, so a generic `Package` icon substitutes), Internal Lot + status badge on one line, material code/name as the row title, Supplier Lot + receive date as the meta line, quantity/box-count right-aligned, `RowActionsMenu` on the far right.
- Default view stays `"table"` (unlike Materials PC's `"card"` default) — this list is typically scanned by lot number/date, which a table suits well, and the user didn't ask for a different default, only for the other views to exist.
- No changes to data fetching, Server Actions, permissions, or the create/details/cancel dialogs — this pass only added alternate renderers for the same `receivings` list already being fetched in `page.tsx`.
- Not click-tested live in a browser this pass — per R0, no lint/typecheck/build was run either; worth a manual check of all three views (especially the card grid's `@container` breakpoints) before relying on this in prod.

The new 18% outer safe zone contains the complete node-and-label block while preserving a clear centre-to-component gap and radial symmetry.
