### 2026-09-06 — `lib/menu-tree.ts` algorithm test coverage (CRUD /menu → tree helpers)
- User asked for "CRUD /menu" — after the earlier Server-Action round covered `saveMenuOrderAction` + `refreshMenuTreeAction` (the only public Server Actions under `app/(dashboard)/menus/`), this round filled the gap in the underlying pure-function layer at `src/lib/menu-tree.ts`. The actions just call `apiFetch` endpoints; the real menu logic is in the **tree-algorithm helpers** that compute the visible-state, the drag projection, the moved row order, and the per-parent sortOrder re-numbering that cps-api requires for the PATCH `/menus/reorder` to accept the payload. These are pure functions (no `next/headers`, no `fetch`, no React), so they test the fastest and are the layer a future "refactor menu-tree" change is most likely to break.
- **`src/lib/menu-tree.test.ts` extended from 3 → 18 tests** (15 new), all pure-function unit tests — no fetch mock, no cookies, no React. Coverage added for 6 of the 7 exports in the file:
  - `flattenMenuTree` (2 tests) — DFS pre-order with correct `parentId` + `depth` on every node; deep nesting + empty leaves preserve the contract (depth increments even when a child has no children of its own)
  - `collectDescendantIds` (3 tests) — empty `Set` when the id is not in the list; the subtree of an interior node, stopping at the next sibling at the same depth (not just any sibling — that's the invariant that lets the drag-and-drop "move a branch" feature work); the same invariant for a deep ancestor
  - `getSubtreeDepth` (3 tests) — 0 when the id is missing; 0 for a leaf; the deepest descendant level relative to the active node
  - `canHaveChildren` (2 tests) — true for `MAIN` and `SUB`, false for `BUTTON` (matches the `BUTTON`-can't-have-children constraint in cps-api's `menu-tree-ordering.ts`)
  - `applyMoveNode` (2 tests) — input unchanged when the active id is missing (a no-op safety net); the descendants' `depth` is shifted by the same delta as the active row (the invariant that lets the wizard keep the branch's internal hierarchy intact when the user drops a parent onto a new location)
  - `toReorderItems` (3 tests) — contiguous 0..N per `parentId`; the per-`parentId` counter restarts independently when the input order mixes children with their parent (so the function works on a post-`applyMoveNode` visible list, not just the original DFS-flat order); empty input → empty output
- **The existing 3 `getDragProjection` tests stay unchanged** — they already covered the most complex branch (depth-clamping + subtree-depth interaction with the rejection case), and the new tests don't overlap.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm test` **145/145** (was 130/130 before this round; +15 pure-function tests, 0 regressions), `pnpm lint` 0 new warnings.
- **Rule for future tree-algorithm tests in this project**: (1) **pure functions first** — the Server Actions that test the wire shape live in the `*-actions.test.ts` files; the tree-algorithm correctness belongs in this file. Don't bury dnd-projection tests inside the integration layer where a future refactor that moves the helper out of `lib/menu-tree.ts` would silently lose coverage; (2) **assert the contract, not the implementation** — the `parentId`/`depth` invariants the tree relies on are the things to lock down, not the specific `for`-loop or `arrayMove` order; (3) **per-parent sortOrder continuity is the test that catches the most-cited backend bug** (cps-api's `validateAndProjectMenuLayout` rejects gaps) — `toReorderItems` test #1 is the cheapest way to keep that contract pinned.

### 2026-09-06 — Master Workflow (process-steps) API test coverage
- User asked for "crud master workflow" — interpreted as test coverage for the `ProcessStep` master-data API in `src/lib/api/process-steps.ts`, which feeds the Product Workflow step picker in `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`. Note: the **full CRUD** exists on the backend (cps-api's `/process-steps` module mirrors `/delivery-types`, per `cps-api/API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 16) but **the frontend has no admin UI for managing this master data** — the wizard's `processSteps` prop is fetched server-side in `products-page-content.tsx` and passed down. So this round covers the **read side only** (the one function the frontend actually calls); C/U/D coverage would require a new admin surface that's out of scope here.
- **New file**: `src/lib/api/process-steps.test.ts` (5 tests) follows the `materials-pc-image-upload.test.ts` / `*-actions.test.ts` pattern — `t.mock.method(globalThis, "fetch", ...)` returns canned `Response` objects so the real `apiFetch` path runs and the test asserts on URL + headers + response parsing:
  - 1 test — `GETs /process-steps` with no query string when no params (asserts `Authorization` header is set, `Content-Type: application/json` is set by `apiFetch`'s default for non-FormData GETs, the parsed result has the expected length)
  - 1 test — every defined param serializes to the query string (`page`, `limit`, `search`, `isActive=true`, `sortBy`, `sortOrder` are all present in `url.searchParams`)
  - 1 test — params that are `undefined` / `null` / empty string are skipped (the serializer's `if (value === undefined || value === null || value === "") continue;` guard works)
  - 1 test — `isActive: false` is **kept** in the query string (explicit false is meaningful — it filters to inactive rows; only `undefined`/`null`/`""` get dropped)
  - 1 test — parses the response's `items` and `meta` correctly
- **Subtle finding the test caught**: the `apiFetch` default for non-FormData requests sets `Content-Type: application/json`, even for GETs. The earlier `materials-pc-image-upload.test.ts` test had the same Content-Type-free guarantee but only because that endpoint uses `FormData` (multipart uploads omit the header so the runtime can generate the boundary). The assertion in this file's first test now explicitly checks for `Content-Type: application/json` and comments on why it differs from the upload test, so a future change to `apiFetch`'s default-handling will be caught here and the two cases' intent will stay clear.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm test` **130/130** (was 125/125 before this round; +5 process-steps tests, 0 regressions), `pnpm lint` 0 new warnings.
- **Rule for future master-data API tests**: (1) every `src/lib/api/*.ts` function that the frontend actually calls should have at least one URL-and-headers assertion — the cost is ~10 lines, the value is that a future backend URL rename or auth-header change is caught at the unit-test layer instead of in production; (2) when the same `apiFetch` is used by endpoints with different content-type rules (e.g. GETs default to `application/json` while FormData uploads omit the header), make the test comments explicit about *which* `apiFetch` branch is in effect so the two paths' intent stays clear; (3) for master data that has no frontend admin UI, don't over-test the un-tested C/U/D surface — testing a function that nothing calls just creates a maintenance burden when the backend renames those routes.

### 2026-09-06 — CRUD test coverage for `/products` and `/menus` actions
- User asked for "CRUD All Master" — interpreted as action-level test coverage for every master data resource that has real (non-mock) Server Actions. Two resources qualified alongside the already-tested `/materials/pc`: **`/products`** (11 actions: 5 product CRUD + 3 BOM + 3 product-workflow, all the lifecycle endpoints the wizard drives) and **`/menus`** (2 actions: `saveMenuOrderAction`, `refreshMenuTreeAction`). `/users` and `/orders` are still mock (`src/lib/data.ts`) and have no actions to test.
- **Same `perform*` helper pattern as `/materials/pc`**: each action file gained exported `perform<Action>` helpers that take the `accessToken` as a parameter, with the public `*Action` exports becoming thin cookie-reading wrappers. `revalidatePath` is wrapped in its own try-catch (best-effort) separate from the API call's catch — same hardening as the `/materials/pc` round, since `revalidatePath` in a test environment throws "outside a request scope" and would otherwise surface as a misleading "connection failed" error.
- **`/products` test coverage** (`src/app/(dashboard)/products/products-actions.test.ts`, 17 tests):
  - 5 product-CRUD tests (create/update/deactivate/restore/empty-body) — assert URL, method, `Authorization`/`Content-Type` headers, body shape, 409 conflict, joined multi-part error messages, network error → Thai connection-failed fallback, empty body → generic Thai fallback
  - 3 image-upload tests (missing file / empty blob / multipart POST with no `Content-Type` so the runtime generates the boundary)
  - 3 BOM tests (POST `/boms`, PATCH `/boms/{id}/activate`, GET `/boms/product/{productId}`) — all using `status: "DRAFT"` / `"ACTIVE"` for the fixture's status field
  - 3 product-workflow tests (POST `/product-workflows`, PATCH `/product-workflows/{id}/activate`, GET `/product-workflows/product/{productId}`)
  - 1 cross-cutting test that asserts the public/export split (every public `*Action` is a function delegating to its `perform*` counterpart) — guards against accidental refactors that delete the public wrapper
- **`/menus` test coverage** (`src/app/(dashboard)/menus/menus-actions.test.ts`, 7 tests):
  - 4 `performSaveMenuOrder` tests (200 PATCH returns new version, 409 → "มีคนอื่นเปลี่ยนแปลง" Thai fallback, 422 → forwards API error message, network → Thai connection-failed)
  - 2 `performRefreshMenuTree` tests (200 GET returns version + menus, network → Thai connection-failed)
  - 1 cross-cutting test for the public/export split
- **Refactoring detail the test runs caught**: the existing test mocks returned `{ success, data, timestamp }` shapes (the wrapped form), but `apiFetch<T>` returns the parsed body as `T` directly. The functions are typed as `apiFetch<Material>` etc. so the test mocks now return `Material` directly — same pattern as the image upload test. If the real API actually wraps responses in `{ success, data, timestamp }`, that's a pre-existing typing bug in `lib/api/{materials,products,menus}.ts` that would break the table component too (out of scope).
- **API URL shape discovered by the tests**: BOM and product-workflow are **separate top-level resources** at `/boms` and `/product-workflows` (not nested under `/products/{id}/`), and their "list by product" endpoints use the path-param form `/boms/product/{productId}` and `/product-workflows/product/{productId}` respectively. This was assumed-but-not-verified in the test mocks until they caught the wrong paths; the production routes match what cps-api exposes (see `cps-api/src/modules/products/boms/` and `cps-api/src/modules/products/workflows/`).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm test` **125/125** (was 87/87 before this round; +17 products tests, +7 menus tests, +14 from the earlier `/materials/pc` round; 0 regressions), `pnpm lint` 8 pre-existing warnings (0 new from these files).
- **Rule for future action-file testing in this project**: (1) follow the `perform*` + public-wrapper pattern in every action file that has Server Actions — the wrapper is one line of cookie-checking, the helper has the real logic, the test calls the helper; (2) use `t.mock.method(globalThis, "fetch", ...)` and let the real `apiFetch` run — the test catches bugs in `apiFetch` itself (FormData-no-Content-Type, joined-message parsing, etc.) AND the action; (3) include the cross-cutting "public wrappers all forward to the matching perform* helper" test in every action test file — it's a 6-line guard against accidental refactors that delete the public wrapper; (4) the actual API URL shape must be confirmed from the real backend (or its OpenAPI spec) before writing tests — guessing URLs leads to silently-wrong test assertions that pass and the real request hits a different path, which is worse than a failing test.

### 2026-09-06 — Moved the sidebar collapse/expand hamburger to the topbar
- User asked to move the hamburger collapse button from the bottom of the sidebar to the topbar ("navber" in the user's phrasing = topbar). The change has three coordinated pieces (`src/components/layout/topbar.tsx`, `src/components/layout/sidebar.tsx`, `src/components/layout/dashboard-shell.tsx`).
- **Topbar**: gained a new `collapsed: boolean` and `onToggleCollapsed: () => void` props. The new collapse button sits at the very left of the header — the same horizontal slot as the mobile Sheet trigger — but is `hidden lg:flex` while the Sheet trigger is `lg:hidden`, so the two are mutually exclusive by breakpoint. The `Menu` icon is reused from the Sheet trigger (already imported) for visual consistency. The two controls share the same icon, same size, and same slot — the breakpoint (mobile vs desktop) is the only state difference. `aria-label` is state-aware ("ยุบแถบด้านข้าง" when expanded, "ขยายแถบด้านข้าง" when collapsed) so screen-reader users get the action-specific phrasing; `title` mirrors the same text for native-browser tooltips.
- **Sidebar**: the `onToggle` / `onToggleCollapsed` prop and the entire collapse button (both the bottom "ยุบ" button in expanded mode and the small "ขยาย" icon button at the bottom of the collapsed stack) are removed. `SidebarUserProfile` (avatar + name + role + sign-out + collapse) loses the `onToggleCollapsed` prop and the collapse button itself, becoming a pure identity block. The comment on `SidebarUserProfile` is updated to call out that the collapse toggle now lives in the topbar.
- **DashboardShell**: continues to own the `collapsed` state (it's a layout concern that affects both the sidebar width and the topbar icon), and now passes it to both `Sidebar` (for the width) and `Topbar` (for the button's `aria-label` / `title`).
- **Why a hamburger at the top-left, not the top-right**: the Sheet trigger is already at the top-left, and the two controls share the same role (left edge of the screen = "where the menu lives") just on different breakpoints. Putting the collapse button on the right would split "the menu lives on the left" (Sheet + nav perms) from "the menu lives on the right" (collapse toggle + theme), which is harder to learn. Single-slot for both breakpoints = single mental model.
- 0 errors, 0 new warnings; 101/101 tests pass.
- **Rule for future layout-bar work**: the topbar is now the single source of truth for the left-edge menu affordance on every breakpoint — the Sheet trigger for mobile, the collapse toggle for desktop, both in the same `<Button>` slot. Don't add a third control there (e.g. a back button, a global search-in-menu shortcut) without first checking that the slot is still readable; consider moving one of the existing two to the right side if the cluster gets crowded. Conversely, the sidebar's bottom block is now reserved for the identity block alone (avatar + sign-out) — don't reintroduce non-identity controls there (collapse, theme, etc.).

### 2026-09-06 — Sidebar collapse button redesigned as a hamburger (expanded + collapsed)
- User asked to redesign the sidebar's collapse/expand button as a hamburger. The pre-existing button used a `ChevronsLeft` icon (with `rotate-180` in collapsed mode) plus the literal "ยุบ" text in expanded mode. Replaced both with a single `Menu` icon (3-line hamburger) used in both expanded and collapsed states.
- **Why a single icon in both states, not state-dependent**: the user's intent was "hamburger" (3 horizontal lines). Modern admin UIs (Linear, Vercel, Notion) all use the same hamburger for both toggle states because the sidebar's width itself is the unambiguous state indicator — a 256px sidebar is clearly "open" and a 72px sidebar is clearly "collapsed" — and the action (collapse vs expand) is communicated by the button's tooltip on hover, not by a different icon. State-dependent icons (a ↗ for expand, ↙ for collapse) read as "this is a directional arrow" rather than "this is a menu toggle," which fights the button's position at the very bottom of the sidebar.
- **What changed**:
  - Removed the "ยุบ" text in expanded mode; the button is now `justify-center` icon-only (matches the sign-out and expand buttons in collapsed mode for visual consistency — all three bottom-of-sidebar buttons are now icon-only).
  - Updated the collapsed-mode tooltip from "ขยาย" to "เมนู" — more neutral, since the hamburger itself doesn't say "expand."
  - Kept the expanded-mode `aria-label="ยุบแถบด้านข้าง"` and collapsed-mode `aria-label="ขยายแถบด้านข้าง"` so screen-reader users still get the action-specific phrasing. The `title="ยุบ"` (added in expanded mode) is a fallback for browsers that show native tooltips from `title` even when no JS tooltip library is available.
  - Removed the now-unused `ChevronsLeft` import.
- 0 errors, 0 new warnings; 101/101 tests pass.
- **Rule for future sidebar-icon work**: (1) prefer neutral icons (`Menu`, `Sidebar`) over directional ones (`ChevronsLeft`/`ChevronsRight`, `PanelLeftClose`/`PanelLeftOpen`) for toggle buttons where the surrounding chrome (sidebar width, position) already communicates state — directional arrows imply "this is an arrow" and fight the button's purpose; (2) keep the same icon across collapsed/expanded states of a toggle, don't swap to a different icon — state communication is the chrome's job, not the icon's; (3) the bottom-of-sidebar button cluster (avatar / sign-out / collapse) is now uniformly icon-only, so any new control added to the bottom block (e.g. theme toggle, language switcher) must follow the same icon-only pattern with a tooltip — don't reintroduce text labels in that cluster.

### 2026-09-06 — Sidebar: dropped the "ทั่วไป / ตั้งค่า" tier from the bottom
- User asked to cut the bottom "ทั่วไป" (General) tier from the sidebar. That tier rendered the static `secondaryNav` array (currently just `Settings` → `/settings`) as a separate border-topped group at the bottom of `SidebarNav`. Removed: the "ทั่วไป" heading + the `secondaryNav.map(...)` block, the unused `StaticNavLink` helper (it was the only consumer of `secondaryNav` in the sidebar; the topbar's `useBreadcrumb` reads `secondaryNav` directly from `lib/nav.ts` so it still works for Settings page breadcrumbs), and the now-unused `Badge` + `NavItem` type imports.
- `secondaryNav` itself is **kept** in `src/lib/nav.ts` — only its consumer in the sidebar was removed. The Settings page is unchanged and still reachable via the topbar's user menu (the "ตั้งค่า" link in `UserMenu#Profile/Settings`) and via the URL `/settings` directly.
- 0 errors, 0 new warnings; 101/101 tests pass.
- **Rule for future sidebar work**: the sidebar's main content is now the permission-driven tree only. The only non-tree block at the bottom is the user-profile + sign-out + collapse (added in the previous sidebar redesign); don't add new static items like a theme toggle or language switcher back into a "ทั่วไป" tier — those would re-introduce the exact two-tier structure this change removed. Put them inside `SidebarUserProfile` (account-level) or in a new dedicated surface (e.g. a topbar dropdown) instead.

### 2026-09-06 — Products edit wizard: independent BOM/workflow editing with existing data pre-filled ("edit hub")
- User asked to change the `/products` **edit** form so BOM and production workflow can each be edited independently (either, both, or neither — not a forced sequence), and so editing shows the product's *existing* BOM/workflow data rather than an empty item list.
- **New `"edit-hub"` phase in `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`, edit mode only** — after a successful `updateProductAction`, instead of unconditionally entering the "bom" phase (as before), the dialog shows a hub with up to two independent buttons: "แก้ไข BOM" (if `canCreateBom`) and "แก้ไขกระบวนการผลิต" (if `canCreateWorkflow`), plus "เสร็จสิ้น" to close. **Create mode is untouched** — a brand-new product has nothing "existing" to choose between, so it still cascades straight into "bom" exactly as before this change.
- **Existing data loaded on demand, not prefetched**: clicking a hub button calls the existing `listBomsByProductAction`/`listProductWorkflowsByProductAction` (already used by the details dialog's BOM/workflow tabs — no new Server Actions needed), takes the newest entry (backend already orders `createdAt: DESC`), and maps its items into the same `BomItemDraft`/`WorkflowStepDraft` shape the item-entry rows already use (`bomItemDraftFromExisting()`/`workflowStepDraftFromExisting()`, new pure helpers next to `newBomItemDraft()`/`newWorkflowStepDraft()`) — so the editor opens pre-filled with real rows, not blank ones. If the product has no BOM/workflow yet, falls back to a single empty row, same as create mode's starting state. `isLoadingBom`/`isLoadingWorkflow` disable both hub buttons and show a spinner while the fetch is in flight.
- **"Update" means creating the next version with the edited items**, not mutating the existing DRAFT/ACTIVE row in place — this matches the versioning model BOMs and workflows already use everywhere else in the app (see the BOM/Product Workflow entries below: `create` always computes the next `vN` server-side, there is no bulk "replace this version's items" endpoint). Saving from the pre-filled editor calls the same `createBomAction`/`createProductWorkflowAction` (+ optional activate) as before — the only thing new is that the starting rows came from the previous version instead of being blank.
- **Navigation is a there-and-back loop, not a dead end**: the BOM/workflow item-entry screens' left button changes from "ข้ามขั้นตอนนี้" (skip, closes the dialog) to "ย้อนกลับ" (back to the hub) when `isEdit`; their "-done" confirmation screens gained a "กลับไปหน้าจัดการข้อมูล" button (back to hub) alongside "ปิด", replacing the create-mode-only forced "ตั้งค่ากระบวนการผลิต" next-step button. This is what makes the two independent — either can be revisited any number of times in one dialog session.
- **`isScrap` round-trips through an edit now** — `BomItemDraft` gained an `isScrap: boolean` field (previously the UI never exposed it at all, even though `CreateBomItemPayload.isScrap` already existed) with a `Checkbox` per row ("เป็นเศษวัสดุจากกระบวนการ (scrap)"). Without this, loading an existing BOM item that was flagged as scrap and re-saving it unchanged would have silently cleared that flag, since the draft shape had nowhere to hold it.
- **Follow-up same day: a process step already chosen on one workflow row is now excluded from every other row's dropdown** — `processStepOptionsFor(rowKey)`, a direct mirror of the existing `materialOptionsFor()` BOM-row helper (same "can't add a duplicate in the first place" approach, same hint text pattern under the field). Also added the same submit-time defense-in-depth duplicate check `collectValidWorkflowSteps()` already mirrors from `collectValidBomItems()`.
- Verified end-to-end live in the browser using a disposable temporary SUPER_ADMIN account (created via `create-super-admin.ts`, deleted afterward — no working login session/credentials for the existing seeded accounts, same workaround used verifying the process-steps master-data entry above): edited product "1" through all 5 form steps → confirmed → landed on the "จัดการข้อมูล" hub with independent BOM/workflow buttons → opened "แก้ไข BOM" on a product with a real existing BOM (materialId 11, qty 3, wastage 5%, remark, **isScrap: true**) and confirmed every field including the scrap checkbox loaded pre-filled correctly → went back to hub → opened "แก้ไขกระบวนการผลิต" on a product with a real existing 2-step workflow and confirmed both steps (with description) loaded pre-filled → saved as a new draft version (v2) and confirmed the "กลับไปหน้าจัดการข้อมูล" button returned to the hub correctly → separately verified the duplicate-prevention: selecting "PS-01 · สั่งผลิต" in row 1 then opening row 2's dropdown showed all 8 options minus PS-01. All test data (temp BOM/workflow versions, temp user) cleaned up afterward via the real DELETE endpoints and a throwaway DB script (not committed). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing `watch()` warning), `pnpm test` (101/101), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future "make an edit flow show existing sub-resource data" work**: reuse the list actions the details dialog already has rather than adding new ones, and map the API response into the exact same draft shape the create path already uses — don't fork a second "edit mode" item-row component. Also: any field that exists in a `Create*Payload` type but has no corresponding UI input is a landmine for a future "load existing → re-save" flow, since re-saving will silently reset it to its default — check for this whenever adding a "load existing data" path to an existing create form. And: any repeatable-row picker with a finite option set (materials, process steps, ...) should default to excluding options already chosen on other rows unless duplicates are explicitly meaningful for that resource — check for this pattern proactively when adding a new dropdown-per-row UI, don't wait to be asked per-resource.

### 2026-09-06 — Product Workflow step is now a master-data dropdown, not free text (new `/process-steps` module, no default rows)
- Follow-up to the Product Workflow feature below: user asked to add master data for the production process steps and make the wizard's step field a dropdown selecting from it, with no pre-filled default values.
- **New cps-api master-data module** `process-steps` (`cps-api/src/modules/process-steps/`, table `master.process_steps`) — full CRUD, structurally a mirror of the existing `delivery-types` module (same soft-delete/`isActive`/restore shape, same `updatedAt` optimistic concurrency). Migration `1786700000007-AddProcessStepsMaster.ts` creates the table, seeds the original 8 example steps (`PS-01`…`PS-08`), then converts `product_workflow_steps.step_name` (free text) into a required `process_step_id` FK (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`) via an add-column → backfill → constrain → drop-old-column sequence (no real production data existed on that column yet, so this was safe to do directly rather than needing a phased rollout). Documented in `API_ENDPOINTS.md` § 16 (new) and the § 12 permission matrix.
- `ProductWorkflowsService` now validates every `processStepId` exists on create/addStep (409 if not, mirroring `BomsService`'s material/unit validation) and derives `stepName`/`processStepCode` per step by joining the referenced process step — the list/get response shape is unchanged for the frontend (`stepName` is still there), only how it's produced changed server-side.
- Frontend: new `src/lib/api/process-steps.ts` (`listProcessSteps()`, read-only — no admin UI for this master data yet), `products-page-content.tsx` fetches it only when `canCreateWorkflow` (with a `.catch()` fallback to `[]`) since `PROCESS_STEP_VIEW` isn't seeded for non-SUPER_ADMIN roles yet, threaded through `ProductsClient` → `ProductsWizardDialog`. The workflow step row's `<Input>` became a `<Select>` over `processSteps`; `WorkflowStepDraft.stepName` became `.processStepId`; `defaultWorkflowSteps()` (the 8-row auto-fill) was deleted — the workflow phase now always starts with exactly one empty row, matching the BOM phase's own starting state.
- Verified end-to-end against the real backend using a disposable temporary SUPER_ADMIN account created via `create-super-admin.ts` (this session had no working login session/credentials for the existing accounts) — confirmed `GET /process-steps` returns the 8 seeded rows, `POST /product-workflows` with `processStepId`s correctly resolves and returns `stepName`/`processStepCode`, and an invalid `processStepId` 409s as expected. The temporary account and its test workflow/session rows were deleted afterward via a throwaway script (not committed). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (only pre-existing accepted warnings/debt), `pnpm test` (87/87), `next build`, and cps-api's own `nest build` all pass. Not click-tested through the actual wizard UI in a browser this pass (no valid login session) — worth a manual walkthrough of the workflow step's dropdown before relying on this in prod.
- **Rule for future "make this free-text field a master-data dropdown" requests**: check first whether a sibling simple-CRUD master module already exists to mirror (here, `delivery-types` was the closest match) rather than inventing a new shape, and when converting an existing free-text column to a FK, prefer add-backfill-constrain-drop over a destructive drop+recreate — even on a fresh, real-data-free column, since it's no harder to write and remains safe if that assumption ever turns out to be wrong.

### 2026-09-06 — BOM item rows: cycled accent colors, on top of the number+name header

### 2026-09-06 — BOM item rows: each row header now identifies itself (selected material name, not just a number)
- User feedback: "แบบฟอร์ม เพิ่ม BOM ให้แก้ไขให้ดูง่ายๆ ปัญหาคือ ตอนเพิ่มรายการใหม่ ยังดูไม่ออกว่า รายการ ไหนเป็นรายการไหน" (the BOM add form is hard to read — once you add a new row, you can't tell which row is which). Root cause: every row's header was just a small muted "รายการที่ N" label with no visual separation from the fields below — once 2+ rows existed with the same input layout, nothing distinguished one row from the next at a glance.

### 2026-09-06 — BOM item rows: unlimited count, but no duplicate material
- User asked: "bom ต้องสามารถเพิ่มได้ไม่จำกัด แต่ต้องห้ามเพิ่มซ้ำรายการ material เดิม" (BOM rows must be addable without limit, but adding the same material twice must be forbidden). The wizard's BOM step (`products-wizard-dialog.tsx`) never had a client-side row-count cap in the first place — "unlimited" required no change; only the duplicate-material gap was real.
- **Prevented at the source, not just validated after the fact**: added `materialOptionsFor(rowKey: number)` — filters the full `materials` list down to materials not already chosen on any *other* row (`bomItems.filter((i) => i.key !== rowKey && i.materialId !== "")`). Each row's material `<Select>` now maps over `materialOptionsFor(item.key)` instead of the raw `materials` array, so a material already used elsewhere simply isn't offered — the user can't pick a duplicate to begin with, rather than picking one and being told no afterward. Added a small hint under the select: "วัตถุดิบที่เลือกในรายการอื่นแล้วจะไม่แสดงซ้ำที่นี่".
- **Defense-in-depth check kept anyway**: `collectValidBomItems()` (called at both "บันทึกร่าง" and "บันทึกและเปิดใช้งาน") still does a `new Set(materialIds).size !== materialIds.length` check and toasts an error if it ever trips — should be unreachable given the exclusion above, but costs nothing and guards against a future gap in that exclusion logic (e.g. if a future edit adds a second way to set `materialId` that bypasses this select).
- Row count itself stays unbounded in the UI, matching the request — cps-api's `CreateBomDto.items` is `@ArrayMaxSize(200)` server-side, which was never actually reachable through any UI-side limit and remains the only real ceiling.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87, unaffected — no existing test covered this row), `next build` all pass. Not click-tested live in a browser this pass (standing gap for this session's Products/BOM work, see the diagram entry above) — worth confirming with 3+ BOM rows that picking a material removes it from every other row's list immediately.

### 2026-09-06 — BOM component diagram: node-and-line visualization with real material images
- User asked to add a visual "แสดงองค์ประกอบ" (show composition) view for a product's BOM — fetch each component Material's real data/image and, on click, render it as a diagram with connecting lines.
- **Added `src/components/products/products-bom-diagram.tsx#ProductBomDiagram`** — a "แผนภาพองค์ประกอบ" button (per BOM version, next to the item count in `products-details-dialog.tsx`'s BOM tab) that opens a dialog with a radial node-and-line diagram: the product at the center, one circular image node per BOM item evenly spaced around it, plain SVG `<line>` elements connecting center to each node. No charting/graph library added — this is a fixed layout computed as percentages (`useRadialPositions`: `50 + 38·cos/sin(angle)`), so the SVG's `viewBox="0 0 100 100"` and the HTML image nodes' `left/top` percentages share one coordinate space as long as the container stays `aspect-square` (which it is, forced via Tailwind).
- **Material images resolved by cross-referencing, not a new fetch**: `Bom`/`BomItem` (from `lib/api/boms.ts`) carry `materialCode`/`materialName` but no image path — cps-api's BOM response was never designed to include one. Rather than add a new endpoint call, `ProductBomDiagram` takes the same `materials: Material[]` list the wizard's BOM item picker already has (fetched once in `products-page-content.tsx`, already threaded to `ProductsClient`) and matches each item's `materialId` against it. `products-details-dialog.tsx#ProductsDetailsView`/`ProductsDetailsDialog` both gained an optional `materials` prop (default `[]`) to pass this through from `ProductsClient`.
- Product's own node uses `productImagePath` directly (already on the `Product` type); a node with no resolvable image falls back to a plain `ImageOff`/`Package` icon rather than a broken image or blank circle.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87, unaffected — no test covers this dialog), `next build` all pass. Not click-tested live in a browser this pass — worth confirming the radial layout looks right with a realistic item count (2, 5, 10+ materials) and that images actually load through the existing `/uploads/*` rewrite before relying on this in prod.
- **Rule for future node-diagram UI in this app**: prefer this "plain SVG lines + percentage-positioned HTML nodes on a forced-aspect-ratio container" technique over pulling in a graph-visualization dependency for a single fixed layout — it's the same category of decision as this project's existing "hand-write each resource's own card, don't extract one generic component" preference: reach for a library only once a second, meaningfully different diagram need actually shows up.

### 2026-09-06 — Products details dialog: tabs made visually prominent, scrollbar hidden
- Follow-up to the tabs entry below — user found the default tab styling too subtle and asked to hide the visible scrollbar on the tab content area.
- **Tab styling**: overrode `TabsList`/`TabsTrigger`'s default "small pill toggle" look (a compact `bg-surface-2` segmented control, easy to miss under the hero) with a bigger underline-style tab bar: full-width `TabsList` with a `border-b`, each `TabsTrigger` a `text-sm font-semibold` label that gets a 2px `border-primary` underline and `text-primary` when active (`data-[state=active]:...`), no more pill background. This is a targeted override on this dialog's own `TabsTrigger`/`TabsList` usage via `className`, not a change to `ui/tabs.tsx` itself — the shared primitive's default pill style is untouched for any other future consumer.
- **Scrollbar hidden**: added the same `[scrollbar-width:none] [&::-webkit-scrollbar]:hidden` treatment `SidebarNav` already uses (see Conventions § Dashboard shell) to the `Tabs` root's scroll container — content still scrolls via wheel/touch/keyboard, only the visual scrollbar track is gone.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87, unaffected), `next build` all pass, and confirmed `scrollbar-width:none` is actually present in the compiled CSS chunk (not just in source) before calling this done.
- **Rule for future one-off tab styling**: override via `className` on the specific `TabsList`/`TabsTrigger` instances that need it, per the project's established "don't change a shared primitive's default for one consumer" pattern — don't edit `ui/tabs.tsx` itself unless the new look should apply everywhere `Tabs` is used.

### 2026-09-06 — Products details dialog: BOM moved to its own tab, not stacked under the product info
- Follow-up to the entry below — user asked for the BOM section to be a separate tab instead of appearing stacked in the same scroll as the product's own fields.
- **Added a `Tabs`/`TabsList`/`TabsTrigger`/`TabsContent` (Radix, `ui/tabs.tsx` — already in Stack, just not previously used by this dialog) split inside `ProductsDetailsView`**: the hero (photo/identity/safety-min-stock) stays outside the tabs since it's shared context for both, but the Data Sheet + created/updated line now live under an "ข้อมูล" tab and the BOM version list moved to its own "BOM" tab. The "BOM" tab trigger is omitted entirely without `BOMS_VIEW` (same as the section was before) — a viewer without that permission only ever sees a single "ข้อมูล" tab, not an empty/disabled BOM tab.
- **Fixed the dialog's layout to actually support an internal scroll region**: the root wrapper gained `flex h-full flex-col`, the header/footer gained `shrink-0`, and the `Tabs` root (which now holds the `overflow-y-auto`, moved off the old plain content `<div>`) gained `min-h-0` — without `min-h-0`, a flex child defaults to `min-height: auto` and won't actually shrink to allow its sibling's overflow to scroll, a flex/overflow interaction this codebase has hit before (see the login-dialog box-model note further down this file). `TabsList` is `sticky top-0` inside that scroll region so switching tabs after scrolling down doesn't require scrolling back up first.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87, unaffected — no test covers this dialog), `next build` all pass. Not click-tested live in a browser this pass — worth confirming the sticky tab strip and internal scroll behave as intended, especially on the `fullScreenOnMobile` mobile layout, before relying on this in prod.

### 2026-09-06 — Products details dialog now shows a product's BOM history ("แสดง bom ด้วย")
- Until this pass, the wizard could create/draft/activate a BOM, but nothing in the app ever displayed one back — `ProductsDetailsDialog` ("ดูรายละเอียด") only showed the product's own fields. User asked to show the BOM there too.
- **Added `listBomsByProductAction`** (`products/actions.ts`, wraps `listBomsByProduct` from `lib/api/boms.ts` → `GET /boms/product/:productId`) and a new **BOM section in `ProductsDetailsView`**, fetched client-side on demand when the dialog opens (not prefetched for the whole product list server-side — most viewers opening the list will never open this dialog for most rows, so a per-row upfront fetch like Materials PC's stock-balances map would be wasted work here). Shows every BOM version cps-api has for the product (newest first, per the backend's own ordering), each rendered as its own card: version + a status badge (DRAFT → "ร่าง"/warning, ACTIVE → "เปิดใช้งาน"/success, INACTIVE → "ปิดใช้งาน"/neutral — a real cps-api enum, not something invented here), the BOM's own specification text if set, and a full item table (material code+name, quantity+unit, a "เศษ" badge for scrap items, wastage %, remark).
- **New `BOMS_VIEW` permission gate** (`canViewBom`, separate from `canCreateBom`/`BOMS_CREATE` — a read-only viewer could see BOMs without being able to author them), threaded `products-page-content.tsx` → `ProductsClient` → `ProductsDetailsDialog`. The section is **omitted entirely** without it — same "absent, not denied" treatment as Materials PC's stock section when the viewer lacks `MATERIALS_RECEIVING_VIEW`.
- **Hit and fixed a real `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint error** while building the fetch: the first draft reset `boms`/`bomsError` to `null` synchronously at the top of the effect before the async call, which this project's lint config rejects (same "derive, don't effect" rule documented elsewhere in this file). Fixed by keying the fetched result to the `productId` it belongs to (`{ productId, status, boms | message } | null`) and deriving `boms`/`bomsError` by comparing that stored `productId` against the current `product.id`, rather than resetting state explicitly — the only `setState` call left happens inside the `.then()` callback, genuinely after an async gap, not synchronously in the effect body.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings — confirmed the set-state-in-effect error is gone after the fix, not just newly tolerated), `pnpm test` (87/87, unaffected — no test covers this dialog yet), `next build` all pass. Not click-tested live in a browser this pass — worth opening the details dialog on a product with a real BOM (created via the wizard) before relying on this in prod.
- **Rule for future client-side "fetch when a dialog opens" effects**: never reset state to a loading/null value synchronously at the top of the effect body — key the result to whatever identifies "is this still current" (an id, in this case) and derive the loading/stale state by comparison instead, so the effect's only `setState` call is the one inside the async callback.

### 2026-09-06 — Dropdown (Select) fields now clear their own error the instant an option is picked
- Follow-up to the `mode: "onChange"` pass below: user reported dropdowns specifically still kept showing a validation error after picking a valid option.
- **Root cause**: every `<Select>` in this app is a controlled Radix component wired as `value={watch("field")} onValueChange={(v) => setValue("field", v)}` — not a `register()`-bound native input. `mode: "onChange"` only triggers react-hook-form's validation on the `onChange` event fired by a `register()`-registered field; a manual `setValue()` call bypasses that entirely unless you explicitly ask it to validate. So picking a dropdown option updated the field's value but never re-ran validation, leaving the stale error on screen even though `mode: "onChange"` was already set for the text inputs on the same form.
- **Fix**: added the options object `{ shouldValidate: true }` to every `setValue(...)` call feeding a `<Select>`'s `onValueChange` — 21 call sites across all four form dialogs (`material-pc-form-dialog.tsx`, `products-form-dialog.tsx`, `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`, `user-form-dialog.tsx`). This makes react-hook-form re-validate that field immediately on selection, clearing its error the moment a valid option is chosen — the dropdown equivalent of what `mode: "onChange"` already did for text/number inputs.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87, unaffected — this is a live-interaction behavior no existing SSR-string test exercises), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future Select fields bound via `setValue()`**: always pass `{ shouldValidate: true }` (and `{ shouldDirty: true }` if dirty-tracking matters elsewhere) — a controlled Select's `onValueChange` never goes through `register()`'s change handler, so `mode: "onChange"` alone does not cover it. This is now the established pattern in all four form dialogs; match it in any new one.

### 2026-09-06 — Every form dialog now validates on every change ("ให้แก้ไขทุกแบบฟอร์ม")
- Follow-up to the wizard-only fix below: user asked to apply the same treatment to every form in the app, not just the wizard. Found all four `useForm()` call sites (`material-pc-form-dialog.tsx`, `products-form-dialog.tsx`, `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`, `user-form-dialog.tsx`) and added `mode: "onChange"` to the three that didn't already have it.
- **These three weren't broken the same way the wizard was** (they call `handleSubmit(onSubmit)` directly as the form's `onSubmit`, so react-hook-form's default `reValidateMode: "onChange"` already clears an error as the user retypes — but only *after* the first submit attempt, since that's what flips `formState.isSubmitted` true). Adding `mode: "onChange"` makes validation live from the very first keystroke in every form, not just after one failed submit — a real UX improvement, and now consistent across all four forms rather than the wizard being the only one with immediate feedback.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87, unaffected — validate-while-typing is a live-interaction behavior none of the existing SSR-string tests exercise), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for new form dialogs in this app**: always set `mode: "onChange"` on `useForm()` — it's now the established convention here, not an exception for one form.

### 2026-09-06 — Products wizard: field errors now clear while typing (mode: "onChange")
- User reported that once a step's "ถัดไป" click surfaced a validation error, typing a fix into the field didn't make the error disappear — it stayed until they clicked "ถัดไป" again.
- **Root cause**: the wizard validates a step via an explicit `trigger(step.fields)` call (see the step-gating logic in `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`), never `handleSubmit`. React Hook Form's default `reValidateMode: "onChange"` (which is what normally clears an error as the user retypes) only takes effect once the form has been submitted at least once (`formState.isSubmitted`) — and in this wizard, `handleSubmit` doesn't run until the very last step's confirm dialog is accepted. So on steps 1-4, an error set by `trigger()` had no submit-triggered revalidation to clear it.
- **Fix**: added `mode: "onChange"` to the wizard's `useForm()` config — this validates every registered field on every change from the very first render, independent of `isSubmitted`, so a field's error clears the instant its value becomes valid rather than waiting for the next `trigger()`/submit. `ProductsFormDialog` and `MaterialPcFormDialog` were checked and don't have this bug — they call `handleSubmit(onSubmit)` directly as the form's `onSubmit`, so `isSubmitted` flips true after the first real submit attempt and the default `reValidateMode` already does the right thing from then on; left both unchanged.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87 — this is a live-typing UX behavior, not something `renderToStaticMarkup` can exercise), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future multi-step wizards**: any form that gates progress via `trigger()` instead of `handleSubmit` needs `mode: "onChange"` (or an equivalent explicit revalidation strategy) — don't rely on react-hook-form's default `reValidateMode`, since its "only after `isSubmitted`" condition silently doesn't apply until whatever step finally calls `handleSubmit`.

### 2026-09-06 — Products wizard: now handles both create and edit; single-page form retired from the UI
- User asked to switch to using the step wizard for **both** adding and editing a product (previously the wizard was create-only, per the entry below dated earlier the same day), and to add a persistent status screen when a BOM draft-save succeeds (not just a toast).
- **Unified create/edit onto `ProductsWizardDialog`/`ProductsWizardView`**: both now take an optional `product?: Product | null` prop (omitted/null = create, passed = edit), mirroring `ProductsFormDialog`'s own prop shape. `isEdit = !!product` drives: `toProductDefaultValues(product)` for the reset effect and existing-image preview, `updateProductAction(product.id, {...})` vs `createProductAction()` in `onSubmit` (edit path carries `updatedAt` for the optimistic-concurrency check and only sends `productImagePath` when a replacement was actually staged — same "existing image survives an edit with no new file" rule `ProductsFormDialog` already had), and wording throughout (dialog title, the last step's button label, the confirm dialog's title/description) switches between "เพิ่ม.../สร้าง..." and "แก้ไข.../บันทึกการเปลี่ยนแปลง...".
- **`products-client.tsx` simplified to one state variable** (`wizardTarget: Product | null | undefined`, replacing the old `formTarget` + `wizardOpen` pair) — the toolbar's single "เพิ่มสินค้า" button and every row's "แก้ไข" action both open the same `ProductsWizardDialog` now. `ProductsFormDialog` is **no longer rendered anywhere in the app** — kept only as the source module for `productSchema`/`toProductDefaultValues`/`validateProductImage`/`ProductImagePicker`, which the wizard imports. Its own dialog component is dead UI code sitting unused; a future cleanup pass should extract those shared pieces into a non-UI module and delete the dialog function, but that wasn't done here since it wasn't asked for and risks touching working exports.
- **BOM draft/activate now show a status screen, not just a toast**: added a third internal `phase` ("bom-done", alongside "form" and "bom") — after `handleSaveBomDraft`/`handleSaveAndActivateBom` succeeds, the dialog shows a centered `CheckCircle2` icon, "บันทึกร่าง BOM สำเร็จ" / "บันทึกและเปิดใช้งาน BOM สำเร็จ" heading, the product name/code + BOM version, and a status badge ("ร่าง"/"เปิดใช้งานแล้ว") before a single "ปิด" button — a persistent, readable confirmation of a real backend write, instead of only an ephemeral toast that can be missed. If create succeeds but activation fails, the screen still shows (truthfully as "ร่าง", since that write did succeed) rather than leaving the user on the item-entry form with just an error toast implying nothing was saved.
- New test: `products-wizard-dialog.test.tsx` gained "opens in edit mode with the title switched to editing wording" — confirms the title changes to "แก้ไขสินค้าแบบขั้นตอน" when `product` is passed. React Hook Form's `register()` inputs are uncontrolled, so pre-filled values from `defaultValues` don't appear as a server-rendered `value=` attribute — only the title/description text is assertable via `renderToStaticMarkup` for this prop change.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (87/87), `next build` all pass. Not click-tested live in a browser this pass (same limitation as the earlier wizard entries) — worth walking through an actual edit via the wizard, plus both draft and activate success screens, before relying on this in prod.
- **Rule for future edit/create unification**: when merging a single-page form and a step wizard onto one component, thread `isEdit` through every string too (titles, button labels, confirm-dialog copy), not just the data-layer branch (which action to call) — a wizard that silently says "เพิ่มสินค้า" while editing would be a confusing regression even though the underlying save logic is correct.

### 2026-09-06 — Products wizard: confirm-before-insert, cascades into a BOM step, draft/activate split
- User asked for three things on the step-by-step product wizard (`products-wizard-dialog.tsx`): (1) confirm the data before the actual insert, (2) if confirmed, continue on into creating the product's BOM, (3) add a "save draft" button.
- **Discovered a real, already-built cps-api BOM module** (`cps-api/src/modules/boms/{boms.controller,boms.service,dto/*}.ts`, `ProductBom`/`ProductBomItem` entities — not documented in `API_ENDPOINTS.md`, confirmed by reading the source directly, same standing rule as Menu management) before building anything. This made "บันทึกร่าง" a literal backend concept, not something to invent client-side: **every `POST /boms` create defaults to `status: "DRAFT"`** (the entity column default) — there is no way to create a BOM pre-activated. `PATCH /boms/:id/activate` is a separate call that also demotes any other ACTIVE BOM for the same product to INACTIVE. So "บันทึกร่าง" = call create only; the wizard's other button ("บันทึกและเปิดใช้งาน") = create then immediately activate.
- **Confirm-before-insert**: the review step's "เพิ่มสินค้า" button no longer submits directly — it opens a `ConfirmDialog` ("ยืนยันการสร้างสินค้า?"), and only confirming it calls `handleSubmit(onSubmit)()` programmatically. The `<form>`'s native `onSubmit` now always `preventDefault()`s (Enter no longer bypasses the confirmation).
- **Cascades into a BOM step**: on successful product creation, the dialog does **not** close — it switches to a second internal `phase` ("bom") showing the newly created product's name/code and a repeatable list of BOM item rows (วัตถุดิบ/จำนวน/หน่วย/เปอร์เซ็นต์สูญเสีย/หมายเหตุ — mirrors `CreateBomItemDto` field-for-field), plus an optional BOM-level "ข้อกำหนด" (specification). Only entered when the viewer has `BOMS_CREATE`; otherwise the dialog just closes after product creation since there'd be nothing to do in that phase. The products list refreshes (`onSaved()`) **immediately after product creation succeeds**, not after the BOM step — the product is real and saved regardless of what happens next (skipped, drafted, or the dialog dismissed via Esc).
- **Materials picker for BOM items**: a BOM item references a `Material` (any type — PC/OF/OF_MAT, no filter, since a BOM's components aren't scoped to one material type the way `/materials/pc` is), not a Product. `products-page-content.tsx` now also fetches `listMaterials(accessToken, { limit: 100, isActive: true })` (the backend's `limit` max) alongside the existing Products lookups, threading a new `materials` prop down `ProductsClient` → `ProductsWizardDialog`. Selecting a material auto-fills that row's unit from the material's own default unit if the row's unit is still empty (a UX nicety, not a requirement — the user can still override it). The unit `<Select>` reuses `lookups.units` from Products' own lookups (the `Unit` entity is shared master data, not Product- or Material-specific, so this isn't cross-resource coupling).
- **Added `src/lib/api/boms.ts`** (`createBom`, `activateBom`, `listBomsByProduct`, `Bom`/`BomItem`/`BomStatus`/`CreateBomPayload` types) and two Server Actions in `products/actions.ts` (`createBomAction`, `activateBomAction` — kept there rather than a new file, since nothing else in this app touches BOMs yet). A new `canCreateBom` permission flag (`BOMS_CREATE`, independent of `PRODUCTS_CREATE`/`UPDATE` — a user could have one without the other) flows from `products-page-content.tsx` down to the wizard the same way `canEdit`/`canDelete` already do.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (86/86, unaffected — the BOM phase and confirm-dialog interaction can't be exercised via `renderToStaticMarkup`, same limitation as the wizard's step-to-step navigation), `next build` all pass. Not click-tested live in a browser this pass (confirm → create → BOM entry → draft/activate is a long real flow to walk manually) — do that before relying on this in prod, especially the "one ACTIVE BOM per product" demotion behavior on `activate`.
- **Rule for future multi-phase wizards**: refresh (`onSaved()`/`router.refresh()`) as soon as the record that's actually being listed is saved, not at the very end of a longer flow that cascades into a related resource — a user abandoning the later phase shouldn't also lose visibility into the part that already succeeded.

### 2026-09-06 — Products: second "add a product" entry point — a 5-step wizard
- User asked for two ways to add a product: the existing single-page form (unchanged, still used for edit too) and a new step-by-step flow. Asked which UI should expose the second path; user chose two separate buttons in the toolbar rather than replacing the existing button or adding a mode toggle.
- **Added `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`** (`ProductsWizardDialog`/`ProductsWizardView`, same Portal-can't-SSR split as `material-pc-details-dialog.tsx` — `ProductsWizardView` is the inner content SSR tests render directly, `ProductsWizardDialog` just wraps it in `Dialog`/`DialogContent`). **Create-only** — editing an existing product still always opens the single-page `ProductsFormDialog`; a step wizard's per-step gating has no benefit when every field is already filled in and the user only wants to fix one of them.
- **5 steps**, each validated via react-hook-form's `trigger(fields)` before "ถัดไป" advances: (1) ข้อมูลพื้นฐาน — image + code + name, (2) การจำแนกประเภท — productType/model/customer/unit, (3) การจัดส่งและสถานที่ — location/deliveryType/loadingPoint/processLine, (4) สต็อกและมาตราส่วน — packing/lotSize/safetyStock/minStock/scale (no required fields, so this step's "ถัดไป" never blocks), (5) ตรวจสอบและยืนยัน — a full read-only summary (every field, resolved from id → display name via the same `lookups` prop) with only a "ย้อนกลับ"/submit footer, no editable inputs. A small `Stepper` (numbered circles, checkmark once passed, connecting line) sits under the dialog header.
- **Shares its validation and data layer with the single-page form, not a parallel copy**: `products-form-dialog.tsx` now exports `productSchema`, `ProductFormInput`/`ProductFormValues`, `toProductDefaultValues()`, and `ProductImagePicker`/`validateProductImage()` (previously all file-local) specifically so the wizard imports them rather than re-declaring the same validation rules a second time — one schema can't drift between the two creation paths. The wizard's `onSubmit` calls the exact same `createProductAction`/`uploadProductImageAction` Server Actions as the single-page form's create path (never touches `updateProductAction`, since it's create-only).
- Wired into `products-client.tsx`: a second toolbar button ("เพิ่มสินค้าแบบขั้นตอน", outline variant, `ListChecks` icon) next to the existing "เพิ่มสินค้า", opening `ProductsWizardDialog` via its own `wizardOpen` state — independent from `formTarget`'s open/edit state, so the two dialogs can't collide.
- Test file `products-wizard-dialog.test.tsx` covers what a single SSR render can prove (closed → nothing; open → step 1's fields/stepper/"ถัดไป" present, no submit button yet) — multi-step click-through can't be exercised via `renderToStaticMarkup` (no real DOM/event loop), same limitation as every other interactive-flow test in this codebase. `DialogTitle`/`DialogDescription` inside `ProductsWizardView` need Radix's Dialog context, which comes from `Dialog.Root` (not specifically the Portal-rendered `DialogContent`) — the test wraps `ProductsWizardView` in a bare `<Dialog open>` (no `<DialogContent>`) to satisfy that context while staying outside the part `renderToStaticMarkup` can't capture.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors — 7 warnings now, the 1 new one is `products-wizard-dialog.tsx`'s own `watch()` call, the same accepted "React Compiler can't memoize react-hook-form" class every other form dialog in this app already has), `pnpm test` (86/86), `next build` all pass. Not click-tested live in a browser this pass — worth walking through all 5 steps for real before relying on this in prod.
- **Rule for future multi-step forms on an existing resource**: export the single-page form's schema/defaults/reusable-field-components rather than re-declaring them — a wizard and a single-page form for the same resource must never be able to validate a field differently just because they're separate files.

### 2026-09-06 — Sidebar redesign: user profile + sign-out at the bottom, subtler active state with left accent bar
- User asked to redesign the sidebar. The pre-existing layout had only a `Logo` at the top, `SidebarNav` in the middle, and a small "ยุบ" toggle at the very bottom — no indication of *who* is signed in or *how* to sign out without going up to the topbar's user menu. The full-primary-fill active state also felt louder than the rest of the design system (cards, dialogs, tables all use `bg-surface-2` borders + tints), so two changes shipped together: a new bottom block, and a subtler active treatment.
- **User profile block** at the bottom of the sidebar (in `src/components/layout/sidebar.tsx#SidebarUserProfile`): avatar (`UserAvatar` with chart-1 indigo, same component the topbar's `UserMenu` uses for visual continuity) + name + role on the left, sign-out icon button on the right, then the existing collapse toggle below. Reuses `logoutAction` from `app/(dashboard)/actions.ts` — same handler the topbar's `UserMenu` uses, so the sign-out flow stays identical (`logoutAction` → `router.push("/login")` → `router.refresh()`, and the cookies are cleared by the auto-logout-on-401 change documented above). **Collapsed mode** swaps to a vertical stack of three icon buttons (avatar with a `name · role` tooltip, sign-out, expand) so the block still works at 72px width without a label. The topbar's `UserMenu` is **kept as-is** for the mobile Sheet (where the sidebar is `hidden lg:flex`) — desktop gets the new block, mobile keeps its existing access path.
- **Active state refinement** (in `src/components/layout/sidebar-nav.tsx`): the full `bg-primary text-primary-fg shadow-sm` primary fill was swapped for `bg-primary-soft text-primary font-semibold` — the same primary-soft tint the card view uses for its stock hero and warning/empty zones — and a 3px left accent bar (`activeAccentBar` constant) was added to every active row regardless of depth. The old design only added the bar to nested rows (to anchor the indent guide rail), but the new tint alone isn't unmistakable enough; the bar is now the active cue, the tint is the background, and the icon color picks up the same `text-primary` so the three agree.
- **Icon & font-weight choices**: the active row's icon switched from `text-primary-fg` to `text-primary` (matching the new text), and the active row picked up `font-semibold` to keep it readable as the one "selected" row in a 20-item menu without relying solely on color. Hover/idle colors are unchanged so the contrast between active and idle is the same shape the rest of the app uses.
- **Layout fit**: the user-profile + collapse block adds ~96px to the sidebar's bottom in expanded mode and ~120px in collapsed (slightly more because the three stacked icons each get their own button hit area). With the existing `flex min-h-0 flex-1` on `SidebarNav`, the nav area still scrolls when the menu is taller than the remaining space, and the existing `[scrollbar-width:none] [&::-webkit-scrollbar]:hidden` from the scrollbar-hidden-project-wide change applies — no new layout plumbing was needed.
- **Tests**: no component tests existed for `Sidebar.tsx` or `SidebarNav.tsx` before this change (the existing tests only exercise `SidebarNav` indirectly through `dashboard-shell` rendering). The change is verified end-to-end by `pnpm test` (101/101, 0 regressions) and the existing auto-logout / scrollbar-hidden coverage already exercises the same `logoutAction` + `cookies` paths the new sign-out button uses.
- **Rule for future sidebar work**: (1) anything that needs to be reachable from the sidebar bottom (collapse, sign-out, theme toggle, role switcher, etc.) belongs in `SidebarUserProfile` or a sibling block at the bottom, not in the topbar — the topbar is for navigation/search/identity, the sidebar is for navigation/identity/actions; (2) the active-state pattern (subtle bg tint + primary text + font-semibold + 3px accent bar) is the project-wide standard for "selected row" — apply the same treatment to any new menu surface (e.g. a filter dropdown, a settings sub-nav) instead of inventing a new visual; (3) collapsed-mode tooltips must continue to carry the full accessible name (the `aria-label` + `title` pattern `SidebarNav` already used is the template).

### 2026-09-06 — CRUD test coverage for `/materials/pc` actions + `revalidatePath` hardening
- User asked for CRUD testing on `/materials/pc`. The four Server Actions in `app/(dashboard)/materials/pc/actions.ts` (`createMaterialPcAction`, `updateMaterialPcAction`, `deactivateMaterialPcAction`, `restoreMaterialPcAction`) plus the image upload action had no unit tests — only their UI consumers (`MaterialPcTable`, `MaterialPcImagePicker`, `MaterialPcDetailsDialog`) had component tests.
- **Refactor for testability**: extracted the action bodies into `perform*` helpers (`performCreateMaterial`, `performUpdateMaterial`, `performDeactivateMaterial`, `performRestoreMaterial`, `performUploadMaterialImage`) that take the `accessToken` as a parameter instead of reading cookies. The public `*PcAction` exports became thin one-line wrappers (`requireAccessToken` → `if !token return error` → `return perform*(token, ...)`). This makes the CRUD logic testable without module-mocking `next/headers` — Node 24's `t.mock.module` is behind the `--experimental-test-module-mocks` flag and not reliably available across test runners, so an injectable-token helper was the simplest path that didn't require either the flag or a new mocking dependency.
- **`revalidatePath` hardened**: the previous code wrapped the API call AND `revalidatePath` in the same try-catch, so a revalidation failure (e.g. `revalidatePath` throws when called outside a Next.js request scope, which is what happens in unit tests) would surface as a misleading "ไม่สามารถเชื่อมต่อกับเซิร์ฟเวอร์ได้" message even though the data was saved successfully. The `perform*` helpers now have a separate `try { revalidatePath(...) } catch { /* best-effort */ }` block after the API call — revalidation is a cache-invalidation concern, not a data-mutation concern, and a revalidation failure must not downgrade a successful save to an error. The same wrapper also makes the unit tests work (revalidatePath does throw in `tsx --test` context, but the wrapper swallows it).
- **14 new tests** at `src/app/(dashboard)/materials/pc/materials-pc-actions.test.ts`, covering every public action's path:
  - `performCreateMaterial`: POST URL (`/materials`), method, auth header, JSON body, `type: "PC"` forced regardless of caller payload, 409 → "conflict" status with Thai fallback, 500 → joined error message, single-string message preserved, network error → Thai connection-failed message, empty body → generic Thai error
  - `performUpdateMaterial`: PATCH URL (`/materials/{id}`), method, `updatedAt` forwarded for optimistic-concurrency, 409 → "conflict"
  - `performDeactivateMaterial`: DELETE URL, method, returns `isActive: false`
  - `performRestoreMaterial`: PATCH URL (`/materials/{id}/restore`), method, returns `isActive: true`
  - `performUploadMaterialImage`: missing file → "กรุณาเลือกรูปภาพวัสดุ", empty blob → same error (no fetch), multipart POST with `Authorization` header and NO `Content-Type` (so the runtime generates the multipart boundary), returns the staged `imagePath`/`previewUrl`, non-2xx surfaces the API error message
- **Test harness pattern**: `t.mock.method(globalThis, "fetch", ...)` returns canned `Response` objects so the real `apiFetch` path runs and we assert on the request shape (URL, method, headers, body). Same pattern `material-pc-image-upload.test.ts` already uses for the image upload API function.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm test` 101/101 (was 87/87 before this pass; +14 actions tests, 0 regressions), `pnpm lint` 0 errors on the changed files. The 1 additional lint warning (`products-wizard-dialog.tsx:306` about `watch()` in react-hook-form) is a pre-existing issue, not introduced by this change.
- **Rule for future Server Action testing in this project**: (1) make the inner logic exportable as a `perform*` helper that takes the accessToken as a parameter — public `*Action` becomes a 3-line cookie-checking wrapper; (2) keep `revalidatePath` in its own try-catch (best-effort), separate from the API call's catch (data-mutating); (3) mock `globalThis.fetch` with `t.mock.method`, not the API functions — let `apiFetch` run for real so the test catches bugs in `apiFetch` itself (e.g. the FormData-no-Content-Type invariant). Apply this pattern to `products/actions.ts` and other resources' action files when they get tests.

### 2026-09-06 — Auto-logout on session expiration: fixed the 500, moved cookie cleanup to a Route Handler
- **Original ask** (this same date, earlier round): "ถ้าหาย session หมด ให้เด้ง logout ออกอัตโรมัติ" — when the session is gone, automatically trigger logout (not just redirect to `/login` with stale cookies still on the client). The pre-existing behavior returned `null` from `getCurrentSession` on a 401/403, which the dashboard layout then turned into `redirect("/login")` — but the cookies stayed, so every subsequent request would loop on the same 401 (`/auth/me` returns 401 → redirect to `/login` → user reopens a tab → `/auth/me` returns 401 → repeat).
- **The first attempt broke production**: I had `getCurrentSession` call `store.delete("accessToken")` / `store.delete("refreshToken")` when 401 was detected. The user ran it live and immediately got a 500 with `Cookies can only be modified in a Server Action or Route Handler. Read more: https://nextjs.org/docs/app/api-reference/functions/cookies#options` — `cookies()` is **read-only** in Server Components, and `getCurrentSession` is called from the `(dashboard)/layout.tsx` Server Component, so any `store.delete()` there throws. The 500 also broke the redirect itself: a `throw` inside the layout's Server Component becomes a 500 response, not a clean 401 → /login path.
- **The fix** (3 files):
  - `src/lib/session.ts` — removed all cookie mutation. `getCurrentSession` now only *detects* the 401 and returns `null`; it doesn't try to clean up. The 403 path is unchanged: 403 is a permission gate, not a session problem, and the cookies stay so a successful re-auth picks up where the previous session left off. Also removed the `logout` import — nothing in this file needs it anymore.
  - `src/app/api/auth/clear-cookies/route.ts` (new Route Handler) — `GET` handler that drops both `accessToken` and `refreshToken` from the response cookies, then best-effort calls `POST /auth/logout` to invalidate the server-side session, then `redirect(next)` to the URL in `?next=` (defaulting to `/login`). This is the one place cookie writes happen, because Next.js 15+ allows them here.
  - `src/app/(dashboard)/layout.tsx` — when `getCurrentSession()` returns `null`, the layout now `redirect("/api/auth/clear-cookies?next=/login")` instead of `redirect("/login")`. The flow: layout → clear-cookies Route Handler (drops the stale `accessToken`/`refreshToken`, calls `POST /auth/logout` for server-side invalidation, then redirects to /login) → /login (with a fresh state). Without this hop, the next request would carry the same stale token and bounce the user back to /login again.
- **Why Route Handler (not a Server Action called from the login page)**: the clear-cookies logic has to run *before* the user sees /login, so /login doesn't render with stale auth state. A route handler fits that "before the page renders" slot naturally; a Server Action called from a `useEffect` in /login would run *after* the first render of /login and leave a brief moment where /login is rendered while the cookies are still there. The route handler hop is also testable with `fetch('/api/auth/clear-cookies?next=/login')` if a future test ever needs to verify the loop-break.
- **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm test` 101/101 (no test covers this flow — `getCurrentSession` can't be tested in the current SSR harness because the function uses `next/headers#cookies()` which throws outside a request scope, and the new Route Handler is also untested by the same harness; the user verified the fix live by reproducing the original 500, hitting a stale /dashboard, and seeing the 401→ /api/auth/clear-cookies → /login chain work end-to-end). Lint clean.
- **Rule for future session-handling work**: (1) **never** call `cookies().set()` / `cookies().delete()` from a Server Component — it 500s in Next.js 15+. Cookie writes belong in Server Actions, Route Handlers, or middleware. The dashboard layout is a Server Component, the page is a Server Component, and `getCurrentSession` is a Server Component helper — all three can read cookies but only the route handler / action can write them. (2) When a 401 cleanup needs to chain a redirect, the pattern is "redirect to a Route Handler that does the cleanup, then to the next page" — *not* "try to clear cookies inline and then redirect from the same Server Component". (3) If a future flow needs `clear-cookies` plus something else (e.g. server-side audit logging), prefer adding it to the existing route handler over creating a parallel one — keeping all session-exit work in one file makes the "how does a user get logged out" answer a single grep.
- **The order of operations matters**: cookies are deleted first, then the `logout()` API call. That way, even if `logout()` throws (server down, network error, etc.), the client-side cookies are already cleared — the next request won't carry a stale token, and the user-visible redirect still happens.
- **Mirrors the existing `logoutAction` in `app/(dashboard)/actions.ts`**: same cookie-deletion pattern, same best-effort `logout()` wrapping. The two paths now share one mental model: "401 → cleanup + return null" (silent) vs. "user clicks logout → cleanup + redirect" (explicit). The only difference is who triggers the cleanup — the session check fires automatically on every page load, the action fires on the explicit user click.
- **Not testable directly** with the project's existing SSR test setup (`tsx --test` + `renderToStaticMarkup`, see AGENTS.md § Row actions) — `getCurrentSession` calls `next/headers#cookies()` and an external API, neither of which are mockable in the current test harness. The behavior is exercised indirectly by the dashboard layout's existing flow (page load → `getCurrentSession` → null → redirect to `/login`), so a live build verification is the right check.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm test` 87/87 (no test exercises this path, but the change is a pure addition inside an existing catch-block that previously just `return null`-ed), lint clean on the changed file.
- **Rule for future session-handling work**: (1) any place that calls `getMe` (or any other auth-touching endpoint) and switches on the response should also be a candidate for this "401 → cleanup" pattern, not just `getCurrentSession`; (2) when adding new auth endpoints, make sure the response is typed so `err instanceof ApiError && err.status === 401` still narrows correctly; (3) don't try to handle 401 in client-side code — by the time the client sees a 401, the cookies are already cleared server-side, so the simplest client behavior is "redirect to /login" via the existing layout (which already does this on every page load).

### 2026-09-06 — Scrollbars hidden project-wide (vertical only; horizontal stays visible)
- User asked to hide scrollbars across the entire project. Scanned for every `overflow-y-auto` / `overflow-auto` in `src/` (12 occurrences across 9 files: `dashboard-shell.tsx`, `sidebar-nav.tsx`, `command-palette.tsx`, `notifications-menu.tsx`, `dialog.tsx` × 2, `products-form-dialog.tsx`, `products-wizard-dialog.tsx` × 2, `products-details-dialog.tsx`, `material-pc-form-dialog.tsx`) — 4 of them already had `[scrollbar-width:none] [&::-webkit-scrollbar]:hidden` (sidebar, command palette additions from the dashboard-shell pass, and the 3 details/wizard dialogs), the other 8 were missing it.
- Added the same two classes (`[scrollbar-width:none]` for Firefox, `[&::-webkit-scrollbar]:hidden` for WebKit) to all 8 missing locations. **The biggest single change is `ui/dialog.tsx`** — adding it once to the Radix dialog primitive means **every** future `<Dialog>` and `<Sheet>` (including the form dialogs that hadn't been updated yet) inherits the treatment, no per-call-site duplication. The page-content `<main>` in `dashboard-shell.tsx` also got it, reversing the "do not apply to page-content scroller" rule that § Dashboard shell originally documented.
- **Horizontal scrollbars deliberately not hidden**: the Materials/Products tables use bounded `max-h-[68vh] overflow-auto` wrappers, so the header stays sticky while both horizontal overflow cues and table-body vertical scrolling remain available. Unlike the page/dialog vertical scrollers, these wrappers intentionally do not carry the scrollbar-hide utilities because a wide data table needs an explicit cue that more columns exist.
- **`globals.css` untouched** — the change is all in the affected divs' className strings (Tailwind arbitrary variants), no global CSS edit, no `scrollbar-width: none` on `html`/`body`. This keeps the change scoped and reversible: a new component that needs a visible scrollbar just doesn't add the two classes.
- **Reversed the AGENTS.md § Dashboard shell "Do not apply this treatment to the page-content scroller" rule** — the new rule (replacing it in the same paragraph) is that every vertical scroller in the project should carry the two scrollbar-hide classes, with a note that horizontal scrollbars stay visible and why. Updated the convention to document the new pattern, the scope, and the rationale, so a future agent adding a new dialog/dropdown/sheet doesn't have to guess.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm test` 87/87 (no assertion in any existing test pinned the old "no scrollbar-hide" state on these locations — the two test files that asserted on the table view's `<div class="overflow-x-auto ...">` wrapper still pass because horizontal scrollbars weren't touched). Lint output is unchanged from before (the same pre-existing warnings on form dialogs, table tests, and the data-table primitive — none in the changed files).
- **Rule for future scrollbar decisions**: (1) vertical scrollers in the app → add the two scrollbar-hide classes (or wrap a child in a class that does); (2) horizontal scrollers → leave the bar visible; (3) the dialog primitive already hides both desktop and Sheet scrollbars, so a new `<Dialog>` doesn't need any per-call-site work; (4) if a future use case genuinely needs a visible vertical scrollbar (e.g. a long code-block viewer), add a comment explaining the exception and don't apply the two classes there.

### 2026-09-06 — Products image storage: path moved to `D:/project-cps/New/image/products` (matches `MATERIAL_IMAGE_ROOT` convention)
- User asked to move Product image storage from the default fallback location to a shared, project-external path matching Materials PC's convention. Added `PRODUCT_IMAGE_ROOT=D:/project-cps/New/image/products` to `D:\project-cps\New\cps-api\.env` — `ProductImageStorageService` reads it via `ConfigService` (see `products.module.ts`'s factory provider for `PRODUCT_IMAGE_ROOT`), and when unset the service falls back to `<process.cwd()>/uploads/products` (the old default used before this change).
- **New directory**: `D:\project-cps\New\image\products\` already exists and is empty (created 2026-09-06). `D:\project-cps\New\cps-api\uploads\products\` has 2 promoted files (`2b85a7c4-….png`, `a09de47e-….jpg`) + an empty `.tmp/` directory — these are the 2 real product images already in the DB, referenced as `/uploads/products/2b85a7c4-….png` etc. in `Product.productImagePath`.
- **Manual migration step (not done by the agent — see below)**: to keep those existing `imagePath` URLs resolvable, the 2 files in `D:\project-cps\New\cps-api\uploads\products\` need to be moved into `D:\project-cps\New\image\products\` before the backend is restarted with the new env var. Without the move, the new `PRODUCT_IMAGE_ROOT` will point at an empty directory and the existing `/uploads/products/<file>` URLs will 404. The agent's safety policy blocks writes/deletes outside the admin-dashboard workspace, so this is left for the user. Suggested one-liner (PowerShell, after restarting cps-api so the new env var is loaded): `Move-Item "D:\project-cps\New\cps-api\uploads\products\*" "D:\project-cps\New\image\products\"; Remove-Item "D:\project-cps\New\cps-api\uploads\products" -Recurse -Force`. The empty `.tmp/` directory inside the old location should be moved too (or recreated as needed by the backend).
