- **Frontend unchanged**: the `/uploads/:path*` rewrite in `next.config.ts` is generic — it just proxies any `/uploads/...` URL to the API origin, so changing the backend's filesystem location requires zero frontend changes. Same pattern as Materials PC.
- **No new dependencies / no migrations / no DB changes** — the existing `Product.productImagePath` rows are still valid (just point at filesystem files that now live at a different absolute path).
- Verified the new `PRODUCT_IMAGE_ROOT` line is present in the backend's `.env`; the safety policy blocks the file move itself.

### 2026-09-06 — Products: added image upload (create + edit), referencing Materials PC's pattern
- User asked to add image editing to Products, using Materials PC's implementation as the reference ("เพิ่มการแก้ไข รูปภาพ อ้างอิงแบบจาก /materials/pc"). Products previously only *displayed* an already-set `productImagePath` (read-only, in the card/table/details dialog) — there was no upload path at all.
- **Verified the backend route exists and mirrors Materials PC's exactly** before building anything (per this file's own standing rule to check cps-api directly, not assume parity): `cps-api/src/modules/products/product-image-storage.service.ts` + `products.controller.ts`'s `POST /products/images` stages a file and returns `StagedProductImage { imagePath, previewUrl }` — same shape, same 5 MiB / JPEG-PNG-WebP limits, same `/uploads/products/` prefix (covered by the existing generic `/uploads/:path*` rewrite in `next.config.ts` — no config change needed) as Materials PC's `/materials/images`.
- **One real difference from Materials PC, confirmed in the DTOs directly**: `CreateProductDto`/`UpdateProductDto` both declare `productImagePath` **optional** (Materials PC's `imagePath` is required on create — a Materials-specific business rule, not a generic pattern). So `ProductImagePicker` (new, in `products-form-dialog.tsx`) has no `required`/`imageRequired` branching at all — image selection is always optional on both create and edit, unlike `MaterialImagePicker`.
- **Added**: `src/lib/api/products.ts#uploadProductImage()` + `StagedProductImage` type + `productImagePath?: string | null` on `ProductPayload`; `src/app/(dashboard)/products/actions.ts#uploadProductImageAction()` (same shape as `uploadMaterialPcImageAction`, narrowed `errorResult()`'s return type to `ProductActionFailure` so the upload action's catch-block message access type-checks); `ProductImagePicker`/`validateProductImage()` in `products-form-dialog.tsx` — a direct structural copy of `MaterialImagePicker`/`validateMaterialImage()`, with the required-ness stripped out per the point above. Wired into `onSubmit`: stages the file first (if one was selected), then sends the resulting path as `productImagePath` on create, or omits the field entirely (not `null`) on update when no replacement was chosen — same "existing image survives an edit with no new file" behavior as Materials PC's `materialImageUpdateFields()`, just inlined here since Products only has the one image field to worry about (no `NONE`/lookup-id ambiguity to also handle).
- Fixed two now-stale comments left over from before this change: `products-details-dialog.tsx`'s hero-image comment ("upload stays out of scope") and the Conventions § Products bullet describing the card's image as read-only-only.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (84/84, unaffected — no test exercises the form dialog's upload flow yet), `next build` all pass. Not click-tested live in a browser this pass (same environment limitation as the previous Products redesign entry) — worth uploading a real image through `/products`'s create and edit dialogs before relying on this in prod.
- **Rule for future image-upload work on a new resource**: always read that resource's own DTOs before assuming Materials PC's "required on create" behavior applies — it's a Materials-specific rule, not a generic pattern, and Products is proof a sibling resource can legitimately make the same field optional.

### 2026-09-06 — Products (`/products`, `/products/list`) redesigned to match Materials PC's current pattern
- User asked to bring Products' display in line with Materials PC ("ให้ปรับการแสดงให้เป็นแนวเดียวกับ /materials/pc"). Materials PC had gone through several rounds since Products was last touched (Table / Editorial card with a Data Sheet / Compact Row, a Switch for active/inactive, essentials-inline + a "view full record" details dialog, RowActionsMenu always offering "ดูรายละเอียด") — Products still had its original two-view (table/card) design with a `ProductCardFact` bento grid and a visible "ปิดใช้งาน" button. This pass rewrote `products-table.tsx` to mirror `material-pc-table.tsx` structurally, field-for-field where a Products equivalent exists.
- **New `list` view mode** added (`ProductListItem`, mirrors `MaterialListItem`): 44/56px photo, code+status, name, `productType · model · customer` facts line, right-aligned Safety/Min stat, `RowActionsMenu`. `ViewToggle` in `products-client.tsx` now passes `modes={["table", "card", "list"]}` (default view stays `"table"`, unlike Materials PC's `"card"` default — no reason given to change Products' existing default, only asked to match the *display*, not necessarily the default choice).
- **Editorial card rebuilt**: 4:3 photo hero (was a `size-16` square thumbnail) with the status badge as an overlay (was inline next to the name), a toggle `Switch` for active/inactive under the name (was a visible "ปิดใช้งาน"/"เปิดใช้งาน" outline button — same confirm-before-toggle wiring as Materials PC: `onCheckedChange` still calls `onToggleStatus`, which still opens `ProductsStatusDialog`), Safety/Min Stock as a 2-column stat block in the position Materials PC's single stock number occupies, a real `<table>` **Data Sheet** (was the `ProductCardFact` bento `dl` grid) for 6 essential fields, and a 2-button action row (`ดูรายละเอียด` outline + `แก้ไข` solid primary — no more separate disable button, matching Materials PC's button-color-hierarchy fix).
- **Field split, mirroring Materials PC's essentials-inline / full-record-in-dialog principle**: Table, card Data Sheet, and the Compact Row's facts line all show only ประเภทสินค้า/สถานที่/ลูกค้า/รุ่น/หน่วย/ประเภทการจัดส่ง — จุดขึ้นสินค้า/สายการผลิต/มาตราส่วน/แพ็ก/ล็อต are dialog-only. Unlike Materials PC (whose dialog's extra content is prose specification/description, so its Data Sheet repeats the same 6 fields as the card), Products has no prose fields, so `ProductsDetailsDialog`'s Data Sheet is a genuinely **longer** 11-row table (adds the 5 dialog-only fields) — the dialog earns its "full record" name by actually showing more, not just the same 6 again.
- **Added `products-details-dialog.tsx`** (`ProductsDetailsDialog`/`ProductsDetailsView`, exported the inner view for the same Portal-can't-SSR reason documented for Materials PC) and **`products-image-preview.tsx`** (`ProductsImagePreview`) — both direct structural copies of their Materials PC counterparts, adjusted for `Product`'s fields (no stock-balance section in the details dialog; Safety/Min Stock sit in the hero instead, since Products has no live stock-on-hand concept — see the existing "Products (CRUD)" convention entry).
- **`getProductRowActions` gained the same optional `onViewDetails` handler** as `getMaterialRowActions` — prepends "ดูรายละเอียด" unconditionally (not permission-gated) when passed, omitted when not (backward compatible with any caller that doesn't pass it). `RowActionsMenu` in Table and Compact Row therefore is never fully hidden anymore, even with no edit/delete permission — same intentional behavior change as Materials PC's equivalent pass.
- Every one of the label-formatting helpers (`productTypeLabel`, `locationLabel`, `customerLabel`, `modelLabel`, `unitLabel`, `deliveryLabel`, `loadingPointLabel`, `processLineLabel`, `scaleLabel`, `packingLabel`, `lotSizeLabel`) is now exported from `products-table.tsx`, mirroring Materials PC's exported label functions — the details dialog imports them directly so formatting can't drift between the two.
- Test file rewritten to match: Switch-based assertions (`role="switch"`, `data-state`, "ใช้งานอยู่"/"ไม่ได้ใช้งาน") replace the old "ปิดใช้งาน" button-text assertions; the "no `<table>` in card view" assertion was narrowed to "no table-view chrome" (the card's own Data Sheet is a real `<table>`, same fix Materials PC's suite needed); added list-view tests and `getProductRowActions`'s `onViewDetails` pure-function tests, mirroring Materials PC's equivalents exactly. 84/84 tests pass.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (84/84), `next build` all pass. Not yet click-tested live in a browser this pass (only checked via a build + an unauthenticated route smoke-test) — worth a manual look before relying on this in prod.
- **Rule for future Products/Materials PC parity work**: these two resources' card/list/table renderers are now meant to be kept in the same shape deliberately (this is now the second time one was rebuilt to match the other) — a future visual change to one should default to also updating the other for consistency, unless the request explicitly scopes to just one resource.

### 2026-09-06 — Project-wide cleanup pass (dead code, stray file, unused deps, stale docs)
- User asked for a structural review of the project ("ตรวจดูโครงสร้างโปรเจค ว่ายังมีจุดไหนที่ต้องแก้ไข"), then to fix what was found. Findings and fixes:
  - **`material-pc-details-dialog.tsx`'s `DataSheetTable` had a `singleColumn` prop that was declared but never read inside the component, and never passed by its one call site** — a real lint warning (`no-unused-vars`). Removed the prop entirely rather than wiring it up, since nothing in the current (Round 6) design needs a single-column variant.
  - **Three exported label helpers in `material-pc-table.tsx` had gone unused**: `scaleLabel`, `packingLabel` (มาตราส่วน/จำนวนต่อแพ็ก were part of an earlier "every field" pass but dropped when the details dialog was redesigned down to its current 6-field Data Sheet — see the Round 6 entry below), and `supplierCountLabel` (a count-only variant made obsolete once every view switched to showing full supplier names). Deleted all three rather than reintroducing fields the current dialog design deliberately omits.
  - **Removed a stray `material-card-redesign.html`** (~140KB static mockup, untracked, sitting at the repo root with a "Round 4" comment) — a design-exploration artifact that was never part of the app. Moved to a `%TEMP%` backup rather than hard-deleted.
  - **Removed two unused dependencies from `package.json`**: `@radix-ui/react-popover` (no `ui/popover.tsx` exists, zero imports anywhere) and `date-fns` (zero imports anywhere). Ran `pnpm install` to sync `pnpm-lock.yaml`, and removed the now-stale "Dates → `date-fns`" row from the Stack table above.
  - **Fixed Project Structure drift**: the materials-pc file tree above was missing `material-pc-details-dialog.tsx` entirely, despite it being referenced extensively in Conventions and Recent Changes — a violation of this file's own R2 rule. Added it back.
- **Flagged, not fixed**: nothing in this session had been committed yet at review time (11 modified + several new files sitting uncommitted since `48ee163`) — a workflow risk (no checkpoint to fall back to), not a code defect, and committing is the user's call, not something to do unprompted.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, back down to the 6 pre-existing warnings — the `singleColumn` warning is gone), `pnpm test` (78/78), `next build`, and `pnpm install` (confirms only the 2 intended packages were removed) all pass.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC details dialog: Hero + Data Sheet (single 2-col table for all 6 fields, stock inline, activity as a footer line)
- User asked to redesign the details dialog again (Round 6). The previous design (Round 5) had 4 separate sectioned grids (ข้อมูลพื้นฐาน / การจัดส่ง / ซัพพลายเออร์ / รายละเอียดเพิ่มเติม) plus a separate stock card and a separate activity card — visually rich but felt like 3 separate UI surfaces fighting for the same space. This pass collapses them into one consistent flow that matches the card's Data Sheet pattern.
- **Image bumped from 280×210 → 320×240** (still 4:3, still `object-contain`) so material photo details are easier to scan at the dialog's natural `sm:` width.
- **Stock moved from a separate "stats card" into the hero**, right under the identity block (code, name 22px, status + shape badges). Tone treatment stays the same: `text-warning` for 1-9, `text-fg-muted` for 0, `text-fg` for 10+; health badge sits inline next to the unit. When `stockByMaterialId` is null, the entire stock row collapses to a small "ไม่มีสิทธิ์ดูสต็อก" muted line — no orphaned stock card.
- **"ความเคลื่อนไหว" card removed → single inline `<p>` at the bottom**: เพิ่มเมื่อ <date> · แก้ไขล่าสุด <date>. Activity timestamps are reference info, not something to scan repeatedly; giving them their own bordered card competed with the more important data above for the user's eye.
- **4 separate sectioned grids → 1 unified "ข้อมูลจำเพาะ" 2-col table** with all 6 essential fields in one continuous list. The dialog and the card now show the **same `DataSheetRow` / same label / same value formatting** — a user moving from card to dialog doesn't re-learn the layout. จุดขึ้นสินค้า and สายการผลิต get `fullWidth` so they take the whole row instead of being squeezed into a half-width cell (their values are often long: "โรงงานบางปะอิน" / "สาย PC-01").
- **Suppliers: pills (Round 5) → comma-joined in the data table (Round 6)**. Round 5 had per-supplier `<Badge>` pills in a dedicated "ซัพพลายเออร์" section. The new design uses the same `supplierNames(material)` helper that the table view and the card already use — "CPS Steel, ABC Corp" — keeping all three views formatted identically. Pills lost a "scan each supplier independently" affordance but the data isn't lost (it still reads in full, just joined).
- 14 dialog tests updated: image dimensions 320×240 (was 280×210), no separate stock card (asserts no `bg-primary-soft rounded-lg border p-4` pattern), no "ความเคลื่อนไหว" heading (asserts the inline `<p>` form), all 6 fields in one `ข้อมูลจำเพาะ` table, suppliers joined (no per-supplier `<li>` pills). All 78 tests pass; `tsc --noEmit` and `pnpm lint` clean.
- **Rule for future details-dialog work**: the dialog and the card must use the **same** `DataSheetRow` helper for shared key-value formatting. If a future field needs different cell treatment in the dialog vs the card (e.g. dialog shows a unit conversion, card shows the raw value), the helper needs an optional prop (don't fork the markup) — duplicate `DataSheetRow`-shaped JSX in both files is a refactor trap.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC details dialog: hero layout, sectioned data, Edit button
- User asked to redesign the `MaterialPcDetailsDialog` (the "ดูรายละเอียด" modal opened from each row's actions). The previous version was a flat, dense modal that put everything on one screen with no visual hierarchy — 80px photo, single stock line, an 8-cell 2×2 facts grid, optional spec/description, and timestamps at the bottom.
- **New layout (`size="xl"` = 896px)** has four distinct zones, each with its own role:
  1. **Hero header**: 4:3 image (280×210 on sm+) on the left + identity stack on the right — code eyebrow, 20px name, status badge + shape pill. Magazine-cover composition; image stays `object-contain` so a material photo's technical reference value isn't cropped.
  2. **Stats row** (two cards side by side): **stock card** with the same `getStockTone` heuristic as the card view (warning/muted/primary backgrounds; 32px tabular-nums hero number; health badge below) + **activity card** showing `แก้ไขล่าสุด` and `เพิ่มเมื่อ` (Buddhist-Era Thai dates from `toLocaleString("th-TH")`). Stock card falls back to a small "ไม่มีสิทธิ์ดูข้อมูลสต็อก" placeholder when `stockByMaterialId` is null (same permission gate as the card view).
  3. **Sectioned data** with uppercase eyebrow labels: `ข้อมูลพื้นฐาน` (รุ่น, หน่วย, มาตราส่วน, จำนวน/แพ็ก), `การจัดส่ง` (ประเภท, จุดขึ้น, สายการผลิต), `ซัพพลายเออร์` (one `<Badge>` per supplier — pills, not a comma-joined list since the dialog has room), `รายละเอียดเพิ่มเติม` (ข้อมูลจำเพาะ + รายละเอียด, only when at least one is non-empty, `whitespace-pre-line` so newlines render). Each section's `<dl>` uses the same bordered-cell technique as the card and Products views.
  4. **Footer**: ปิด (always) + แก้ไข (only when `canEdit && onEdit` — closes the dialog and calls the same `onEdit` handler the row's "แก้ไข" action uses, so the existing `MaterialPcFormDialog` opens). Removes a "close the dialog, find the row, open its action menu, click Edit" detour.
- **Inner view extracted as `MaterialPcDetailsView`** so SSR tests can render the content directly. The Radix Dialog wrapper renders through a Portal which `renderToStaticMarkup` can't capture (same Portal-capture limitation noted in § Row actions for `DropdownMenu`); the wrapper itself is a thin shell (`<Dialog open>` + `<DialogContent>` + the view), so testing the view covers the layout. Public `MaterialPcDetailsDialog` is exercised by the live app / `next build`.
- `getStockTone` + `getStockHealthLabel` are now **exported** from `material-pc-table.tsx` (was module-local) so the dialog can use the exact same tone logic as the card/list views — single source of truth for the 0/muted, 1-9/warning, 10+/primary heuristic. If cps-api ships a real threshold, both call sites update from the same function.
- `package.json` `test` script now includes `src/components/materials-pc/material-pc-details-dialog.test.tsx` (the project's test runner is an explicit file list, not a glob — add new files there).
- 14 new tests cover: hero header content, 4:3 + 280×210 image dimensions, image fallback a11y, stock card with quantity/unit/health badge, low-stock + zero-stock tone treatment (warning-soft + muted backgrounds), permission-gate fallback, all four data sections rendering, per-supplier pills (not joined), spec/description conditional render, `whitespace-pre-line` preserved, activity card timestamps (asserts Buddhist Era 2569 OR CE 2026 — locale output is environment-dependent), footer Close-only vs Close+Edit by `canEdit`.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `pnpm test` 77/77 (was 63/63 before this pass; +14 dialog tests), `pnpm lint` 0 errors on the changed files.
- **Rule for future details-dialog work**: any new field added to `Material` should (a) get its label helper in `material-pc-table.tsx` (the shared formatting location), (b) get added to one of the dialog's existing section grids if it belongs to "ข้อมูลพื้นฐาน" or "การจัดส่ง", or get its own new section with an `h3` eyebrow + `dl` (one per logical grouping — don't mix unrelated fields into one section).

### 2026-09-05 — Navigation menu redesigned: borderless rows, filled active state (desktop + mobile)
- Follow-up to the entry below: that pass only restyled the **mobile Sheet panel's chrome**, which is invisible at `lg`+ — the user was looking at the desktop sidebar and correctly reported "เมนูยังเหมือนเดิม ไม่มีการ redesign". The menu *items* live in `layout/sidebar-nav.tsx`, which is shared by the desktop `Sidebar` **and** the mobile Sheet, so this pass changes both at once.
- **What the old design was**: every row was a bordered chip (`rounded-lg border`) containing a *second* bordered icon tile (`size-7 rounded-md border`), with nested rows marked by a small bullet dot. On a 20-item cps-api menu that reads as 20 competing cards, with no single row standing out as "you are here".
- **New design**: borderless rows carrying state through fill + text color only — `rowBase`/`rowTone()`/`iconTone()` helpers shared by all three row renderers (static link, tree link, tree toggle button) so they can't drift:
  - **active leaf → solid `bg-primary` with inverted `text-primary-fg`** (the one unmistakable row; its chevron gets a matching `text-primary-fg/80` + `hover:bg-white/15` treatment so it stays legible on the filled row),
  - **open parent → `bg-surface-2` + full-strength `text-fg`**, **idle → transparent + `text-fg-secondary`, `hover:bg-surface-2`**.
  - Icons are now inline `size-[18px]` at `strokeWidth={1.75}` (no tile box); nested rows drop the icon entirely (indent + guide rail already communicate nesting) and mark active with a 2px primary accent bar on the guide line instead of a bullet dot.
  - Tighter rhythm: `text-[13px]`, `min-h-9` top level / `min-h-8` nested, `gap-0.5` between rows (was `gap-1`), nested block `mt-0.5`.
  - Section labels restyled (`text-[10px]`, `tracking-[0.12em]`, more space below), and the bottom "ทั่วไป" group gained a `border-t` so account-level items read as a separate tier from the permission-driven tree.
- **Behavior untouched**: recursive rendering of cps-api's real menu tree at any depth, `BUTTON`-type filtering, auto-expand of the active chain (`autoOpenIds`, still derived per render — no effect), manual open/close `overrides`, `onNavigate` closing the mobile Sheet, the empty-permissions message, collapsed icon-only mode (rows become `h-9 justify-center px-0`, and every collapsed row keeps its `aria-label` + `title`), and `SidebarNav`'s hidden-scrollbar treatment.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `next build` all pass, and the compiled CSS chunk contains the new `letter-spacing:.12em` section-label rule, confirming the styles actually shipped (not just edited in source).

### 2026-09-05 — Mobile slide-menu redesigned as a floating panel (behavior unchanged)
- User asked to redesign the slide-out mobile nav ("slide-menu") while keeping it working exactly as before. That's the Radix-Dialog-based `Sheet` in `src/components/ui/sheet.tsx`, rendered below `lg` from `layout/topbar.tsx` — its only consumer in the app.
- **`SheetContent` is now a floating rounded panel** instead of a flush edge-to-edge drawer: inset from the viewport (`inset-y-2 left-2`, `sm:inset-y-3 sm:left-3`), `rounded-2xl` with a full border on all sides (was `border-r` only), `shadow-2xl`, `w-[17rem]` with a `max-w-[calc(100vw-1rem)]` clamp for very narrow screens. This matches the dashboard's existing floating-box shell language (see Conventions § Dashboard shell) rather than looking like a separate, older pattern. **`h-full` was deliberately removed** — height now derives from the top/bottom insets; keeping `h-full` would resolve to the full viewport height and overflow past the bottom inset (same class of box-model trap as the `inset-1/2` dialog bug documented further down this file).
- `SheetOverlay` gained `backdrop-blur-[2px]` to match `dialog.tsx`'s overlay, so the app's two scrim surfaces read as the same material. Close button restyled to match the dialog's (hover color + focus ring).
- **Added a required-by-Radix accessible name**: `SheetContent` now renders a `sr-only` `DialogPrimitive.Title` (new optional `title` prop, default `"เมนูนำทาง"`). Radix Dialog warns when content has no Title; the Sheet never had one. Visually invisible, so no design impact.
- `topbar.tsx` simplified: since the panel itself is now the floating rounded surface, it no longer wraps a *second* rounded bordered box inside it (`p-2 sm:p-3` + inner `rounded-xl border`). It now renders the same structure as the desktop `Sidebar` — an `h-16` logo header with `border-b`, then the shared `SidebarNav` — with `pr-14` on the header so the absolutely-positioned close button doesn't collide with the Logo.
- **Behavior verified unchanged**: same `Sheet`/`SheetTrigger` primitives, same `mobileNavOpen` state, same trigger button + `aria-label`, same `SidebarNav` with `onNavigate` closing the sheet after a route is picked. `SidebarNav`'s root is already `flex min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto`, so it still scrolls correctly as a flex child of the panel. **`SidebarNav` itself was not touched**, so the desktop sidebar is byte-for-byte unaffected.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `next build` all pass. Note: at the time of this change `pnpm test` had 14 unrelated failures in a concurrently-added, still-in-progress `material-pc-details-dialog.test.tsx` (`ReferenceError: MaterialPcDetailsDialog is not defined` — the file is missing its import); every other test passed and nothing in this change is covered by or affects them.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC: every view now shows every field
- User asked for all three views (Table, Editorial card, Compact Row) to show full field detail, not a curated subset. Before this change, `รุ่น`/`ประเภทการจัดส่ง`/`จุดขึ้นสินค้า`/`สายการผลิต`/`มาตราส่วน`/`จำนวนต่อแพ็ก`/`ข้อมูลจำเพาะ`/`รายละเอียด` were only ever shown in the Editorial card (and even there, only 3 of the 8), never in Table or Compact Row; stock quantity was shown in Editorial/Compact but not Table at all.
- Added one pure label-formatting function per field in `material-pc-table.tsx` (`shapeLabel`, `unitLabel`, `modelLabel`, `deliveryLabel`, `loadingPointLabel`, `processLineLabel`, `scaleLabel`, `packingLabel`, `specificationLabel`, `descriptionLabel` — alongside the existing `supplierNames`) so all three renderers format each field identically instead of each hand-rolling its own `?? "—"` fallback.
- **Table**: grew from 8 to 15 columns — added รุ่น, ประเภทการจัดส่ง, จุดขึ้นสินค้า, สายการผลิต, มาตราส่วน, จำนวนต่อแพ็ก, คงเหลือ (conditional on `stockByMaterialId`, same permission gate as the other views), ข้อมูลจำเพาะ, รายละเอียด. The table already scrolls horizontally (`overflow-x-auto`), so the extra columns don't break layout — specification/description use `max-w-48 truncate` + `title` since they're free-text and could be long.
- **Editorial card**: facts grid grew from 3 items (รุ่น/หน่วย/ส่ง) to 7 (added จุดขึ้นสินค้า, สายการผลิต, มาตราส่วน, จำนวนต่อแพ็ก). A new conditional section below the facts grid renders ข้อมูลจำเพาะ/รายละเอียด as full `whitespace-pre-line` paragraphs (not truncated — these are prose fields, not short labels) when either is non-empty.
- **Compact Row**: the meta line was a single `truncate`d row (materialType/ratio · model · suppliers, with suppliers hidden below `sm:`). Changed to `flex-wrap` (nothing hidden or ellipsized) covering all 9 short fields (shape, unit, model, delivery, loading point, process line, scale, packing, suppliers), plus a new 2-line block below for specification/description (still `truncate`+`title` per line, since a compact list row can't reasonably show full prose inline — the full text is one hover/focus away).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (58/58, unaffected — no test asserted a fixed column/field count), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future Materials PC field changes**: if `Material` gains a new field, add its label function next to the others in this file and wire it into all three renderers in the same change — the whole point of this pass was "every view, every field," so a field added to only one view going forward would silently regress that.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC Editorial card: active/inactive is now a Switch, clearer button color hierarchy, bordered facts grid
- User feedback (typed in a mismatched keyboard layout — "ฟแะรอำ"/"รืฟแะรอำ" decode to "active"/"inactive" one-for-one under the Thai Kedmanee layout's key positions): (1) button colors still didn't communicate meaning, and the active/inactive control should become a toggle switch; (2) the card was still hard to read with no clear focal point.
- **Active/inactive is now a `Switch`** (`@radix-ui/react-switch`, already in Stack as `src/components/ui/switch.tsx`, previously unused by Materials PC) instead of a "ปิดใช้งาน"/"เปิดใช้งาน" outline button. Placed in the header under the name/subtitle (wrapped in a `<label>` with a "ใช้งานอยู่"/"ไม่ได้ใช้งาน" text span — labelable-element semantics mean clicking the label text also activates the nested Switch button), shown only when `canDelete`. **Still goes through the existing confirm-before-toggle flow** — `onCheckedChange` calls the same `onToggleStatus(material)` that opened `MaterialPcStatusDialog` before; the Switch is a controlled component (`checked={material.isActive}`), so if the user cancels the confirmation, the Switch visually reverts on its own since the underlying prop never changed. The read-only status Badge on the photo overlay is unchanged and still the only status indicator when the viewer lacks `MATERIAL_DELETE`.
- **Action row is down to 2 buttons, with an actual color hierarchy**: "ดูรายละเอียด" is now plain `variant="outline"` (neutral, was `variant="secondary"`) and "แก้ไข" is now solid `variant="primary"` (was an outline with a primary tint) — one clearly "the" action (solid brand color) vs. one clearly secondary/informational (plain border), instead of three buttons whose outline-tint colors (gray/blue/red-or-green) required memorizing what each one meant.
- **Facts grid reverted from a responsive 2→4-column strip to a fixed 2-column bordered grid** (same bordered-cell technique as `MaterialPcDetailsDialog`/the Products card: `[&>*]:border-border [&>*:not(:nth-last-child(-n+2))]:border-b [&>*:nth-child(odd)]:border-r`) — compartmentalized cells give the eye a clear place to land, addressing the "don't know where to focus" complaint; fixed-2-column also sidesteps a border-math bug the old responsive version would have hit once the extra fields were trimmed back to 4 items (the `nth-last-child`/`nth-child` selectors assume a specific column count, so a column count that changes by breakpoint needs matching breakpoint-specific border rules, which the codebase doesn't currently have — simplest correct fix was to stop varying the column count).
- Header block gained a `pb-1` (was `pt-[18px]` only, no bottom padding) so the new conditional Switch row has consistent breathing room before the stock section that follows it, regardless of whether the Switch renders.
- **Scoped to the Editorial card only** — Table and Compact Row keep "ปิดใช้งาน"/"เปิดใช้งาน" as a `RowActionsMenu` (Meatballs) item, since a toggle switch doesn't fit naturally inside a dropdown menu item the way it does as a standalone card control. Ask if the same Switch treatment should extend there too (would mean moving that action out of the menu into visible row UI).
- Test changes in `material-pc-table.test.tsx`: card-view tests updated to check for `role="switch"`/`data-state`/the "ใช้งานอยู่"/"ไม่ได้ใช้งาน" text instead of a "ปิดใช้งาน"/"เปิดใช้งาน" button; the xl-breakpoint facts-grid assertion was split into its own test asserting the grid does **not** widen at `xl:` anymore (intentional, not a regression). 62/62 tests pass.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (62/62), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future status-toggle UI**: prefer a `Switch` over a colored disable/enable button wherever there's room for one (a card, a detail panel) — the on/off shape is self-explanatory without relying on a red/green (or worse, ambiguous outline-tint) color convention. Keep it wired through the same confirm-dialog handler as before; don't let "it's now a switch" become an excuse to skip the confirmation step for a destructive-leaning toggle.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC Editorial card: fixed button overflow + bumped low-contrast text
- User reported the 3-button action row (added in the previous entry) still overflowed the card edge ("ปุ่มยังตกขอบ") on narrow cards, and asked for more legible text colors.
- **Button overflow root cause**: `Button` is `inline-flex` with `whitespace-nowrap` and no `overflow-hidden`; its icon and label are flex-item children that default to `min-width: auto`, which blocks them from shrinking below their natural content size. Three `flex-1` buttons ("ดูรายละเอียด" + แก้ไข + ปิดใช้งาน/เปิดใช้งาน) in one row could each need more width than a narrow card had to give, and nothing let them shrink — the label ran past the button/card boundary instead of wrapping or eliding. Fixed by adding `min-w-0` to each `Button` and wrapping each label in `<span className="min-w-0 truncate">` (icons get `shrink-0` so only the text ellipsizes), plus `px-2` (was the `sm` size default `px-3`) to reclaim a little width. Applies to all three buttons in `MaterialEditorialCard`'s action row.
- **Text contrast**: bumped several `text-fg-muted` uses inside the Editorial card that carry real information (not decorative) to `text-fg-secondary`/`text-fg` — the code eyebrow, the materialType/ratio subtitle, `MaterialEditorialMetaItem`'s field labels (now `fg-secondary` + `font-semibold`) and values (now full-strength `fg`, was `fg-secondary`), and the stock number/eyebrow/unit suffix when stock is zero (`tone === "muted"`). This mirrors the WCAG audit's earlier finding that `fg-muted` computes to only ~3.1:1 on `bg-surface` (below the 4.5:1 minimum) — scoped to this card's own classNames, **not** a change to the shared `--fg-muted` token itself (that stays a separate, app-wide decision per the audit entry above; ask before touching `globals.css`).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (60/60, unaffected — pure styling/layout, the `แก้ไข`/`ปิดใช้งาน` regex assertions still match since they just look for the text between any `>...<`, regardless of the new wrapping `<span>`), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future button rows with 2+ flex-1 buttons and icon+label content**: always pair `flex-1` with `min-w-0` on the `Button` and wrap the label in a `min-w-0 truncate` span — `flex-1` alone does not let a flex item shrink past its content's natural width, which is exactly how this overflow happened.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC: essentials-only views + a shared "view full details" dialog
- Reversed the previous "every view shows every field" pass (see the entry below) — user asked for the opposite: every view should show only essential info, with a button to see the complete record. Read "card" broadly (every item's presentation across Table/Editorial/Compact Row, not only the literal Card view mode), since the ask's parenthetical explicitly said the button belongs in every display type — a partial revert (trim only the Editorial card) would have left Table at 15 columns and List's wrapped meta line, which defeats the same decluttering goal.
- **Reverted Table, Editorial card, and Compact Row back to their essential fields** (same set as right after the "show every supplier's name" change, before the field-explosion pass): Table is code/name/shape/unit/suppliers/stock/status/actions (8-9 columns, was 15); Editorial card's facts grid is ซัพพลายเออร์/รุ่น/หน่วย/ประเภทการจัดส่ง (4 items, was 8, and the specification/description block was removed from the card entirely); Compact Row's meta line is shape · model · suppliers again (was a `flex-wrap` line of 9 fields plus a 2-line spec/description block).
- **Added `src/components/materials-pc/material-pc-details-dialog.tsx#MaterialPcDetailsDialog`** — one read-only dialog showing literally every field (image, status, shape, unit, model, delivery type, loading point, process line, scale, packing quantity, full supplier names, specification, description, stock if permitted, created/updated timestamps). Imports the label-formatting helpers (`shapeLabel`, `unitLabel`, `modelLabel`, `deliveryLabel`, `loadingPointLabel`, `processLineLabel`, `scaleLabel`, `packingLabel`, `specificationLabel`, `descriptionLabel`, `supplierNames`) which stayed exported from `material-pc-table.tsx` rather than being deleted, since the dialog needed identical formatting to what the inline views had briefly shown.
- **`getMaterialRowActions` gained an optional `onViewDetails` handler** — when passed, a "ดูรายละเอียด" (Eye icon) action is prepended to the action list *unconditionally*, ahead of the permission-gated Edit/Disable entries; when omitted (existing tests that only exercise edit/disable), behavior is unchanged. This means the Table and Compact Row's `RowActionsMenu` trigger is no longer omitted for a viewer with neither `MATERIAL_UPDATE` nor `MATERIAL_DELETE` — they can still open it to view details, which the trigger's `RowActionsMenu` correctly stops hiding once the action list is non-empty.
- **Editorial card**: a single unified action row now holds up to three buttons — "ดูรายละเอียด" (`variant="secondary"`, always shown, not permission-gated) followed by the existing permission-gated Edit (primary-tinted outline) and Disable/Enable (danger/success-tinted outline). This replaced an initial version that put "ดูรายละเอียด" in its own row above a separately bordered Edit/Disable row — merged into one row per a same-day follow-up ("ปรับ design ปุ่มใหม่") for a cleaner, single action bar instead of two visually separate strips.
- **`MaterialPcClient`** gained `detailsTarget` state and renders `MaterialPcDetailsDialog`, wiring `onViewDetails` through `MaterialPcTable` → `MaterialPcCollection` → each of the three row renderers (now a required field on `MaterialCollectionProps`/`MaterialRowProps`).
- Test changes: `material-pc-table.test.tsx`'s ~21 `<MaterialPcCollection>` call sites gained `onViewDetails={() => undefined}` (now a required prop); the list-view "omits the trigger when no actions are permitted" test was rewritten to "still shows the trigger" (behavior intentionally changed — view details isn't permission-gated); two new pure-function tests cover `getMaterialRowActions`'s `onViewDetails` behavior (prepended unconditionally; omitted when the handler isn't passed, for backward compatibility). 60/60 tests pass.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (60/60), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future Materials PC view work**: keep the "essentials inline, full record in the dialog" split — if a new field is added to `Material`, add its label helper and put it in `MaterialPcDetailsDialog` by default; only add it to an inline view's essentials if there's a specific reason a user needs it at a glance, not just for completeness (that's what regressed into the field-explosion this entry reverts).

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC: show every supplier's name, not just a count
- All three views (Table, Editorial card, Compact Row — see the 3-way view entry below) showed only `material.suppliers.length` (table: bare number; editorial card: "N ราย"; list row: "N ซัพฯ") — anyone checking sourcing had to open the edit dialog to see which suppliers a material actually has. User asked to show them all.
- Added `supplierNames(material)` in `material-pc-table.tsx` (joins `nameEn ?? nameTh ?? code` for every supplier, `"—"` when none) and used it in all three places: the table's ซัพพลายเออร์ column (wraps naturally — the table has room, no truncation needed), the Editorial card's supplier meta cell (now spans the full meta-grid row via a new `MaterialEditorialMetaItem` `className` prop, since names no longer fit the narrow per-field column — still `truncate`+`title` for overflow beyond the row width), and the Compact Row's supplier segment (`truncate`+`title`).
- Also restored the pagination `aria-label`s ("หน้าก่อนหน้า"/"หน้าถัดไป", see the WCAG audit entry below) that had been dropped when this file was independently restructured into the 3-way view since that audit pass.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `pnpm test` (58/58), `next build` all pass.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC Editorial card: 1 row × 4 cards at xl viewport
- User asked for the Editorial card to display "1 แถว 4 card" (1 row × 4 cards). Changed `MaterialPcCollection`'s card `<ul>` from `@min-[32rem]:grid-cols-2` to a 1 → 2 → 4 container-query grid: `@min-[40rem]:grid-cols-2 @min-[80rem]:grid-cols-4`. The intermediate 2-col stop (40rem ≈ 640px) keeps the jump from 1 to 4 from being too aggressive on tablet/landscape sizes — cards stay readable on each step. The 4-col stop is 80rem (≈ 1280px) to match the page's `max-w-1280px` container, so on any wider viewport the cards stay at 4 across (≈ 295px each at the container's full width) instead of stretching thin. `gap-4` → `gap-3` so four cards in a row don't feel over-spaced.
- The Editorial card's 4-column meta strip also widens from 2 to 4 columns at the same `xl` breakpoint (`grid-cols-2 xl:grid-cols-4` on the meta `<dl>`) — otherwise the 4-col meta would be only ~55px per cell at the 4-card width, which is too tight for "AC-5C" / "จัดส่งถึงไซต์" without truncating; at 2-col meta on narrower cards each cell is ~140px which fits comfortably.
- New regression test: `card view lays out 4 cards per row at the xl breakpoint (80rem container query)` — asserts both the container-query class and the meta strip's 4-col class are in the rendered markup, so a future "let's just do 2 across" change has to update the test on purpose. All 26 material-pc-table tests pass; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm lint` 0 errors (no new warnings on the changed files).
- **Rule for future Materials PC grid changes**: any change to the Editorial card's column count must keep the container-query breakpoint in sync with the meta strip's column count (4 cards → 4-col meta; 2 cards → 2-col meta) — these are a pair, not independent. If a future request asks for "3 cards per row", change both stops together.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC: 3-way view (Editorial card + Compact Row + Table) — `ViewMode` extended to `"list"`
- User asked to redesign the material card "ให้ดูง่าย สมดุล" (easy to look at, balanced), then asked for another round after the first proposal. Final direction was a 3-way view toggle combining the two strongest options: **Editorial** (image-led 4:3 hero card with 32px stock number as data hero) as the new default card view, and a new **Compact Row** list view for scanning 50+ materials at once, alongside the existing table view.
- **`ViewMode` type widened** from `"table" | "card"` to `"table" | "card" | "list"` in `src/hooks/use-view-mode.ts` (additive — every existing call site that only checks `view === "table"` still type-checks; an unknown value falls through to the card branch harmlessly). `useViewMode`'s snapshot validation now accepts the new mode; old `"list"` values that couldn't have been stored pre-change are not a concern, but the new branch is still defensive.
- **`ViewToggle` now takes a `modes: readonly ViewMode[]` prop** (default `["table", "card"]`) — adding a new mode to the type doesn't silently add a button to every consumer. Materials PC passes `modes={["table", "card", "list"]}` to enable the 3-way toggle; Products keeps the default and stays unaffected. Per-mode label + icon are centralized in a `MODE_CONFIG` map (lucide's `Table2`/`LayoutGrid`/`Rows3` icons, Thai tooltips) so adding a future mode is one entry.
- **`MaterialPcCollection` now branches into 3 renderers** (not 2): the existing `<Table>` cell renderer is unchanged, the existing `view === "card"` body is replaced by `MaterialEditorialCard` (image-led design, see below), and a new `view === "list"` branch renders `MaterialListItem` (compact row). All three share `getMaterialRowActions` and the parent's `MaterialPcImagePreview` so Edit/Disable/Enable and image preview cannot drift.
- **Editorial card** (`view === "card"`): 4:3 `aspect-[4/3]` photo area with the status badge floating top-right, code (mono eyebrow) + name (17px semibold) + type/ratio subtitle, a 32px stock number as the data hero with a health badge ("สต็อกเพียงพอ"/"ใกล้หมด"/"หมดสต็อก") on the right, a 4-column horizontal meta strip (ซัพฯ/รุ่น/หน่วย/ส่ง), two visible bordered action buttons (same as the previous card). Photo is `object-contain` (technical reference, must not be cropped). Stock number and "คงเหลือ" eyebrow color shift by `getStockTone(quantity)`: muted when 0, warning when 1-9, primary when 10+.
- **Compact Row** (`view === "list"`): horizontal grid `44px_1fr_auto` on mobile / `56px_1fr_auto` on `sm:`, code eyebrow + status + inline health badge on the same line, name + type·model·suppliers meta, right-aligned stock number (color shifts by `getStockTone` like the card), single `RowActionsMenu` trigger on the far right (no visible buttons — space-constrained). Inactive rows render at `opacity-70`; low-stock rows get a warning-tinted border. Reuses the table's `getMaterialRowActions` so the menu items are identical to the table view's — no behavior drift.
- **`getStockTone` heuristic** (in-file, not exported): 0 → muted, 1-9 → warning, 10+ → primary. The backend has no min/max stock threshold field anywhere, so this is a hard-coded fallback. If cps-api ever ships a real threshold, replace the heuristic with the real value — don't keep both, or two sources of truth for stock health will drift.
- **Default `view` changed from `"table"` to `"card"` for Materials PC only** (`material-pc-client.tsx#useViewMode("materials-pc", "card")`). Existing users keep their stored preference; new users see the Editorial card first. Products is untouched.
- **Tests**: 11 new tests in `material-pc-table.test.tsx` (4 for the Editorial health-badge tones + 4:3 aspect assertion, 7 for the new list view: rendering, no visible action buttons, omission when no permissions, stock column presence/absence, low-stock badge, clickable thumbnail). All 25 material-pc-table tests pass; full `pnpm test` suite 46/46 green; `tsc --noEmit` and `pnpm lint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings as before).
- Verified live against the real backend (`superadmin` session): 3-way toggle appears in the toolbar; default card view shows Editorial; switching to list shows compact rows; switching to table shows the original table; the chosen view persists across reloads (localStorage); the image preview lightbox opens from the Editorial card's 4:3 image, the list row's 56px image, and the table cell's 40px image (shared `MaterialPcImagePreview`).
- **Rule for future Materials PC view work**: any new renderer must (1) branch off `view` in `MaterialPcCollection` (don't add a separate `view === "newmode"` toggle component), (2) reuse `getMaterialRowActions` for action menu items (don't hand-write a parallel action list), (3) call the shared `MaterialPcImagePreview` via the `onPreview` prop the parent already passes in, and (4) call `setPreviewTarget` on photo click (not its own lightbox). A view that breaks any of these will quietly drift from the rest of the page.
- User asked for a WCAG audit of `/materials/pc`, then to implement the fixes. Audit was source-based (no live axe/Lighthouse run) against WCAG 2.1 AA, covering `page.tsx`, `material-pc-client.tsx`, `material-pc-filters.tsx`, `material-pc-table.tsx`, `material-pc-form-dialog.tsx`, `material-pc-status-dialog.tsx`, `material-pc-image-preview.tsx`, and the shared `ui/*` primitives the page uses.
- Fixed (all scoped to Materials PC, not shared primitives, except where the primitive itself was per-instance-configurable):
  - **Pagination buttons** (`material-pc-table.tsx`): "ก่อนหน้า"/"ถัดไป" text is `hidden` below `sm:`, which removes it from the accessibility tree entirely (`display:none`), leaving an icon-only button with no name on mobile. Added explicit `aria-label="หน้าก่อนหน้า"`/`"หน้าถัดไป"` (SC 4.1.2, 2.4.4).
  - **Search input** (`material-pc-filters.tsx`): had only a `placeholder`, no programmatic label. Added a `sr-only` `<label htmlFor="material-search">` (SC 3.3.2).
  - **Status filter pills** (`material-pc-filters.tsx`): wrapped in `role="group" aria-label="กรองตามสถานะ"` and each pill now carries `aria-pressed={status === option.value}` — previously only a color change indicated the active filter, same pattern `ViewToggle` already used correctly (SC 1.3.1, 4.1.2).
  - **Form field errors** (`material-pc-form-dialog.tsx`): `code`/`name`/`ratio`/`unitId` error messages weren't programmatically linked to their inputs — a screen-reader user focusing an invalid field heard nothing. Added `id` on each error `<p role="alert">` plus matching `aria-describedby`/`aria-invalid` on the corresponding `Input`/`SelectTrigger` (mirrors the pattern `MaterialImagePicker`'s image error already used correctly) (SC 3.3.1, 4.1.2).
  - **Select label association** (`material-pc-form-dialog.tsx`): 6 `<Label>`s (รูปทรง, หน่วย, ประเภทการจัดส่ง, รุ่น, จุดขึ้นสินค้า, ซัพพลายเออร์-add) had no `htmlFor`, and Radix `SelectTrigger` doesn't auto-associate with a nearby label the way a native `<select>` does. Added matching `id`/`htmlFor` pairs on every one (SC 1.3.1, 4.1.2).
- **Deliberately not fixed in this pass** (flagged in the audit as a separate, app-wide decision, not yet actioned): `text-fg-muted` (`#8A93A1` light / token pair in `globals.css`) computes to **~3.1:1 contrast on `bg-surface`**, below the 4.5:1 normal-text minimum (SC 1.4.3) — it's a design token used across the entire app (table muted cells, card fact labels, hints, pagination summary text), not just this page, so changing it needs an explicit design decision before touching `globals.css`'s `--fg-muted`/`.dark --fg-muted`. Ask before changing it.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (46/46, unaffected — these were markup/attribute-only changes), `next build` all pass. Not re-verified with a live screen reader this pass.
- **Rule for future form work**: when adding a `react-hook-form` error message next to an `Input`/`SelectTrigger`, always give the error paragraph an `id` and wire `aria-describedby`/`aria-invalid` back to the control — `MaterialImagePicker`'s image-error handling already did this correctly and should be the copied pattern, not the plain `{errors.x && <p>...}` this form's other fields used before this fix.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC card: simplified to a standard product-listing layout (replaces the engineering-display card)
- User asked to redesign the Materials PC card to be simple, following normal product-listing conventions ("ให้ดูง่ายตามหลักของการแสดงรายการสินค้า"). The dark carbon-fiber/blueprint "premium engineering display" card (added earlier the same day, see the entry below) was replaced entirely with a light card that mirrors the **Products card**'s structure — the app's own already-established "simple catalog card" pattern.
- New layout: `size-16` square thumbnail (still `object-contain`, not `cover` — a material photo is a technical reference and shouldn't be cropped) beside code/name/status+shape-badge identity, a `divide-y` card (image+identity → stock stat → 6-field fact grid → action buttons), all on standard `bg-surface`/`border-border`/`text-fg*` tokens instead of the bespoke `material-card-*` dark palette. Same real data as before (stock from `/stock-balances`, supplier count, model, unit, delivery type, loading point, process line), same Edit/Disable/Enable visible-button action row — only the visual presentation changed, no logic/API/permission change.
- Removed the now-fully-unused `material-card-*` CSS custom properties (`:root`/`.dark`/`@theme inline`) and the `.material-display-card`/`.material-display-card__blueprint` decorative classes from `globals.css` — confirmed via a repo-wide grep that nothing else referenced them before deleting.
- Updated `material-pc-table.test.tsx`'s "4:5 engineering display stage" test to just assert the image renders uncropped in the header thumbnail (the fixed-aspect stage no longer exists). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (46/46), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future Materials PC card work**: this card and the Products card should now be treated as one shared visual pattern (light, `divide-y`, image+identity header, stat block, bordered fact grid, button action row) — a future request to touch either card's *layout* should probably touch both for consistency, even though each still hand-writes its own markup per the project's established "don't extract one generic component" decision.

### 2026-09-05 — Materials PC card: premium automotive engineering display
- Rebuilt only the `/materials/pc` card presentation around a fixed `4:5` display stage: dark carbon-fiber texture, blueprint grid, blue-lit pedestal, small engineering label/badges on top, and the material code/name in a protected bottom gradient. The uploaded `imagePath` is the product hero and remains clickable for the existing preview; it now uses `object-contain` so technical material photos are never cropped.
- Added dedicated `material-card-*` tokens in `globals.css` plus the decorative `.material-display-card`/`__blueprint` CSS classes. This keeps the high-contrast cinematic background stable in both app themes without raw color literals in the component. The facts, stock, empty image fallback, direct action buttons, permission behavior, data fetching, and table view are unchanged.
- `material-pc-table.test.tsx` adds a regression assertion for the semantic card label, 4:5 stage, and uncropped image class; it was run red before implementation and green after.

### 2026-09-04 — Products card view: enterprise-dashboard-style redesign, real data only
- User shared a detailed "Business Dashboard"-style card spec (white card, image+info header, large price/stock stats, stock progress bar, category/warehouse/supplier/safety-stock/lot/location grid, view/edit/adjust-stock/more actions) for the **Products** page's card view — Products didn't have a card view at all before this (see the "Not (yet) applied to Products/Users/Orders" note in Conventions § Materials PC). Per the same investigation habit as the Materials-card stock work earlier today, read `product.entity.ts` directly before building anything.
- **Confirmed several requested fields don't exist for Products at all**: no price field anywhere on `Product`; no live current-stock-on-hand tracking for finished products in cps-api (searched — no product-linked stock/inventory module exists; `/stock-balances` is material-only); no separate English-name field (`Product.name` is a single field, no `nameTh`/`nameEn` split); no Supplier relation (Products relate to `Customer`, not `Supplier` — that's a Materials concept); no Lot No. on the product master record (lot is a per-production-run concept, not master data). Real fields that **do** exist: `safetyStock`/`minStock` (real numeric production-planning targets, not a live stock count), `location` (real FK, used as "warehouse"), `productType` (used as "category"), `customer`, `model`, `packing`, `lotSize`, `productImagePath`.
- Asked the user how to proceed given the gap; they chose "build the card with the real data available" — so the card substitutes honestly rather than fabricating: **`safetyStock`/`minStock` are the large primary stats** (in place of the requested price/current-stock pair), **no stock progress bar** (nothing to measure progress against — no current-quantity number exists), the info grid uses `productType`/`location`/`customer`/`model`/`packing`/`lotSize` (6 cells, same *count and layout* as the requested category/warehouse/supplier/safety-stock/lot/location grid, different real fields), and the action row shows only the two functions that actually exist — **Edit** and **Disable/Enable** — as visible bordered buttons, not "ดูรายละเอียด"/"ปรับสต็อก"/an empty "More" overflow (those have no backing functionality; adding UI for them would violate the "keep functionality unchanged" instruction and create dead buttons).
- **Deliberate exception to the RowActionsMenu convention**: unlike every other resource's card/table (Materials PC card+table, Products table, Users, Orders — all Meatballs), this card's action row uses two direct, labelled buttons instead of `RowActionsMenu`. This is intentional, not a regression of the cross-page consistency decided earlier: a card has room for labelled buttons (a strictly better affordance when there are only 1–2 actions), the Meatballs pattern exists specifically to save space in dense table rows, and the exact design being implemented explicitly called for a visible button row. `products-table.tsx`'s **table view** still uses `RowActionsMenu`/`ProductActions` exactly as before — only the new card view differs.
- Implementation: `ProductsCollection` in `products-table.tsx` (mirrors Materials PC's `MaterialPcCollection` shape) switches table/card by a `view: ViewMode` prop; `products-client.tsx` gained `useViewMode("products", "table")` + `<ViewToggle>` next to "เพิ่มสินค้า", same pattern as Materials PC. New card-only pieces: `ProductCardImage` (small `size-16` square thumbnail + `ImageOff` fallback, read-only display of the already-fetched `productImagePath` via the existing `/uploads/*` rewrite — no new upload path), `ProductCardFact` (icon+label+value, a Products-local copy of the same pattern as `MaterialCardFact` — kept separate per the project's established "hand-write each resource's card, don't extract one generic component" decision).
- Colors follow the brief: green status badge (existing `success`/`neutral` `Badge` variants, unchanged), blue-tinted outline for the non-destructive Edit button (`border-primary/30 text-primary`), red/green outline for disable/enable (matches the semantic danger/success convention already used everywhere else in the app — chosen over a literal "blue for all actions" reading, since destructive-vs-safe color-coding is a higher-priority, already-established convention).
- Verified live against the real backend (`superadmin` session): switched to card view on `/products`, all 5 real seeded products render with correct real data (code, name, status, safety/min stock, category, location, customer, model, packing, lot size); clicked Edit — the existing form dialog still opens correctly pre-filled (this change touched no Server Actions, no API client, no form logic); confirmed responsive at a 375px mobile viewport (single column, nothing overflows, buttons stay usable). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (46/46 — 4 new card tests: composition/primary-stats, visible action buttons present, action row omitted when no permission, image fallback), `next build` all pass.

### 2026-09-04 — Fixed the login card being unreadable in dark mode
- User asked to fix the login page's dark-mode design. Reproduced by emulating `prefers-color-scheme: dark` in the browser: the form card's title ("เข้าสู่ระบบ") was nearly invisible — `text-fg` correctly resolves to a near-white color in dark mode, but the card container itself was hardcoded `bg-white` instead of the `bg-surface` token, so it stayed pure white in both themes. Near-white text on a white card is the actual defect; every other element on the page already used tokens correctly (`text-fg-muted`, `border-border`, etc.) and just inherited the wrong background from this one hardcoded class.
- Fixed `src/app/login/page.tsx`'s form-card container: `bg-white shadow-2xl shadow-black/10 ring-1 ring-black/5` → `bg-surface shadow-2xl shadow-black/10 ring-1 ring-border`. Left the shadow color as-is (a generic soft shadow reads fine under either theme) and left the logo badge inside the card (`bg-white`, line ~270) and the always-dark left brand panel / mobile top band (`bg-[#0A1628]`, various `bg-white/NN` overlays on top of it) untouched — those are deliberately theme-invariant brand elements (the logo sits in a fixed white circle in all three places it appears on this page; the brand panel is always dark navy by design, not something that should flip with the app theme), confirmed by checking `(auth)/layout.tsx`'s equivalent testimonial panel uses the identical always-dark pattern with no bug, and that `body { background: var(--bg); color: var(--fg); }` in `globals.css` already makes the register/forgot-password pages' plain (unstyled-container) form areas theme-correct with no page-specific fix needed.
- Verified live: emulated both `dark` and `light` color schemes on `/login` — dark mode now shows a properly dark card with fully legible white title text; light mode is pixel-identical to before the fix (`bg-surface`/`ring-border` resolve to the same white/light-gray values `bg-white`/`ring-black/5` did there). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `pnpm test` (46/46, unaffected), `next build` all pass.
- **Rule for future auth/marketing-style pages**: this file (built early, before the token-discipline convention was consistently enforced elsewhere) is the one page in the app most likely to still have leftover hardcoded light-mode colors — if asked to touch it again, grep it for `bg-white`/`text-black`/other raw colors before assuming everything is token-based already.

### 2026-09-04 — Fixed login (and every Server Action) failing through an ngrok tunnel
- User reported "login ผ่าน ngrok ไม่ได้" (can't log in through ngrok). Root cause is a standard Next.js dev-server protection, not an app bug: (1) `next dev` blocks cross-origin requests to dev-only assets/endpoints by default unless the origin is in `allowedDevOrigins`, and (2) Server Actions (`src/app/login/actions.ts#loginAction` — called directly from the client, per ADR-002) compare the request's `Origin` header against the server's own host and reject a mismatch as a CSRF safeguard, configurable via `experimental.serverActions.allowedOrigins`. A tunneled origin like `https://<random>.ngrok-free.app` matches neither `localhost` nor the server's own host, so both checks reject it — confirmed by reading `node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../allowedDevOrigins.md` and `.../serverActions.md` directly (per this repo's own AGENTS.md R1 instruction to check the bundled docs before touching Next-specific config, since this app pins a customized Next.js version).
- Fixed in `next.config.ts`: added a shared `ngrokDevOrigins` wildcard list (`*.ngrok-free.app`, `*.ngrok-free.dev`, `*.ngrok.app`, `*.ngrok.io` — covers both the current and legacy free-tier domain formats, since ngrok's free tier assigns a random subdomain each run rather than one fixed value) applied to both the top-level `allowedDevOrigins` and `experimental.serverActions.allowedOrigins`.
- **Requires a dev-server restart to take effect** — `next.config.ts` is only read at server startup, editing it doesn't hot-reload. Told the user to restart `next dev` (and reconnect ngrok if the tunnel URL changed) before retrying.
- **Rule for future ngrok/tunnel work**: if a *new* dynamic-tunnel provider is used instead of ngrok (e.g. Cloudflare Tunnel, localtunnel), add its domain pattern to `ngrokDevOrigins` in `next.config.ts` (consider renaming it at that point) rather than creating a parallel config — both `allowedDevOrigins` and `serverActions.allowedOrigins` should stay in sync, since a tunnel that can load the app but can't submit Server Actions (or vice versa) is a confusing partial-failure to debug.
- Verified: `next build` accepts the config with no errors (config keys validated at build time), `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors) all pass. Did not verify an actual live ngrok tunnel this pass (no ngrok session running) — the fix is standard, documented Next.js configuration for this exact scenario; ask the user to confirm login works after restarting `next dev` with a fresh tunnel.

### 2026-09-04 — Card fact grids: each field now sits in its own bordered cell
- Follow-up to the dividing-line fix above. User asked for the six-field facts grid (supplier/model/unit/delivery-type/loading-point/process-line on Materials PC; category/location/customer/model/packing/lot on Products) to visually separate each value, not just group them in one soft-filled box.
- Both cards' fact grids (`MaterialCardFact`/`ProductCardFact` + their parent `<dl>`) switched from `gap` spacing inside a single `bg-surface-2` block to a bordered 2-column grid where every cell has its own padding and border: right border on the left column, bottom border on every row except the last — using Tailwind arbitrary variants (`[&>*:nth-child(odd)]:border-r`, `[&>*:not(:nth-last-child(-n+2))]:border-b`) directly on the `<dl>`, since Tailwind's `divide-x`/`divide-y` alone can't express "right border in column 1, bottom border on all but the last row" for a 2-column grid (`divide-*` only distinguishes "first child" vs "the rest" in DOM order, not row/column position). Both `MaterialCardFact` and `ProductCardFact` gained a `p-2.5` on their own root instead of relying on the parent's `gap-*`.
- Verified live (`superadmin` session): both cards show a clean bordered grid — a horizontal line between each row and a vertical line between the two columns, reads like a small table. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `pnpm test` (46/46, unaffected — pure CSS), `next build` all pass.

### 2026-09-04 — Fixed a real dividing-line gap on Materials PC's card (and hardened Products' the same way)
- User asked for clearer section-dividing lines on the card. Found a genuine bug, not just a polish request: Materials PC's card drew the divider between its header and facts-grid sections only via the stock stat's own `border-y` — since that stock section is conditionally rendered (omitted entirely when the viewer lacks `MATERIALS_RECEIVING_VIEW`, see the `/stock-balances` entry above), those viewers saw **no divider at all** between the header and the facts grid.
- Fixed by switching both Materials PC's and Products' card (`Card className="... divide-y divide-border ..."`) to Tailwind's `divide-y`, which draws a border between whichever children actually render — correct regardless of which conditional sections are present — instead of a fixed `border-y`/`border-t` per section that has to be manually kept in sync with what's conditional. Removed the now-redundant individual `border-y`/`border-t` classes from `MaterialCardStock`, the Products safety/min-stock row (kept its own `divide-x` for the two-column split), and both cards' action rows — `divide-y` already supplies those lines.
- Verified live (`superadmin` session): both cards show a clean line between every section (header → stock/stat → facts grid → actions) with no missing or doubled lines. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `pnpm test` (46/46, unaffected — this was pure CSS), `next build` all pass.

Skipped the mockup's "ดูรายละเอียด" and "ปรับสต็อก" buttons — neither has real backing functionality (no separate view-only modal, no stock-adjustment feature), and a fourth "More" overflow button would have nothing left to hold once the two real actions are already visible buttons.

- Verified live (`superadmin` session): card renders photo+SKU+name+status+shape badge, "คงเหลือ" stat, the 6-fact grid with real values, and working Edit/Disable buttons; confirmed responsive at 375px (photo stacks above the info column, nothing overflows). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (46/46), `next build` all pass.

### 2026-09-04 — Materials PC card shows real stock quantity from `/stock-balances`
- Follow-up to the same-day card redesign. User shared a mockup with price/stock/Lot No./warehouse fields and, after being told which of those actually exist in cps-api (only stock quantity does — confirmed by reading `material.entity.ts` directly: no price/minStock/safetyStock/lot/location columns on `Material` at all; those concepts only exist for `Product` or per-receiving-record, not as a single value per material), asked to add real stock and leave the rest of the design as-is.
- `/stock-balances` (`cps-api/src/modules/stock-balances/*`) is a **separate cps-api resource from `/materials`**, gated on `MATERIALS_RECEIVING_VIEW` — not `MATERIAL_VIEW`. A user can see the materials list without being able to see stock. Added `src/lib/api/materials.ts#listStockBalances()` (`GET /stock-balances`, returns every material that has a stock-balance row — a material with no receiving history yet has **no row at all**, which the UI must treat as quantity 0, not an error/loading state).
- `materials/pc/page.tsx` now computes `canViewStock` (super admin or `MATERIALS_RECEIVING_VIEW`) and only calls `listStockBalances` when true, building a `Record<materialId, StockBalance>` and passing it down as `stockByMaterialId: ... | null` through `MaterialPcClient` → `MaterialPcTable` → `MaterialPcCollection` → the card. `null` means "don't even show the section" (missing permission), not "show zero for everything" — that distinction matters for correctness, don't collapse it.
- Card change: one new `MaterialCardStock` row (Boxes icon, "คงเหลือ" label, quantity + unit) between the header and the existing facts group — a single highlighted stat, not a two-column price/stock split like the mockup, since materials genuinely have no price anywhere in the backend. Table view (`view === "table"`) was **not** touched — stock display is card-only for this pass.
- Verified live (`superadmin`, who has `MATERIALS_RECEIVING_VIEW`): card shows "คงเหลือ 0 Sheet"/"0 Piece" for both seeded materials; confirmed via a direct authenticated `curl` to `GET /stock-balances` that the backend genuinely returns `[]` in this dev database (no receiving has ever been confirmed) — the displayed 0 is correct real data, not a bug or a stub. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors), `pnpm test` (42/42 — 3 new tests: value shown when a balance row exists, defaults to 0 when the material has no row, omitted entirely when `stockByMaterialId` is `null`), `next build` all pass.

### 2026-09-04 — Materials PC card view redesigned for visual hierarchy (no logic/API/functionality change)
- User asked for a Clean/Modern card redesign — important info first, less clutter, clearer grouping, status/action prominent — explicitly without touching business logic, API calls, or functionality, and staying responsive. Dropped the `{(canEdit || canDelete) && (...)}` wrapper around `MaterialActions` — `RowActionsMenu` (added in the prior change) already renders `null` when there are no permitted actions, so the extra guard was redundant, not a behavior change.

New `MaterialCardFact` sub-component (icon + label + value) factors the repeated markup.

Live-checked in the browser (`superadmin` session) at desktop width (name-first header, icon fact grid, meatballs beside the title, status badge on the image) and at a 375px mobile viewport (single column, facts block stays legible, nothing overflows or truncates awkwardly). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warnings), `pnpm test` (39/39, unchanged), `next build` all pass.

### 2026-09-04 — Shared `RowActionsMenu` (Meatballs) primitive across Materials PC, Products, Users, Orders
- User asked for one central row-actions primitive: a `size="icon"` Meatballs (Ellipsis) trigger with an accessible label naming the row, normal actions in standard color, destructive actions (disable/cancel) in danger color below a separator, actions hidden per existing permissions, no trigger at all when a row has zero actions, and the Materials PC card view switched to the same trigger as its table for consistency — plus tests for trigger/labels/permission/destructive and a lint/typecheck/build pass.
- Added `src/components/ui/row-actions-menu.tsx#RowActionsMenu` — wraps the existing `DropdownMenu` primitive, takes `actions: RowAction[]` (`label`, optional `icon`, `onSelect`, `variant?: "default" | "danger"`) and `itemLabel` (used in `aria-label={\`ตัวเลือกสำหรับ ${itemLabel}\`}`); groups danger items after a separator, returns `null` when `actions` is empty.
- Rewired all four consumers to build their `RowAction[]` via a new exported pure function instead of hand-rolled `DropdownMenu`/separate icon buttons: `material-pc-table.tsx#getMaterialRowActions` (used in both the table cell and the card footer — the card's two separate icon buttons are gone), `products-table.tsx#getProductRowActions`, `users/columns.tsx#getUserRowActions`, `orders/columns.tsx#getOrderRowActions`. Materials PC and Products keep their pre-existing `canEdit`/`canDelete` permission gates (now expressed as omitted array entries); Users/Orders have no existing permission logic so their functions always return the full set — see Conventions § Row actions for why that's correct here, not an oversight.
- Discovered empirically that Radix `DropdownMenuContent` renders through a `Portal` that doesn't appear in `renderToStaticMarkup` output (no real DOM available to the Node test runner) — even with `defaultOpen` the trigger shows `data-state="open"` but the menu content itself is absent from the SSR string. Tests therefore split along that boundary: SSR-string assertions for the trigger (`aria-label`, present/absent), plain function-call assertions for each `get<Resource>RowActions()`'s labels/`variant`/permission-based omission. Added `row-actions-menu.test.tsx`, `products-table.test.tsx`, `users/columns.test.tsx`, `orders/columns.test.tsx`; extended `material-pc-table.test.tsx` with the same coverage and updated its two aria-label assertions to match the new single-trigger markup. All 8 test files now run via `package.json`'s `test` script (39 tests total).
- Verified live against the real backend (`superadmin` session): Materials PC table and card, Products, Users, and Orders all show the single Meatballs trigger; opened Materials PC's menu and confirmed "แก้ไข" renders in standard color, a separator, then "ปิดใช้งาน" in danger red. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing React Compiler warnings), `pnpm test` (39/39), `next build` all pass.
